# pdn-soundmodem - mode validation report and provenance ledger

Status: current as of 2026-09-17. Describes how every mode string accepted by `ModemCatalog` has been validated, in simulation and over the air, and keeps the dated append-only ledger of what proved each one working. The catalogue is `src/Packet.SoundModem/Modems/ModemCatalog.cs`; entries here cover modes whose codecs now ship as packages (IL2P as M0LTE.Il2p, the datac OFDM engine as M0LTE.Ofdm, Reed-Solomon and LDPC as M0LTE.Fec and M0LTE.FecLdpc) as well as the modems still in this tree.

The ledger pays special attention to modes that were **broken and later fixed**, so a regression can always be traced back to the evidence that closed it the first time.

## Purpose and maintenance rule

This file exists because "does mode X work" is otherwise answered by grepping commit messages and hoping. Two things must stay true as the project moves:

1. **Every mode string in `ModemCatalog.KnownModes` appears in the matrix below**, with its current status. When a mode is added, removed, or renamed in the catalog, this file changes in the same PR (mirrors the `docs/dev/plan.md` discipline).
2. **Every time a mode's validation status changes - especially broken → fixed, or working → regressed - append a dated entry to the [Validation ledger](#validation-ledger)** citing the exact commit hash, PR number, and/or issue number that did it, plus the evidence (mask figures, corpus decode counts, on-air BER). Do not edit or delete a prior ledger entry to "correct" it in place; append a new one and let the old one stand as the historical record. Where the provenance is genuinely not known, write **TBD** - never invent a commit, PR, or issue number.

Status legend used in the matrix:

- **working** - validated both in simulation/interop and confirmed over real RF (or, for corpus-based checks, against a real captured off-air/rig recording).
- **partial** - validated in simulation, but the on-air/interop result is mixed, marginal, or has a known open issue against it.
- **untested** - no meaningful validation recorded beyond the mode constructing (`ModemCatalog.Create` succeeding), or coverage is too thin to call it exercised.
- **not-yet-on-air** - solid simulation/interop validation; no real-RF attempt has been made yet.

## Validation ledger

Dated, append-only. Newest entries at the bottom.

- **2026-07-15/16 - PSK detector default.** Coherent (Costas) detection made the default PSK detector for all `bpsk*`/`qpsk*` modes (issue #5, PR #13), validated against the QtSoundModem interop matrix (PR #14, resolving issues #6/#10/#11 on evidence - #6 and #10 pinned as characterized regressions, #11 left open as a genuine marginal-interop gap).
- **2026-07-16 - C4FSK bench validation.** `C4fskModem` (c4fsk9600/c4fsk19200) landed and was bench-proven 8/8 us→NinoTNC on both modes at first live attempt - graduated straight from aspiration to `NinoTncParityTests.cs` coverage (recorded in `docs/dev/plan.md` and `NinoTncAspirationTests.cs`).
- **2026-07-17/18 - real off-air BPSK300 default reversal.** Coherent detection could not decode a real GB7RDG NinoTNC BPSK300 IL2P+CRC capture (differentially-encoded coherent BPSK) - issues #40/#42. Fixed by reverting the default to differential detection plus a 4-pair frequency-diversity bank (PR #56, PR #52), with the capture itself checked in as `OffAirBpskTests.cs` (PR #41). Coherent remains an opt-in detector; QPSK keeps its coherent default.
- **2026-07-18 - settable centre frequency.** `afsk*`/`bpsk*`/`qpsk*` gained a per-mode audio-centre override (issue #39, PR #51), validated by `CentreFrequencyTests.cs`.
- **2026-07-18 - `ModemCatalog` created.** PR #58 established the single mode→`IModem` factory this report is keyed against; `ModemCatalogTests.cs` guards every arm against being added to `Create()` but forgotten in `KnownModes` (or vice versa).
- **2026-07-19/20 - MS110D App D Phase A lands.** PR #34: Walsh-75/BPSK/QPSK, autobaud, DFE, mask-gated at first light. PR #60 adds WN7 8PSK + WN8 16QAM.
- **2026-07-20→23 - MS110D Poor-channel equalization found broken, then fixed.** An adversarial review of the equalizer campaign found the 3-pass bidirectional equalization degrading Poor-channel BER (averaging equalized symbols across passes with different fading states introduces noise instead of diversity) - **issue #63**, plus three measurement-instrument defects (RX-ring wrap under turbo re-reading overwritten samples on Long blocks; a mask harness that could report vacuous per-point passes; CI executing zero tests since an xUnit v3 migration). All four fixed on the Phase A closeout branch - bidirectional equalization gated on `IsFlatChannel()` (flat channel keeps 3-pass bidirectional, fading channel uses single-pass RLS) - **PR #68**, closing issue #63 and re-measuring every Phase A mask from scratch on the repaired instruments (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/phase-a-closeout.md`).
- **2026-07-24→26 - MS110D Phase B: WN0-WN6+WN13 brought to simulated at-mask.** Entry program issues #64 (equalizer heuristics fitted to the Poor rig), #65 (turbo robustness gaps), #67 (test-coverage gaps), #69 (the Phase B program issue). Landed as PRs #70-#95 and #97 (B0 instruments → B1 autopsies → B2 science core → B3 family closure → B4 gate flip); formal closeout **PR #98** (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/phase-b-closeout.md`): 8 of 10 Poor points hard-gated (WN0-6, WN13), WN7/WN8 close as written, measured-only verdicts (see the WN7/WN8 matrix rows above for the current figures).
- **2026-07-27 - WN2 (BPSK r1/4, K=48) found broken over real RF, then fixed.** The first off-air lab campaign (Flex→125 dB attenuator→RSP1 rig, `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-27-ota-lab-campaign/`) found WN2 Poor failing every burst (`SignalLost`) despite clean acquisition - **issue #101**. Root cause: the K=48 DFE's cold-restart least-squares solve, already running near-collapse in clean sim, over-regularises at the real rig's ~20 dB-below-nominal absolute receive level (no AGC upstream) and initialises dead. Fixed by a slow, global input-level AGC ahead of the DFE - a dead-zone no-op at nominal level, so byte-identical on every simulated mask (WN2 canonical 30/6.08 M = 4.93E-6, disjoint 29, both `agc 0`) - **PR #103**, merged to `main`. On-air confirmation run, 2026-07-27: WN2 Poor now decodes coded-BER-0 over the same rig, the AGC firing 19-32× on the low-level bursts that were previously all-`SignalLost`. (The confirmation figures are recorded here as reported this session; the campaign's own dated evidence write-up for the post-fix re-run had not been committed to the repository as of this report - follow up by adding it under `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/`.)
- **2026-07-27 - WN6/WN13 (QPSK r3/4, r9/16) found broken over real RF, resolved by a rig fix.** The same lab campaign found WN6 and WN13 Poor locking the correct waveform, then the DFE collapsing into the first fade and never recovering (`SignalLost`) - **issue #102**. A hardware-free discriminator (Wiener-phase and level-scaling injection into the clean sim capture, `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-27-ota-lab-campaign/phase-noise-experiment.md`) isolated the cause to **receiver reference phase-noise**: a 1-3 Hz effective linewidth (WN13 holds to ~2.5 Hz, WN6 to ~1-2 Hz, tracking coding strength) reproduces the exact collapse signature, and sits squarely inside the RSP1 rig's measured 0-8 Hz reference wander. **No modem code change** - the indicated and applied fix is disciplining the Flex reference to an external GPSDO. On-air confirmation, 2026-07-27: both WN6 and WN13 Poor decode coded-BER-0 once the Flex locked to the external 10 MHz reference, the residual offset collapsing from ~−202 Hz drifting to +36 Hz static. (Issue #102 remained open on GitHub as of this report - the fix is operational/rig, not a merged code change, and the confirmation run's dated write-up had not been committed. Follow up: close #102 with a pointer to the confirmation evidence once written up, and consider whether the demodulator should be hardened against a few-Hz reference wander for field deployments with less capable references - noted as an open, non-urgent question in issue #102 itself.)
- **2026-07-27 - FreeDV DATAC OFDM simulation baseline.** First AWGN/Poor/Good BER-vs-SNR characterization of all six `freedv-datac*` modes against codec2's own published operating points, on a simulation harness generalized off the MS110D ladder (`sm-ota sim`). AWGN faithful to codec2 (datac3's 90 % knee −3.2 dB vs published −3 to −3.5 dB). Poor (MPP) reads 6-28 points below the published N/100, attributed to a mix of methodology (independent-burst vs continuous-stream) and channel-model differences, not a demodulator defect; level-invariant to ≥60 dB, ruling out a WN2-style front-end bug. **PR #104**, merged into branch `ms110d-ota-harness` - **not yet forwarded to `main`** as of this report, so the evidence directory (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-27-freedv-datac-sim-baseline/`) is not yet present there. Not yet attempted on-air.
- **2026-07-27 - MS110D full on-air campaign: every masked waveform meets its mask on the real rig.** An 18-run AWGN+Poor sweep of all ten MS110D waveforms over the Flex→RSP1 rig under the GPS-locked reference (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-27-110d-full-campaign/`). **AWGN** clean coded-BER-0 thresholds, monotonic in robustness: WN0 −3.5, WN1 −2.5, WN2 −1.6, WN3 −0.6, WN4 +1.5, WN5 +3.7, WN6 +5.7, WN7 +8.8, WN13 +2.6 dB; WN8 clean to the ~+13.5 dB the rig delivers at 3.7 W (its +16 dB mask point is above the path/artefact ceiling). **Poor** (D.6.1) clean thresholds at or below every defined mask: WN0 −0.5 (mask −1), WN2 −1.1 (+5), WN4 +4.0 (+10), WN5 +5.1 (+11), WN6 +9.5 (+14), WN13 +7.0 (+11); WN1/WN3 clean (no Poor mask defined). WN7/WN8 Poor (masks +19/+23) were not run - above the rig ceiling at this power, deferred to the real-antenna phase. This forwards **WN0/WN1/WN3/WN5** from not-yet-on-air to working, and adds AWGN+Poor confirmation for WN2/WN4/WN6/WN13. This directory (and the sibling `2026-07-27-ota-poor-validation/`) is also the dated on-air write-up the #101/#102 caveats above pointed at - those are now committed.
- **2026-07-28 - FreeDV DATAC OFDM: first on-air campaign, all six modes validated on hardware.** A 12-run AWGN+Poor sweep of all six `freedv-datac*` modes over the Flex→DIGU→RSP1 rig via the DAX route (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-28-freedv-ofdm-oncar/`; harness PR #108). All six acquire and decode over the air. **AWGN matches the #104 sim baseline**: datac1 +1.8 dB exact, datac3 −3.8 vs sim −3.2 (within 0.6 dB) - the clean on-air validation of the managed OFDM engine. Poor: datac3/datac1 within ~0.7 dB of sim; the very robust narrow modes (datac4/13/14) read below sim, reflecting the **known sim-Poor pessimism** (#104) plus the 3 kHz-SNR reference vs their 250-500 Hz occupied bandwidth (a −13 dB 3 kHz-ref threshold is ~−2 to −3 dB in-band), not the rig being kinder than a fading channel. DAX/ALC costs ~1.2 dB at the top rung at 0.5 drive. Forwards freedv-datac0/1/3/4/13/14 from not-yet-on-air to **working**. Follow-ups: re-anchor the sim Poor against codec2's own `ch` (makes the Poor cross-check as clean as AWGN), and forward the OFDM harness (#108) + sim baseline (#104) to `main`.
- **2026-07-28 - FM transmit/receive path built; FM-native modes validated offline through the whole FM chain.** The FM-native modes reach the air as frequency modulation (per `docs/dev/mode-modulation-reference.md`, from the NinoTNC firmware modulator table), so they need an FM chain the SSB/OFDM ladders do not: a software FM modulator (`FmModulator` - audio → complex FM baseband at a **settable peak deviation**), an IQ→FM discriminator RX seam (`IqToFmAudioConverter`, the FM mirror of `IqToAudioConverter`: instantaneous-frequency/phase-derivative demodulation feeding the mode's own demodulator), a peak-deviation measurement tool (`sm-ota fm-deviation`, and the per-burst read-back in the scorer), an FM §E2 ladder (`sm-ota ladder --mode <fm-mode>`: render → sim-baseline channel → FM-mod at the mode's target deviation → FM-demod → decode + delivered-SNR tracking), and a live DAX FM transmit route (`FlexTransmitterOptions.SliceMode` = `FM`, drive calibratable to the target deviation). Injecting the ladder's self-calibrating noise as audio *before* the modulator is faithful to the on-air ladder (which transmits its noise lead-in through the radio's FM modulator too) and keeps the mod/demod round trip an SNR-preserving identity, so the scorer recovers the delivered SNR. **Offline `--dry-run` result (no radio):** all six FM modes decode their frame bit-exact through the software FM round trip at their target peak deviation - `afsk1200` 3.0, `fsk9600` 2.4, `fsk4800-il2p` 1.2, `c4fsk9600` 2.5, `c4fsk19200` 5.0, `qpsk3600` 5.0 kHz - with the measured SNR tracking the injected SNR to <0.4 dB across the ladder and the scorer catching the cliff (a −15 dB rung is lost). Covered by `FmBasebandTests` (deviation modulate→measure round trip, discriminator recovery) and `FmLadderPassTests` (the end-to-end dry-run, mirroring `OfdmLadderPassTests`). A supporting fix: `SimModem.RenderBurst` rendered at TXDELAY 0 (2 opening flags), which the fast classic-HDLC baseband detectors (`fsk9600`/`c4fsk9600` at 9600 Bd) cannot lock onto - it gained an optional TXDELAY parameter (default 0, so the datac/sim baseline is unchanged) and the FM ladder renders with a realistic 150 ms run-in. **Not yet on-air:** the live per-mode deviation calibration is a radio step (transmit, measure the achieved peak deviation with `sm-ota fm-deviation`, adjust `--audio-amplitude` until it equals the target) run by the on-air session; the matrix on-air column is unchanged pending it. Draft PR off `main` (this change). Note the standing warning from the reference doc: "wide/narrow" (25/12.5 kHz) is **channel spacing**, not deviation - the drive is calibrated to the Tgt Dev column, verified by measurement.
- **2026-07-28 - FreeDV DATAC OFDM Poor/MPP sim re-anchored against codec2's own `ch`; the #104 shortfall is methodology, not a defect.** The #104 sim Poor read 6-28 points below codec2's published MPP N/100, confounded between the channel model and the measurement method. This pass separated them (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-28-freedv-poor-reanchor/`): built codec2's `ch` + `freedv_data_raw_tx/rx` from source, generated a faithful MPP fading file (a spectral-synthesis port of codec2's `doppler_spread.m`, `gen_fading.c`, validated by the codec2 tx→ch→rx anchor reproducing published within ±9), and added a continuous-stream measurement to the harness (`sm-ota sim-stream`, `tools/Packet.SoundModem.Ota/SimStreamCommand.cs`; modem untouched). **Finding: the gap was overwhelmingly the independent-burst-vs-continuous-stream methodology, not the channel.** On the identical `WattersonChannel` Poor, switching to codec2's continuous-stream method recovers +2 to +22 points (largest for the shortest bursts - the long-PER-tail codec2 documents), and the re-anchored sim now lands on the published points: **datac0 71 vs 70, datac3 75 vs 74** (exact), datac1 88 vs 92, datac13 86 vs 90, datac14 85 vs 90 (within 4-5), datac4 82 vs 90 (within 8). The channel-model axis (our modem through codec2's real `ch --mpp`) confirms our Watterson Poor is faithful - comparable-to-milder than `ch` for five of six modes (so it is not the optimist; the wideband datac1 is the one where `ch` runs harsher). The surviving few-point residuals on the heavily-coded long modes (datac4, datac1) are a small managed-modem coded gap under fading - our modem trails codec2's own modem by ~4 points on the *same* `ch` channel - plus single-realisation tail noise, characterised not fixed. **This closes the #104 Finding-1 re-anchor follow-up.** Evidence dir + `analyze.py` decomposition + `waterfalls.svg`; **PR #115** (draft).
- **2026-07-28 - SSB audio-carrier coverage: bpsk1200 AWGN validated on-air; afsk300/qpsk600 sim-only (CFO wall, #116); D.6.1 Poor is a mode limit for all three.** The three NinoTNC-lineage audio-carrier modes taken through the harness (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-28-ssb-coverage/`). **Sim** (CFO-free `sm-ota sim`, 100 bursts/pt, frame layer): AWGN 90%-frame thresholds qpsk600 ≈ +3, bpsk1200 ≈ +4, afsk300 ≈ +8 dB; **D.6.1 Poor fails for all three** (<15 % success even at +15 dB) - narrowband, uncoded/lightly-coded carriers have no equaliser/interleaver for 2-path fading, so Poor is a *mode limitation* (and is exactly why bpsk1200 returned 0 Poor decodes on the air), not a rig or harness fault. **On-air** (Flex GPS-locked → 125 dB → RSP1, IQ route, 11.8 W): **bpsk1200 AWGN validates** - 11/18 decode, clean to delivered ≈ +2 dB (≈ +3 corrected for the 2.3 dB self-cal offset), matching sim; acquired-burst CFO −120…+60 Hz shows it rides the RSP1 drift. This forwards **bpsk1200 / bpsk1200-multi** from not-yet-on-air to **working (AWGN)**. **afsk300 and qpsk600 do NOT acquire on-air** (0/12 each) - their acquisition has no carrier-offset search, and the RSP1's undisciplined LO drifted +35 → ~−90 Hz over the session; the sim dry-run reproduces it exactly (bpsk1200 tolerant to ±120 Hz injected CFO; afsk300 decodes only the burst at CFO ≈ 0). Per operator (2026-07-28): characterise the two narrow modes in **sim only** and defer their on-air validation to a disciplined receiver - **issue #116** (which also tracks a first-burst −250 dB self-cal artifact and bpsk1200's ~2.3 dB delivered-vs-asked offset). FM modes should be CFO-immune (the discriminator ignores carrier offset) - relevant to the #114 FM leg.
- **2026-07-28 - FM-native coverage: modem sim-validated; on-air deferred to FM-capable hardware (Flex TX wall, #118).** The six FM-native modes through the harness (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-28-fm-coverage/`). **The FM modem is sound** - all six decode through the software FM round trip (mod→discriminate→demod; #114 unit tests prove it bit-exact), and several are already ✅ in the matrix via bench NinoTNC / WA8LMF corpus (afsk1200, c4fsk9600, c4fsk19200, fsk4800-il2p). **Sim AWGN thresholds**: `sm-ota sim` (CFO-free baseband) afsk1200 ~+8, fsk4800-il2p ~+11, qpsk3600 ~+14 dB; the fast HDLC FSK modes (fsk9600, c4fsk9600, c4fsk19200) can't run through `sm-ota sim` (TXDELAY-0 defeats their detectors) and through the faithful FM round-trip ladder sit high - fsk9600 ~+15-18, c4fsk9600 ~+24-30, c4fsk19200 ~+30 dB - the **FM discriminator noise triangle** (wideband FM trades bandwidth for a threshold penalty, physically expected). **D.6.1 Poor fails for all six** (mode limitation, no fading equaliser). **On-air over the Flex→RSP1 rig is blocked by a transmitter hardware wall, not the modem**: the Flex 6500 waveform IQ path is single-sideband ≤10 kHz one-sided (too narrow for the two-sided FM Carson bandwidths - only `fsk4800-il2p` at ~7 kHz clearly fits), and the DAX→NFM route corrupts the data with voice DSP (deviation limiter + pre-emphasis + audio filter). FM is CFO-immune, so the RSP1 drift (#116) is not the blocker here - the transmitter is. Per operator (2026-07-28): sim-validate + defer on-air to FM-capable hardware - **issue #118**.
- **2026-07-28 - bpsk300 (PSK300, the primary NinoTNC live mode): on-air AWGN waterfall added; Poor a mode limit.** bpsk300 through the OTA harness for a proper BER-vs-SNR curve (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-28-bpsk300-coverage/`) - it already had a real GB7RDG off-air *decode* (#40/#42) but no rig characterisation. **On-air** (Flex GPS-locked → RSP1, IQ route, 11.8 W, dial 0; the RSP1 offset had thermally settled back to ~0): its differential detector + 4-pair frequency-diversity bank rode the residual cleanly (pilot 6/6, self-cal 0.10 dB). **AWGN threshold ≈ −3 to −4 dB** (3 kHz-ref) - clean decode to delivered ~−1.3 dB, a robust negative-SNR mode (IL2P Reed-Solomon FEC + narrow ~400 Hz 300 Bd occupancy). **D.6.1 Poor mostly fails** (2/36, only at delivered ~+7 dB): the diversity bank rides carrier offset, not multipath, and there is no equaliser for the 2 ms fade - a mode limitation like the other SSB audio modes. **Sim confirms both** (`sm-ota sim`: AWGN 88 % @ −3 dB, Poor 11 % best @ +3). bpsk300's status is now a full on-air AWGN waterfall on top of the GB7RDG decode.
- **2026-07-28 - qpsk2400 (2400 QPSK / DW2400, a primary NinoTNC live mode) folded into the CFO wall (#116).** On the Flex→RSP1 rig (offset settled to ~−10 Hz via a bpsk300 probe) qpsk2400 **acquired 0/4 at dial 0 and 0/6 at dial −10** - nulling the offset did not rescue it. The dry-run decodes it **only at CFO exactly 0** (CFO-fragile like afsk300/qpsk600 but tighter; as coherent QPSK also exposed to the RSP1's phase-noise wander - the RX-side of the closed #102). Sim baseline (`sm-ota sim`, CFO-free): AWGN ~+10 dB 90 %-frame (56 % @ +9), Poor fails. **Of the four primary NinoTNC live modes (300 BPSK / 2400 QPSK / 3600 QPSK / 9600 GFSK), only 300 BPSK (`bpsk300`) validates on this rig** - 2400 QPSK is #116; 3600 QPSK + 9600 GFSK are the Flex-FM-TX wall (#118). On-air deferred to the disciplined-UberSDR campaign; making these modes work on *undisciplined* radios is a further modem-side enhancement (carrier-offset tracking / AFC). Sim CSV in `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-28-ssb-coverage/data/`.
- **2026-07-31 - WN8 (16QAM) Poor: coin-flip → decoding; the MFB-form receiver ships.** The WN8 redesign program (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/wn8-program-plan.md`, issue #130) ran its measurement ladder and build in one day: W1b's matched-filter bound decoded every block of both specimens to zero on the exact channel (the waveform was never the floor - the Phase B walls belonged to the receiver architecture), W3 measured label-free probe-anchor trajectory estimation 11 dB inside the receiver's requirement (killing B3.4's "only labels provide" verdict), and W5 shipped `Ms110dMfbBlockDecoder` for QAM16 blocks: per-burst delay-profile window placement (acquisition can centre on the echo - the §B3.5b phenomenon at WN8), label-free composite-FIR probe anchors, matched projection, and a SISO-soft/hard cancellation schedule with convergence-gated decision-directed anchor re-fit, terminating on exact fixed points, detected period-2 cycles (label-free residual selection), or honest revert. Corpses: 269,237 → **112** canonical, 269,154 → **32** disjoint. Battery on the final binaries: AWGN WN8 **0 errors** at full budget; **Poor 2.90E-4 canonical / 2.27E-2 disjoint** at full §5.3 budgets (the disjoint tail = 4 single-block non-convergences across 8 bursts); **all 108 non-WN8 censuses byte-identical** to the W0 baseline, guard pins exact, hermetic 790/0; off-rig direction improves on both alternate geometries (slow-fade estimation recorded as the measured weak edge). Still measured-only vs the 1E-5 mask, and sim-only by rig physics - W6 takes the gate/verdict decision. Evidence `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-31-wn8-w5a/-w5b1/-w5b2/`.
- **2026-07-31 - WN8 redesign program CLOSED (exit ii): the final measured state.** The W6 decision leg (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-31-wn8-w6/`) added a schedule-diversity fallback (hard-first rerun on revert - recovered one of the four disjoint block failures) and took the decision battery: **Poor WN8 2.90E-4 canonical / 1.75E-2 disjoint** at full §5.3 budgets, AWGN 0, all 108 non-WN8 censuses byte-identical. Not §5.3-green at the 1E-5 mask on both families, so the program's pre-committed rule closes it as **exit (ii) - a dramatically improved, honestly-recorded measured-only ceiling** (from coin-flip both families at entry; sim-only by rig physics - the +23 dB mask region is above the OTA rig ceiling). Phase B's B3.10/B3.4 banked negatives carry their earned amendments in the program record; exit (iii) is permanently refuted (the W1b matched-filter bound's 0/0 stands as the achievability witness). Successor levers with instruments waiting: the three disjoint bursts whose first blocks defeat both schedules, the slow-fade weak edge, schedule diversity beyond two, the unused 12PSK-ring moment observables. Umbrella issue #130 closed with this verdict; `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/wn8-program-plan.md` is now historical.
- **2026-07-31 - NinoTNC wiki corpus captured on the studybox bench; two fresh interop findings.** All 15 DIP modes captured at three TXDELAY tiers (45 WAVs, `tf@studybox:~/nino-corpus/`, settings + provenance in its README and `docs/dev/archive/bench/ninotnc-corpus-2026-07-31.md`), every file energy-verified complete and QC-decoded through the paired catalog mode (`NinoCorpusQcTests`): **34/45 full-copy**. The exceptions are ours, on good captures: **`qpsk600` copies 0-1/3 real NinoTNC transmissions even in a clean audio loop** - fresh, hard #11 evidence consistent with its measured ±5 Hz CFO brick wall (the #116 characterisation); **`qpsk3600` copies only 1-2/3** where the July bench scored 12/12 (issue filed - regression or level/firmware sensitivity); `c4fsk9600` confirms its ≥~250 ms TXDELAY acquisition floor; single-frame misses on `afsk300`@240 ms, `afsk1200-il2p`@120 ms, `bpsk1200`@500 ms. Also banked: three N9600A4 v3.44 behaviours (stale-TXDELAY first frame; fire-and-forget SETHW needing GETALL verification; a ~253 B AX.25 frame never transmitting in mode 1100) - details in the corpus doc.
- **2026-07-31 - QPSK detector default reversed to differential; qpsk600/qpsk3600 now copy the full NinoTNC corpus.** The corpus-decode goal's diagnosis chain (self-loopback 10/10 at every frame length; ±400 ppm resample scan ruling out constant clock offset; level/spectral/burst forensics exonerating the recordings) localised the QPSK failures to the coherent Costas default's fragility on real multi-second NinoTNC bursts - the same defect class the 2026-07-18 BPSK reversal fixed (#40/#42), compounded by the measured CFO walls (±5 Hz qpsk600, ±8 Hz qpsk2400 - the #116 CFO-0 characterisation). V.26A is differentially encoded by construction; under `PskDetector.Differential` **all nine QPSK corpus files decode 3/3** (previously 0-2/3 for qpsk600/qpsk3600), the corpus total moves 34/45 → **40/45** (the remainder are non-QPSK stragglers, each with a named symptom), and the sim CFO half-widths widen ±5→±10-40 (qpsk600), ±8→~±60 (qpsk2400), ~±60 (qpsk3600) for 0.4-3.5 dB at the AWGN 90% knee - the same robustness-over-knee trade as the BPSK reversal. `ModemCatalog.DefaultDetectorFor` now returns differential for every PSK family (coherent stays selectable via `ModemOptions`); hermetic 790/0 including the QtSM interop fixtures under the new default. Closes **#144** (the "regression" was coherent-detector fragility, moot under the new default); addresses **#11**'s mechanism pending a live QtSM retest; narrows **#116** to the remaining bank/offset-search work (qpsk600 to ±100-class). Evidence `docs/dev/archive/cfo/evidence/2026-07-31-cfo-1-qpsk-differential/`.
- **2026-07-31 - corpus-decode goal closed: 43/45, every miss attributed.** After the QPSK reversal (34→40), the remaining stragglers fell to forensics: `bpsk1200`@500/`afsk1200-il2p`/`afsk300`@240 failures were **capture-chain glitches** - isolated single-sample discontinuities (3.6-7× the band-limited slope, ~1 per 2.5-9 s, sample-localised inside exactly the failing bursts) - cured by recapture, with the capture driver now self-healing (band-limit glitch scan + retry); inter-burst demod state was exonerated by fresh-modem-per-burst splitting. `c4fsk9600`'s two cells are the final carve-out: clean recordings, with the mode's acquisition band characterised (50 ms below the hard floor 0/3, 120 ms marginal 2/3 repeatable, ≥250 ms reliable) - a documented detector limitation, future work. Corpus final on studybox with provenance; QC harness (`NinoCorpusQcTests`) gains split-burst and detector/report knobs.

- **2026-07-31 - c4fsk real-capture decode broken→working: corpus 43/45 → 45/45; the "acquisition floor" was a misdiagnosis.** The two `c4fsk9600` carve-outs (txd50 0/3, txd120 2/3) were **not** an acquisition floor and not recording defects: an instrumented replica of the demodulator, calibrated to zero errors on our own TX and on the txd250 capture, showed **every symbol error is an outer→inner demotion at 0.53-0.67 normalised** - pattern-dependent ISI in real NinoTNC transmissions squeezes outer symbols under the ⅔ slicing boundary, and the three TXDELAY tiers transmit *different payload text* (the tier tag is embedded in each frame), so the failure tracked content, not preamble length (20 ms of trimmed txd250 preamble decoded; 150 ms of grafted known-good preamble did not rescue txd50). Fix: a 5-tap symbol-spaced decision-directed NLMS equalizer at `C4fskModem`'s decision instants (identity start, silence-gated by the existing energy gate, reset with the deframer) - nine corpus bursts drop 103→25 symbol errors, all within RS budget, **corpus 45/45**, full suite green (790/790), hermetic ISI regression in `C4fskEqualizerTests` (both C4FSK modes fail the closed-eye channel without the equalizer, pass with it). Along the way the same investigation caught a separate TX-side bug in `M0LTE.Il2p` 0.1.0 - the IL2P+CRC trailer is computed over the original frame while Type 1 header translation is lossy (single C bit, PID canonicalisation), so degenerate-C frames (both C bits clear, the NinoTNC-observed wire style) always fail CRC at any conforming receiver and our whole c4fsk TX self-loopback scored 0/N. Package fix (Type 0 fallback when the header doesn't round-trip byte-exactly under CRC) published as 0.1.1 and bumped here, with a both-C-bits-clear loopback regression (`Il2pCrcDegenerateHeaderTests`) across three il2pc framings. One attribution made during the investigation is hereby **retracted**: the codec bug does *not* explain the "`sm-ota sim` render invalid for c4fsk" note - re-verified after the fix, `sm-ota sim` still scores 0/N for c4fsk even at 60 dB SNR with a 250 ms run-in (see the 2026-08-01 dig-in below for the real mechanism, which turned out to be a sim-harness artifact, not a structural limitation).

- **2026-08-01 - sim c4fsk 0/N dug in: a harness artifact plus a real equalizer weakness; c4fsk is now sim-characterisable.** The "structural" claim above is in turn **retracted** - two stacked causes: **(1) Sim-harness artifact:** `SimChannel`'s 0.15 s noise lead-in sat *inside* `EnergyBusyDetector`'s noise-floor seeding window (8 × 20 ms = 160 ms, seeded from the LOUDEST warm-up block), so the 8th seed block already contained burst audio, the floor seeded at ~signal level, and the burst never rose the 6 dB needed to assert - the energy-gated C4FSK modes discarded every sample at any SNR, while ungated fsk9600 sailed through and the FM ladder never hit it (6 s lead-in). Lead-in default now 0.5 s; ungated modes and datac re-verified unchanged. The 2026-07-28 "can't run through `sm-ota sim`" carve-out for c4fsk is obsolete. **(2) Real equalizer weakness, exposed the moment the gate opened:** the 2026-07-31 decision-directed equalizer random-walks along a null space while training on our own 0x77 preamble (±outer alternation: any taps with w[c]−w[c−1]−w[c+1]=1 fit it perfectly), so noise drove tap drift that *grew with TXDELAY* - an inverted preamble trend (25 dB AWGN, c4fsk9600: txd20 12/12 → txd250 4/12; clean at 60 dB where nothing drives the walk). Fix: freeze adaptation while the decision stream is an outer alternation, with hysteresis both ways (8 conforming freeze / 4 non-conforming unfreeze - a single-run gate leaked at 19200's noisier 5-sample/symbol decisions); a NinoTNC's 2-up-2-down preamble never matches the test, and the bench corpus stays **45/45**. An identity-leak variant was tried and **rejected on measurement**: its few-per-cent tap bias cost the most marginal real capture (corpus txd50 burst 1 sits at exactly its RS budget - 44/45 with leak). The hermetic canary recalibrated 0.62×→0.63× (preamble pre-convergence was what bought the last hundredth). **Known residual, filed as future work:** within ~5 dB of the modes' noise floors, long preambles still degrade decode *even with adaptation disabled* - not the equalizer; suspected envelope-tracker upward ratchet on noise spikes (attack 0.08 vs decay 1e-5). Post-freeze at 25 dB SNR3k: c4fsk9600 txd250 10/12 (mild), c4fsk19200 txd250 1-3/12 (its 14.4 kHz width makes 25 dB SNR3k ≈ 18 dB in-band - deep-threshold territory). Clean at 30 dB+ at every TXDELAY, both modes.

- **2026-08-01 - backtracking deframer shipped (M0LTE.Il2p 0.1.2); c4fsk energy-gate removal attempted, measured, and retreated.** Toward closing the C4FSK acquisition-floor gap vs the NinoTNC (our 10-20 ms is the energy gate's assert latency; theirs is one 16-bit word), the deframer was made **backtracking**: every physical bit lands in a 16 KB ring, and any failed collection (header RS, body RS, CRC) rewinds the read cursor to one bit past the failed sync and re-hunts - bit-for-bit equivalent to never having false-locked, so a real frame swallowed by a garbage collection is always recovered (a 23-bit hunt warm-up after register clears guarantees termination). This removes the *deafness* rationale for gating bit streams - RS+CRC already guaranteed no false frame could emerge - and hardens **every** IL2P mode against idle-channel false-locks; shipped as 0.1.2 with recovery tests (frame inside a failed garbage header; frame inside a header-passing bogus body; frame after 100k bits of 75%-ones garbage) and bumped here with a modem-level idle-robustness test. **The c4fsk bit-path ungating itself is NOT landed**: four equalizer-lifecycle designs (rising/falling edge tap resets, a frozen-state "in preamble" oracle guard, input-power and decision-error adaptation sanity gates) each fixed one regime and measurably broke another - hermetic ISI bursts, txd0 short-preamble parity, or (worst) real-capture decode, where seconds of pre-burst idle-noise adaptation wrecked the corpus to 39/45 while every hermetic test stayed green. The coupling is real: the old gate was silently doing double duty as the equalizer's per-burst lifecycle authority. Retreated to the proven gated receiver (suite 796/796, corpus 45/45, sim unchanged); ungating is follow-up work whose registration should treat the equalizer lifecycle as the design problem, with the corpus + preamble-floor ladder + hermetic ISI + txd0 parity + after-noise cold start as the mandatory five-regime battery. The measured floors (2026-08-01 bench table) stand: c4fsk9600 10 ms, c4fsk19200 20 ms behind real NinoTNC preambles.

- **2026-08-02 - ARDOP at a shifted centre: a real pdn↔pdn ARQ session, and the transmit inhibit under one.** `ardop` is a daemon modem entry rather than a `ModemCatalog` mode, so it has no row in the matrix above, but the standing rule applies: `becbdde` (#167) shipped ARDOP sharing a passband with the packet modems at a configurable centre, and both of the things that made that new were until now tested only in pieces - the frequency shift on a two-tone signal (`ArdopChannelShiftTests`) and `TransmitInhibit` against a synthetic predicate (`TransmitInhibitTests`). `ArdopSharedChannelSessionTests` closes the gap at the session level: two pdn stations wired exactly as `Daemon/Program.cs` wires one (TNC transmitter on the channel's inhibit-bypassing path, receive off a channel tap, `ArdopChannelShift` either side, `TransmitInhibit` pointed at the live engine), an afsk1200 modem sharing each channel, and a sound card substituted by real-time 20 ms block pacing. **At 950 Hz - the centre from #167's worked config - a session connects at 500 Hz, carries a payload byte-exact in both directions (including the BREAK turnover that makes the listener the sending station) and disconnects in order; a packet frame offered mid-session waits the session out and is decoded at the far station afterwards.** The same test at ARDOP's native 1500 Hz is the control. pdn↔pdn is the only possible test at a shifted centre: ardopcf cannot move off 1500 Hz, so it cannot be the far end by construction - `ArdopHostLiveTests` remains the interop ground truth, at 1500 Hz only. **Not proven: any of this over real RF** (no on-air ARDOP session at any centre, shifted or not), and no interop claim for a shifted centre beyond pdn↔pdn. Two things the bench measured on the way, neither asserted on: the first cut fed audio only during bursts and the packet modem's sample-clocked `EnergyBusyDetector` latched forever, which is *why* the bench now feeds continuous audio; and ARDOP's bursts, which bypass `TransmitInhibit`, still take the channel's p-persistent CSMA roll and so wait on the packet modems' busy state - at a shifted centre ARDOP's own signal is inside the packet modem's passband, costing a measured ~20 % of session time at the shipped p=63. The bench is load-sensitive (one run in seven stalled a transfer past its wait on a contended box); waits are patient rather than assertions loose.

- **2026-08-24 - `afsk300` (plain AX.25) decodes off air for the first time, out of a capture the station kept because it could not read it.** The 40 m station's signal survey wrote an `unclaimed` capture at 1134 Hz audio (7.050570 MHz) - packet-shaped energy outside every configured modem. Replayed through an ordinary `afsk300` receiver pointed at 1120 Hz it reads first time: `PD4R-12>ALL` UI, 116 bytes, **FCS clean**, `:>>>>> PD4R-12 <<<<< qrv on 144.925 (fm 1k2) 144.775 (ssb) 14.105 (ssb) 7.049 (ssb) 438.175 (fm 9k6)`. Measured off the audio: 2-FSK, tones 1023/1227 Hz (204 Hz shift), 300 baud, ~21 dB over the noise, framing **plain AX.25** with no IL2P. This mode's row has said "no full off-air frame decode recorded" since the matrix was written, its bench standing resting entirely on spectral and acquisition comparison against a NinoTNC capture; it now has one, on a GPS-disciplined Flex 6500 receive chain. It does **not** close #116, which is about acquiring this mode through an *undisciplined* receiver's LO drift - the Flex has no such drift, and nothing here says the mode has grown a carrier-offset search. Two findings travel with it. **The station could have copied it live and did not**, because its `afsk300-il2pc` bank reads IL2P+CRC and PD4R-12 sends plain AX.25: fed the same audio at its own configured 850 Hz centre, that bank decodes the frame when told to read AX.25 and nothing when told to read IL2P+CRC. Same population as the GB7BWR-2/PD4R-11 finding of 2026-08-03 and the same answer - a second modem entry, `{ "subChannel": 3, "mode": "afsk300", "rfFrequency": 7050570 }`, added to the station at 15:41Z, which copied the next beacon **live off the air** eight minutes later at **+1 Hz** off that centre (`rx[3] afsk300 PD4R-12>ALL 116 bytes snr 16.1 dB +1 Hz`, 15:49:31Z). The offline decode picked the frequency; the air confirmed it. **And `pdn-decode` swept all 46 modes over the file and reported silence**, because every mode listens at its catalogue centre and `afsk300`'s is 1700 Hz; a survey capture is by definition a signal nothing was tuned to, which made it the one file the tool was guaranteed to get wrong. It now reads the survey's own measured centre out of the JSON sidecar beside the WAV, takes `--centre`, and will sweep a grid of centres under `--sweep`. The capture and its provenance are committed as `samples/offair/2026-08-24/`, alongside the truncated capture of the same beacon an hour earlier that will not decode at any centre - the burst-detector floor defect (#353) cut the frame in half.

- **2026-08-02 - `afsk300-il2pc` decoded off air for the first time, through a public web receiver; `bpsk300` confirmed on the same path.** The daemon gained `--device ubersdr:<instance>`, which streams a public [UberSDR](https://github.com/madpsy/ka9q_ubersdr) receiver's **iq48** (48 kHz complex baseband, ±24 kHz) and demodulates SSB from it in-process at the channel's DSP rate, so an ordinary band-plan config runs unchanged on somebody else's antenna. Run against `m9psy-1.instance.ubersdr.org` (RX888 + 40 m full-wave loop, GPSDO-disciplined, Dalgety Bay) on the three-modem 40 m plan `afsk300-il2pc` 7.050300 / `ardop` 7.050950 / `bpsk300` 7.051600 - dial computed at 7.049450 USB - it decoded **8 CRC-valid frames from 4 distinct stations in the first four minutes**: `GB7BEX-15`→`GB7IOW-1` on AFSK300 IL2P+CRC, and `GB7OXF-2`→`GB7RDG-2` (×5), `GB7WEM` and `GB7BWR` on BPSK300 IL2P+CRC, 0 RS corrections throughout. (The "−15 to −30 Hz carrier offset" originally reported for the BPSK frames was the winning branch's comb position, not a measurement - see the 2026-08-04 entry; the decodes themselves stand.) **`afsk300-il2pc` had no on-air validation at all before this** - it moves 🚧 not-yet-on-air → ✅ working, and it did so on a mode whose bench status was already corpus-proven both directions against a NinoTNC, so the new information is purely the RF leg. `bpsk300` was already ✅ working off the GB7RDG capture and the Flex→RSP1 BER waterfall; this adds a second, independent receive chain (someone else's front end, our SSB demodulator) reaching the same verdict, and incidentally exercises the frequency-diversity bank on real off-frequency carriers.
  What this does *not* claim: the front end is not ours, so nothing here characterises absolute sensitivity, and the station cannot transmit, so no interop direction is proven that was not already. Level measured off that instance is ≈ −26 dBFS RMS demodulated. Deterministic DSP correctness (sideband selection, absolute frequency placement, decimation-by-1, LSB) is pinned by `SsbDemodulatorTests` (in `M0LTE.Dsp`, where those demodulators live) and does not depend on a band being open; the live path itself has a gated end-to-end test (`UberSdrLiveStreamTests`, `UBERSDR_LIVE=<instance>`). Shipped in **0.10.0** (0.9.0 was taken by an unrelated release the same afternoon); design notes in [plan.md](plan.md) § 2026-08-02 and [the configuration reference](../reference/config.md#ubersdr).

- **2026-08-02 (later) - afsk300-il2pc broken→working *at rate*: the receive filter was swallowing the neighbours; the narrow-branch diversity bank takes the live slot from 3/13 to 13/13.** Tom pointed the session at the live 7.0503 slot (m9psy-1 `ubersdr:` path) with the observation that this mode has never decoded well there. The investigation ran on captured IQ (corpus/ubersdr/, decoded offline through the daemon's exact receive chain with an instrumented branch matrix) and closed with a causal proof, not a correlation: static CFO was exonerated (±60-70 Hz single-demod tolerance measured on own TX; real stations at −3/+35/−35 Hz), and the mechanism is **discriminator capture** - the ±400 Hz receive filters shipped since July reach ~200 Hz past the slot each way, and the slot's everyday neighbour (an SSB QSO parked at ~7.0500) lands inside them at comparable power. Injecting a clean burst into the *recorded* interference bed: the old filters need the packet **+6 dB above** the neighbour; the new `Afsk300MultiModem` bank (±250 Hz branches stepped 35 Hz across ±175 Hz, now the catalog default for all three afsk300 framings) decodes 8/8 down to **−9 dB below** it - the off-centre narrow branches also act as interference-dodging filters, so diversity buys both offset coverage and QRM rejection. Scored on the first 30 minutes of captured traffic (13 unique CRC-valid frames: M0LTE→GB7IOW SABMs, GB7BEX-15→GB7IOW-1 retries, a GB7NOT beacon from Nottingham at −35 Hz): **old single 3/13 → bank 13/13** (narrowed single alone: 7/13). Fixture `samples/offair/m9psy-40m-afsk300-il2pc-qrm.wav` + `OffAirAfsk300Tests` guard the exact failure mode; `Afsk300MultiModemTests` guard the bank. Landed on the `afsk300-multi` branch (see the PR that carries this entry); released as 0.14.0. **Continuing:** a two-vantage overnight capture (m9psy-1 + wessex.zapto.org, auto-analysed per segment) is accumulating a wider corpus - bank-vs-single across more stations and conditions lands as an addendum here when it completes.
- **2026-08-02 - NinoTNC ID beacons caught on the PSK SSB channels (`afsk300`, in a new role).** A NinoTNC cannot identify itself inside 300 BPSK, 600 QPSK, 1200 BPSK or 2400 QPSK, so per the [operator's manual](https://tarpn.net/t/nino-tnc/n9600a/n9600a_operation.html) it idents *alongside* them - "a beacon in 300 AFSK AX.25 (1600/1800 Hz tones)", host callsign → `IDENT`, default 9.5 minutes while transmitting - and sends none at all in the three self-identifying modes (300 AFSK AX.25, 300 AFSK IL2Pc, 1200 AFSK AX.25). For everyone else on the channel that is a recurring burst they can see and cannot read: their data modem is a PSK demodulator and the ident is FSK. `IdBeaconGhost` closes it - a receive-only `afsk300` receiver attached per PSK SSB modem, placed at **+200 Hz relative to the modem it accompanies** (Nino's PSK carrier is 1500, the beacon centre 1700), so a retuned modem carries its ghost with it rather than the ghost being pinned to an absolute 1700 Hz that is only correct when both dials agree. It is built through the catalogue's `afsk300` entry rather than by naming a class, so it is whatever that mode currently means - since #191, the narrow-branch diversity bank, which matters more here than on a data slot: a ghost sits beside a PSK carrier by construction and a quadrature discriminator follows the strongest thing in its passband, while the bank's ±175 Hz of coverage is the right shape for the only error this placement has (a dial differing from the transmitting station's - the carrier-to-ident offset is fixed inside one TNC). It takes no KISS sub-channel, does not contribute to carrier sense, and never transmits. Idents are tagged onto their burst as `KK4HEJ · ID`, listed in the decode panel with an **ID** badge, and written to the frame log as `ID beacon (AFSK300)`; what a ghost does not get is a *band* of its own, having no slot to shade - its tag lands against the band of the modem it rides on, which is where the ident sits. No SNR figure - the per-burst SNR comes from a band tracker and the trackers are keyed by modem - but a real frequency offset, measured off the identifying station's carrier (see the following entry, which had to fix that measurement first). `"idBeacons": false` disables it. **Validation is file/simulation only**: `IdBeaconGhostTests` covers the mode rule, the offset arithmetic (including a drift guard pinning `ModemCatalog`'s 1500 Hz PSK default to the offset that assumes it), decode at the default centre, at a retuned one, and across ±140 Hz of dial error, and that the base PSK modem delivers nothing from the same audio; end-to-end through the real daemon wiring, an `sm-samples`-generated `KK4HEJ`→`IDENT` afsk300 file replayed under `--wav-loop` against a `bpsk300` modem logged 17 idents at 1700 Hz with the PSK modem silent throughout. **No real off-air NinoTNC ident has been copied yet** - that is the outstanding leg, and it needs a live PSK SSB channel with a NinoTNC on it rather than any new modem work. This does not change `afsk300`'s own matrix status (still on-air-blocked by #116 in its data role); it adds a second job for an already sim-characterised mode. The **FM half of the same manual rule - where a NinoTNC idents in 1200 AFSK AX.25 on every FM mode except 1200 AFSK AX.25 itself - is not implemented**; it is the obvious follow-up and the same mechanism, differing only in the ghost's mode and its fixed (baseband modes have no settable centre) placement.
- **2026-08-02 (later still) - the 300 AFSK bank's reported frequency offset was a branch index, not a measurement; it is now a measurement, to ~2 Hz.** Building the ID-beacon ghosts on `afsk300` put the bank's `FrequencyOffsetHz` onto the waterfall for a second kind of frame, and probing it across the bank showed it was not a frequency at all. `Afsk300MultiModem` emitted the *first* branch to complete a frame and feeds branches in ascending order, so what reached the display was the lowest-centred branch that could still copy the signal - measured: true 0 → **−105**, true −140 and −70 → **−175** (clamped at the bottom branch), true +140 → +35, and the value moved with buffer framing (the same on-frequency signal read −35 through the daemon's device-sized blocks and −105 through one large buffer). Fine for deciding *whether* to emit, since the deduper is content-based and the bytes are identical whichever branch wins; wrong as the number an operator tunes by. #191's own test recorded the weakness honestly ("a lower bound on the true offset, not a measurement") rather than overclaiming - this replaces the weak contract with a real one.
  **The measurement was already being computed and thrown away.** The slicer's threshold is the midpoint of the mark and space envelopes, which is the discriminator's DC level, which is the carrier offset: summing the two tones cancels the ±shift and leaves `sin(4π·offset/rate)·cos(4π·shift/rate)`. `AfskDemodulator.CarrierOffsetHz` inverts that - no new filter, no per-sample cost, and derived from the same envelopes the bit decisions come from, so a frame that decoded has a settled reading by construction. Min/max envelopes rather than a mean is what makes it survive an HDLC preamble's 87.5 % mark duty cycle. Two things fell out of the probing: the chain mixes I from the sine and Q from the cosine (the transpose of the usual convention), so **the discriminator runs downward as frequency runs up** and the reading needed negating - never noticed before because the sign only picks which tone is mark, and mark assignment is free here (NRZI is polarity-agnostic; both IL2P receivers hunt sync in both polarities). And the reading compresses beyond ~±40 Hz where the discriminator clamp truncates the outer peak (±53 reads ±45, ±70 reads ±53), staying monotonic, which is all the branch ranking needs.
  `Afsk300MultiModem` now holds every branch's copy to the end of its chunk and emits the one with the smallest measured residual - the best-matched branch, which with 35 Hz spacing is never more than half a step out and so always reads in the honest zone; `branch + residual` is the answer. Cost: a frame waits up to 100 ms (one dedupe chunk) against frames that take seconds at 300 baud. **Measured after: −160 → −158.0, −140 → −138.5, −105 → −103.4, −70 → −68.5, −35 → −33.4, −17 → −15.9, 0 → +1.5, +17 → +18.0, +35 → +36.6, +70 → +71.5, +105 → +106.5, +140 → +141.5, +160 → +161.1** - worst error ~2 Hz across ±160 Hz, against up to 105 Hz before, and correct even at the two half-step points where no branch is "the" branch. A residual ~1.5 Hz bias is left uncharacterised; it is 0.5 % of the baud rate and below what any tuning decision turns on. `Afsk300MultiModemTests.The_Reported_Offset_Is_A_Measurement_Of_The_Signal` pins all thirteen points (a return to naming branches would show up as a ~35 Hz step); a lone `Afsk300Modem`, which reported no offset at all before, now reports one too. Decode counts are untouched - the off-air QRM fixture and every other afsk300/afsk1200 test passed unchanged through the change. **Not applied to `Afsk1200MultiModem`**, which has the identical "first branch wins" shape and would take the same fix: out of scope for this leg, and afsk1200 carries the WA8LMF corpus baseline, so it wants its own before/after rather than a drive-by.
- **2026-08-03 - the overnight two-vantage validation: the bank holds at 2× (38/45 vs 19/45), and the slot turns out to speak three framings.** The addendum the 2026-08-02 entry promised. Overnight capture ran from `wessex.zapto.org` (southern England; 17 clean 30-min segments) alongside the original `m9psy-1` evening material - every segment auto-decoded through the daemon's exact receive path by an instrumented 33-branch matrix including the released classes verbatim. Across **45 unique IL2P-family frames from 7 stations** (GB7BEX-15, M0LTE, GB7NOT, GB7RDG, GB7BWR-2, IW2OHX-15, PD4R-11): **old ±400 single 19, released ±300 single 24, released `Afsk300MultiModem` bank 38 (84 %)** - and the shipped bank matched the harness prototype frame-for-frame (zero discrepancies). Winning branches ranged −140…+105 Hz; the IW2OHX-15 (Milan) and GB7NOT (Nottingham) decodes exist *only* because of off-centre branches. Three findings banked alongside the score. **(1) The slot is a three-framing neighbourhood**: UT1HZM (Ukraine) beaconed 22 frames of *classic AX.25* (NinoTNC mode 12) that only the diagnostic HDLC branch could hear; GB7BWR-2 (2/2) and PD4R-11 (1/1) decode rs=0 on *plain IL2P* and always fail the CRC check - the signature of mode-13 (no-trailer) transmissions read by a mode-14 receiver, which also retracts the previous evening's suspicion of a GB7BWR CRC-trailer interop bug; and IW2OHX beacons in two framings by turn. A single-framing modem structurally cannot hear two of the three populations; the operator fix is three modem entries on the same slot frequency (`afsk300-il2pc` + `afsk300-il2p` + `afsk300`), which the daemon's sub-channel model already supports. **(2) Banked negative - the DCD-falling-edge deframer reset**: across the corpus the instant reset cost the *old wide* single 4 frames (a no-reset variant decoded all four; reset saved none anywhere), but the shipped narrow single and bank showed no measurable reset cost, and a 16-bit-hysteresis prototype recovered none of the four even at wide filters - so no code change ships, the #61 rationale stands, and any future reset-policy work starts from this measurement (evidence: `corpus/ubersdr/analysis/`, harness in the session scratchpad, summarised here per the banked-negative discipline). **(3)** `m9psy-1`'s per-address listening quota is a rolling ~24 h window, not a midnight reset - its side of the capture stayed quota-blocked all night while Wessex carried the corpus (the capture loop's own backoff made 55 polite 10-minute probes over the night; 0.14.1's equivalent in-daemon patience was verified live separately, against the same refusing instance).

- **2026-08-03 (later) - the corpus answers "who is misconfigured vs who is auto-identing", and finds the slot's twin.** Re-scanning the same overnight IQ at the ident position (audio 2350, the bpsk300 carrier +200 Hz) found no AX.25 PSK-companion firmware idents at all - but it did find **a second, busier afsk300-il2pc activity centre at ≈ 7.0517 MHz** (62 unique CRC-valid frames in the first evening half-hour: a GB7BEX-15↔GB7RDG-2 connected session, GB7WEM-7↔GB7RDG-2, `EI0RSI-7>ID` node beacons naming ukpacketradio.network), which is exactly the NinoTNC-native convention - dial 7.050000 USB, audio centre 1700 - and which straddles the plan's bpsk300 slot at 7.0516. The **first off-air `IDENT`-addressed frame this project has copied** came with it: `GB7WEM>IDENT` "Sysop G3KEY IO91un … Access to GB7CIP", **framed IL2P+CRC in-mode at the station's own data centre** - a BPQ/operator ID beacon, not the operator-manual's "300 AFSK AX.25 alongside a PSK mode" firmware ident, so the #193 ghosts' outstanding on-air leg (an AX.25-framed companion ident beside a live PSK carrier) remains unwitnessed, and their AX.25-only framing choice now has a measured caveat: real ID traffic also arrives IL2P+CRC-framed. Meanwhile the 7.0503 slot's own oddballs sorted cleanly into deliberate configuration, none of them firmware idents: UT1HZM (classic AX.25, 5-minute APRS-grid beacons, ~+12 Hz of centre), GB7BWR-2 (IL2P *without* CRC, 15-minute MAIL beacons, on-centre), PD4R-11 (IL2P without CRC), IW2OHX (deliberate dual-framing beacons ~200 Hz apart). Evidence: `corpus/ubersdr/` + the ident-region analysis in the session scratchpad.

- **2026-08-03 (later²) - RETRACTION and the true mechanism: there is no 7.0517 AFSK network; an AFSK discriminator cross-decodes BPSK300, and Tom caught it.** The previous entry's "second afsk300-il2pc activity centre at ≈ 7.0517" is **wrong**. Tom challenged it; the decisive experiment took five minutes: our own clean `bpsk300-il2pc` transmission at carrier 2150 **decodes through the Afsk300 chain** (single modem at carrier+200, and the bank) - a phase reversal is a frequency impulse to a quadrature discriminator, the slicer turns pulse/no-pulse into the differential bit stream, and the polarity-agnostic IL2P deframer accepts it, CRC and all. Squaring the off-air bursts (BPSK² → one line at 2·fc; h=0.67 FSK² → two lines at 2·f_mark/2·f_space, verified on our own TX both ways) puts those stations' carrier at **2150.0 Hz = 7.051600 MHz exactly - the BPSK300 channel, where they belong**; the +60…+165 Hz "AFSK centres" were the cross-decode's preferred discriminator offset, not station frequencies. Consequences: the `GB7WEM>IDENT` frame is an ID beacon carried **over BPSK300-il2pc on-channel** (so the previous entry's caveat against #193's AX.25-framed ghosts is withdrawn - though the cross-decode itself is the new caveat: an IL2P-framed AFSK listener parked ±200 Hz off a live BPSK300 carrier will decode the *data channel's own traffic*; #193's ghosts are HDLC-framed, which this mostly protects - mostly, because BPSK-carried HDLC would cross-decode the same way). The overnight BPSK census, via the artefact: GB7RDG-2↔GB7OXF-2 (the main artery), GB7BEX-15, GB7WEM-7, EI0RSI-1, IW2OHX-8↔PD4R-12. The morning entry's "three-framing" reading of the 7.0503 slot is amended by the same toolkit: **UT1HZM is genuine FSK but 150 Hz shift** (h = 0.5 - twin squared lines 300 Hz apart; some non-NinoTNC soundmodem) framing classic AX.25 at ≈ 7.05031; **GB7BWR-2 and PD4R-11 are genuine FSK, plain IL2P** (mode-13 signature stands); but **IW2OHX-15 is BPSK300 IL2P+CRC at ≈ 7.05028** - right framing, wrong modulation, on the FSK channel - proven by the real `BpskMultiModem` decoding its beacon, whose full text self-describes: "IL2P A 300Bd - 850Hz - 20m/40m". Instrument lesson, banked: any AFSK survey near a live BPSK channel must carrier-classify by squaring before believing a decode's modulation; decodes prove the bit stream, never the carrier.

- **2026-08-03 (later³) - §E4: MS110D goes on the air for real, and every waveform of the ladder is copied over a propagation path.** Every MS110D result before today was made into a **dummy load** with the listener metres away (near-field leakage) or, later, down a wired 125 dB pad chain into the studybox RSP1. Both are rigs, not radio: the execution plan says so explicitly ("the path is local leakage… a property of the *room*, not of propagation"). Today ANT1 became a real antenna, and the campaign's gating item [A9] finally ran. Transmitting from M0LTE's FLEX-6500 on **7.1285 MHz** (waveform centre 7.126500, modem +2000 Hz) at a measured **43-48 W** forward *during the burst*, into `wessex.zapto.org` in southern England: **WN0, WN1, WN2, WN3, WN4, WN5, WN6, WN7 and WN13 all acquired, matched their WID, and decoded BIT-EXACT (BER 0.00E+000), every one ending on EOM** - 8 bits at WN0 up to 2272 bits at WN7 (8PSK, 4800 bps), CFO −0.1…−0.3 Hz throughout. **WN8 (16QAM, 6400 bps) is the ceiling of this path**: it acquired cleanly with a correct WID and then delivered BER 0.491 and `SignalLost`, which is a link budget running out rather than a demodulator fault. WN3 missed once at a search metric of 0.290 against a 0.32 threshold and was bit-exact on a retry 70 s later - a fade. This is the first evidence in this repo that the waveform survives a real ionospheric/ground-wave path at all, and it lands with no code change to the demodulator: revision `708dc87`, the same modem the mask suite scores. Evidence, per-burst figures, station facts and the harness defects found: [`evidence/2026-08-03-e4-first-on-air/`](archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-03-e4-first-on-air/README.md).
  **The blocker was not the modem, and is worth carrying forward: `--dial-correction` must be re-measured *per receiver*, not per session.** Against a second site (M0EYT, Wimborne) whose combined reference error was −64.8 Hz - *inside* the demodulator's ±75 Hz acquisition grid but at its edge - eight consecutive bursts at 45-50 W across WN0/1/2/4 produced **no acquisition at all**, indistinguishable from a broken receiver. Against Wessex, whose error measured +1.98 Hz, the identical commands decoded everything first time. A third site (M0XDK-1, Northampton) could not hear 50 W at all while hearing other UK stations at 20-29 dB, so receiver choice is itself a campaign variable. Also banked: a tone's apparent strength badly overstates a burst's - a carrier puts its power in one 5.9 Hz bin while MS110D spreads it over 2789 Hz, ~27 dB of difference before PAPR, so "I can see the tone plainly" and "the burst is 3 dB above noise" are the same signal.

- **2026-08-04 - the BPSK bank's reported frequency offset was a branch index too; it is now a measurement, to well under 1 Hz.** The same defect the 300 AFSK bank had (2026-08-02, above), in `BpskMultiModem`, found by Tom in the live frame log rather than by a test: 26 hours of the GB7RDG 40 m channel (Flex 6500, GPSDO-locked, `bpsk300` at 7.051600 MHz) produced 431 frames whose `offset_hz` took **nine distinct values and nothing else** - the 4 pairs × 7.5 Hz comb itself - with **354 of 431 (82 %) reading exactly −30 Hz**, the most negative branch. `EI0RSI-1` read +30, then −30 eight seconds later, then −15, inside 23 seconds; no carrier moves 60 Hz in 8 seconds. Mechanism identical to the AFSK case: branches are fed in array order, several copy every frame (the differential detector tolerates wide offset on any one branch, so overlap is the normal condition, not the exception), the deduper is first-wins, and what reached the log was *the lowest-centred branch that could still read it* - a label for where we listened, dressed as a reading of where the station was. On a GPSDO-locked rig it looks exactly like a channel mistuned by 30-odd Hz, and was diagnosed as one before anybody read the source (**issue #202**).
  **The measurement was, again, already being computed and thrown away.** The differential detector forms z·conj(z one symbol ago) and takes its *real* part as the bit decision; the imaginary part it discards is the carrier's per-symbol rotation. Squaring the normalised product strips the 0-or-π data step - for random payload and for the all-reversal training preamble alike - leaving a phasor at twice the offset, which is exactly `BpskCarrierOffsetEstimator`'s algorithm; it is now inlined into `BpskDemodulator.CarrierOffsetHz` at the cost of a `sqrt` and a handful of multiplies per sample, against the two transcendentals the mixer was already spending. One difference from the standalone estimator, and it matters: that class peak-holds since its last reset, which would freeze on the first strong burst of a 26-hour session, so this windows the recent signal (~1000 contributing samples) and a frame's reading describes *that* frame. Unambiguous over ±baud/4 - ±75 Hz at 300 Bd, far wider than any bank span. The coherent path needs none of this: its Costas NCO is already tracking the carrier, so the loop's own frequency correction is the residual, gated on DCD.
  `BpskMultiModem` now holds every branch's copy to the end of its dedupe chunk and emits the one with the smallest measured residual - the best-matched branch - reporting `branch + residual`; where no branch could measure, it reports **null** rather than a comb position, because "we did not measure it" is the honest answer and the step is not a substitute for one. Same shape, and now the same code path shape, as `Afsk300MultiModem`. **Measured after, clean loopback swept ±33 Hz in 3 Hz steps: worst error 0.09 Hz**, continuous and symmetric, with no pile-up anywhere on the comb; `bpsk1200` tracks ±100 Hz to within 0.16 Hz. Under AWGN at −11.25 Hz, 12 seeds per point: every frame that decoded also produced a measurement (the 0.5 coherence gate never fired on a decodable frame) with worst error 0.20 Hz at +4 dB, 0.61 Hz at 0 dB and 1.25 Hz at −2 dB, where the mode's own AWGN threshold is ≈ −3 dB. **On real RF**: the committed GB7RDG off-air capture, whose carrier the standalone estimator independently reads at ~8 Hz, now comes out of the shipping bank at **+8.56 Hz** - off-comb by 1.06 Hz, so the number could not have been produced by naming a branch (`OffAirBpskTests.The_Bank_Reports_The_Measured_Offset_Of_A_Real_Off_Air_Frame` cross-checks the two paths, which share no selection logic). `BpskMultiModemTests.The_Reported_Offset_Is_A_Measurement_Of_The_Signal` pins eight synthetic points chosen at exact half-steps of the comb with a 2 Hz tolerance, so a return to naming branches cannot pass any of them; a lone `BpskModem`, which reported no offset at all before, now reports one too. Decode counts are untouched - the GB7RDG off-air fixture, the NinoTNC parity corpus and the QtSM interop fixtures all passed unchanged. **Consequence for this document**: the −15 to −30 Hz offsets quoted for `bpsk300` in the 2026-08-02 web-receiver run were branch labels; the entries above are annotated and the decodes themselves are unaffected. **Still not applied to `Afsk1200MultiModem`**, which keeps the identical first-branch-wins shape for the same reason as before - it carries the WA8LMF corpus baseline and wants its own before/after rather than a drive-by.

- **2026-08-05 - the IL2P+CRC modes can be told to accept plain IL2P as well, so a CRC-less neighbour is heard instead of thrown away.** No mode changes status: this is an opt-in receive tolerance, off by default, and the default behaviour of every mode is unchanged. It is filed here because it changes what a configured mode *can* hear, and because the 2026-08-03 overnight corpus above already named the population it is for: GB7BWR-2 and PD4R-11 beacon plain IL2P (NinoTNC mode 13) on the 7.0503 slot, and a mode-14 receiver structurally cannot hear them. The prompting evidence is a second, independent sighting on the BPSK slot: a station running `bpsk300` at 2150 Hz on 7.0516 MHz had survey captures it could not read, one of which decoded offline as `bpsk300-nocrc @ 2116 Hz -> 46 B GB7BPQ>BEACON: =5828.54N/00612.69W- {BPQ32}` with the carrier measured at ~2123 Hz, 27 Hz off its own centre and well inside its own diversity bank - right frequency, right modulation, right baud, wrong IL2P variant, and a BPQ32 node at the far end. The previous operator answer was a second modem entry on the same frequency (2026-08-03, above); `"acceptPlainIl2p": true` on the modem entry is the one-modem answer, and it costs the CRC: a plain frame stands on Reed-Solomon alone, arrives with `crc_valid` null, and an IL2P+CRC frame whose trailer fails is now indistinguishable from one and delivered too (`docs/reference/config.md` says so where the option is documented). The seam is `Il2pReceiver`, which every IL2P-carrying modem now pushes its bits through. Measured and pinned while building it (`Il2pReceiverTests`): a `crcMode: false` deframer handed a well-formed IL2P+CRC frame emits the **same AX.25 frame, byte for byte, exactly 32 bits early** at every payload size, so a naive second deframer would have delivered every ordinary frame on the channel twice - the plain reading is held for those 32 bits and dropped if the CRC reading claims the same bytes. Proven through audio on two families (`Il2pPlainPassthroughTests`: `bpsk300` and `afsk300-il2pc` each ignore a plain frame by default, accept it once with the option on, and still deliver an ordinary IL2P+CRC frame exactly once). Simulation only so far - no on-air confirmation that the GB7BPQ beacon itself now copies at the station that reported it. **Superseded the same day by the 2026-08-05 (later) entry at the end of this list**: the reading is no longer optional, only the delivery is.

- **2026-08-05 - ARDOP can share a 48 kHz channel: the 12 kHz engine is bridged, in simulation.** Until now `ardop` alongside any 48 kHz mode (`fsk9600`, `c4fsk*`, `freedv-*`, `ms110d-*`) was refused at start-up - the engine is hard 12 kHz and the channel runs at one rate. `ArdopChannelShift` grew into `ArdopChannelBridge`: receive decimates the channel's 48 kHz down to 12 kHz before the existing centre shift, transmit upsamples the shifted burst back up, all outside the TNC (the `FreeDvDatacModem` pattern, whose engine is native 8 kHz), and the refusal is gone. Proven hermetically at the frame level (`ArdopChannelBridgeTests`): a real ARDOP data frame round-trips modulate -> bridge to 48 kHz -> bridge back -> demodulate **bit-exact** in all three wirings (12 kHz shifted - the pre-bridge control; 48 kHz shifted; 48 kHz default-centre), and the two-tone pair leaves at 48 kHz on the asked-for centre with its 50 Hz spacing unstretched. The env-gated pdn-to-pdn session bench (`ArdopSharedChannelSessionTests`, `ARDOP_SESSION_BENCH=1`) gained a 48 kHz-channel case with the same wiring the daemon uses. **Not proven: any of this over RF, and no live ARQ session on a 48 kHz channel has been run against real ardopcf** (which cannot be the far end of a shifted centre by construction; at the default centre it can, and that remains the interop ground truth to collect). The same change fixes a latent config bug: the ARDOP latency tunings (Flex packet buffer, ALSA capture depth, 20 ms RX blocks) keyed off the legacy `--ardop` flag and silently missed stations configuring ARDOP as a modem entry - they now key off the modem entry itself.

- **2026-08-05 - the spec-fixed waveforms became movable: ms110d-*/freedv-* take a `frequency`/`rfFrequency` like any carrier mode, in simulation, with the shift measured clean.** Their audio centres were pinned by their standards (1800 Hz / the OFDM centres), so an `rfFrequency` on one dictated the whole plan's dial and forced everything else above it on USB. `FrequencyShiftedModem` translates the audio either side of the unchanged, validated DSP (analytic SSB shift, 639-tap Hilbert - the 129-tap default rolls off exactly where MS110D's 170 Hz skirt sits relative to a 48 kHz rate), so the planner now places these modes like any other; the RF placement is exact either way, and on air a moved waveform is still standard, because interop is set by the RF centre alone. Proven hermetically: frame round-trips through moved modems (`ms110d-wn6` at 3000 and 5000 Hz, `ms110d-wn2` at 3600, `freedv-datac3` at 2500 - `FrequencyShiftedModemTests`); the mirror image of a 5000 Hz-centred WN6 burst measured **85.7 dB down** (gate: 50); probe-measured centres follow the override to tolerance for both families (`ModemCentreFrequencyTests`); a centre that would fold the skirts into DC or Nyquist is refused at start-up with the numbers; and the planner places Tom's 40 m layout (afsk300 7.0503 / bpsk300 7.0516 / **ms110d-wn6 7.0535, ABOVE them**) on a planner-chosen dial (`BandPlannerTests`), which the dictated dial could never produce. Decode-margin gates: a CI smoke pins zero lost decodes at a working SNR moved vs native, and the env-gated `MS110D_SHIFT_GATE=1` ladder (A/B/B' at native/3000/5000 across the AWGN knee) pins the knee unmoved; `sm-ota sim --centre` is the standalone instrument for the same A/B. The ladder earned its keep before merge: the first cut (shift only, no receive filter) measured a **3 dB knee movement at a 5000 Hz centre** (0/30 decodes at 5 dB against 27/30 native; a 3000 Hz centre lost ~1 dB), and raising the Hilbert to 1279 taps changed nothing - because it is not a Hilbert defect: downshifting a real signal by delta folds all channel noise below delta onto the wanted band (the analytic mix only suppresses the input's own conjugate), so a 3200 Hz shift doubles the demod band's noise. The decorator now bandpasses receive to the on-air band BEFORE the downshift, which removes the folding region entirely: re-measured 30/30 at 5 and 6 dB at both moved centres, native/3000/5000 within binomial wobble at 4 dB (23/22/18 of 30), the moved arms fractionally ABOVE native at 5 dB because the bandpass also strips out-of-band noise the native arm still ingests. ARDOP's sibling shift never met the fold because its shifts are a few hundred Hz. **Not proven: nothing on air at a moved centre yet.** Behaviour note for existing configs: an `rfFrequency` on one of these modes keeps its RF placement to the Hz but may report a different dial, since the planner now centres the ensemble instead of obeying the pinned mode.

- **2026-08-05 (later) - every IL2P+CRC mode now reads plain IL2P unconditionally; the option only decides whether the host is given it.** Still no mode changes status, and the default behaviour of every mode's *host* path is unchanged - but what a configured mode **hears** has changed on every IL2P+CRC entry in the matrix above, which is why this belongs here. The operator's reasoning is the point and it is worth carrying: IL2P+CRC exists precisely because Reed-Solomon alone was letting too much corrupt traffic through, so he does not want RS-only frames reaching his node on that port, and he does want to see them. One switch was answering both questions. Now the second, plain reading always runs; a frame it alone produces is marked `MonitorOnly` and reaches the waterfall (badged **`RS ONLY`** in warning orange, with a tooltip saying whether it went to the host), the frame log (`crc_valid` null), the journal line (`plain il2p (rs only, not passed to host)`) and the signal survey, where it counts as a decode and stops its burst being captured as `missed` - deliberate, and the better trade: a badged row naming the sender beats another WAV of the same beacon every ten minutes, and it leaves the capture budget for bursts nobody has explained. `acceptPlainIl2p` now only releases such a frame to the KISS host, and the KISS quality sidecar is skipped for a withheld one so a host is never told about a frame it did not receive. **New evidence out of the change, on audio that has been in this repository since issue #40**: the committed GB7RDG NinoTNC BPSK300 capture holds its connected-mode frame **twice, 12.5 s apart, and only the first one's CRC verifies** - every branch demodulates the second perfectly with zero FEC corrections and no branch can make its four trailer bytes check out. It was being discarded in silence on every run of the suite; it is now a badged, withheld row (`OffAirBpskTests.The_Second_Burst_In_The_Capture_Is_Reported_As_Plain_And_Withheld`). The same capture pins the invariant that matters for every other frame on such a port: **if any branch's reading verified the trailing CRC, that is the reading reported and delivered**, whichever branch produced it and whatever the option says - the branches nearest this capture's carrier are precisely the ones that cannot verify it, so a bank that chose on centring alone reported a good frame as RS-only (`A_Verified_Frame_Is_Reported_As_Verified_Whatever_The_Option_Says`; the choice is made on what each reading proved, not on where the frame was being routed). End to end on the operator's own 12 kHz survey capture at 2150 Hz through `bpsk300`: option off, host path empty and monitor path `46 B GB7BPQ>BEACON: =5828.54N/00612.69W- {BPQ32}` marked plain and monitor-only; option on, host gets it once, still marked plain. **Cost, measured rather than assumed**, because this puts a second deframer on every IL2P+CRC channel permanently and nine on a BPSK bank: the added work is 24-bit sync hunting at **5.0-5.8 ns per received bit**, so a nine-branch 300 baud bank spends 0.0014 % of one core and a 19200 bps C4FSK modem 0.01 %; over 120 s of real off-air audio, best of five passes, the whole receive path measures 4805 ms with it against 4828 ms without (`bpsk300` bank), 5895 against 5811 (`afsk300-il2pc`) and 556 against 550 (`qpsk2400`) - inside the run-to-run spread in both directions, and a Pi will not notice. Still simulation and archive audio only; no on-air confirmation that the GB7BPQ beacon itself now appears at the station that reported it.

- **2026-08-06 - ms110d-* transmit waveform switchable at runtime over KISS SETHW; ACKMODE hardened on the way.** No mode changes status, filed on the acceptPlainIl2p precedent: it changes what a configured mode can DO. `Ms110dModem.SetTxWaveform(wn, interleaver?)` is the typed public API (an immutable modulator swapped under a volatile reference, applied from the next burst; receive untouched - autobaud); KISS SETHW (type 6, `payload[0]` = WN 0-8/13, optional `payload[1]` = interleaver 0/1, no +16 form) is its wire face, echoed back to the sender on success and journalled either way; the shift decorator forwards both paths, so a moved MS110D is equally controllable. Proven hermetically (`KissHardwareControlTests`, `Ms110dRuntimeWaveformTests`): a WN6->WN2 switch more than doubles the burst airtime for the same payload (it really is the slower waveform, not a renamed string) and the burst decodes autobaud; invalid WN refused with the modem unchanged; the whole path works through a 3000 Hz-shifted modem. Three ACKMODE/server defects fixed and pinned while building the surface: a dedicated port's acks and echoes carried the REAL sub-channel nibble while everything else on that port is relabelled 0 (a nibble-0-only host would mis-file or drop them); the per-socket write path was unlocked, so a continuation-thread ack could tear a concurrently broadcast frame mid-FEND (both frames then vanish at the client's decoder); and an id-only ACKMODE frame (two bytes, no data) fell through a `> 2` guard and vanished - it is now acked immediately, since transmission of nothing is trivially complete. The KISS command surface is the driver contract; it is in `docs/reference/ports-and-endpoints.md`.

- **2026-08-06 (later) - the noise-folding audit: every pre-existing frequency shift checked; ARDOP's bridge had the same defect and is fixed the same way.** The FrequencyShiftedModem gate's finding (downshifting a real signal by delta folds all channel noise below delta onto delta - f; Hilbert quality is irrelevant) is generic to any real-signal receive downshift, so every `FrequencyShifter` consumer was audited. Immune by construction: `SsbDemodulator`/`StreamingSsbDemodulator` (the UberSDR daemon path and `sm-ota monitor`) stay complex through the shift and apply a complex sideband bandpass before ever taking a real part, which is the step that folds; every transmit-side shift (up-shifts, or complex IQ out); the AFSK/BPSK banks (internal complex mixers, per-branch filters). **Guilty: `ArdopChannelBridge.Receive`** - unshifting a centre above 1500 Hz is a downshift by centre - 1500, and a white-noise PSD test measured **+2.93 dB** across 550-850 Hz of the engine band at a 2400 Hz centre (fold overlaps a session of bandwidth bw once centre > 3000 - bw/2, so 2000 Hz sessions suffer above centre 2000 - placements a nominal rig's Concern warning flags but a wide-window headless Flex plans legitimately). Fixed exactly as the decorator was: bandpass to the on-air band (widest session ± 300 Hz margin, 639 taps at the engine rate) BEFORE the unshift, only when shifted - the unshifted path stays bit-for-bit; re-measured flat (< 1 dB), every existing round-trip and the shifted-decode tests unchanged, and the PSD test stays as the regression pin. **Also fixed: `sm-decode --centre`** (same geometry on off-air recordings, same bandpass-then-unshift). `nino-compare shift` keeps its semantics - a generic instrument that cannot know the signal's band - and its help text now carries the caution plus the threshold; its documented slot-to-1500 use folds harmlessly below every reference band. The validated ARDOP placements were never exposed: centre 950 unshifts UPWARD (fold lands at Nyquist, far above the band), and the default 1500 has no shift at all.
