- **2026-08-06 (later²) - the whole-codebase review campaign: `afsk1200-multi` cold-start TX broken→working; the last two branch-index offsets become measurements; the QPSK family reports offset at all.** A full-repo code review (10 finder angles, 33 adversarially confirmed findings, resolved across PRs #227-#234) landed three changes that belong in this ledger. **(1) `afsk1200-multi` transmit trained a cold receiver badly and now does not**: its `Modulate` still sent the all-flags TXDELAY fill that 4ab6d9e replaced with `TrainingPreamble` zeros in `Afsk1200Modem` and `Afsk300Modem` - the identical Bell 202 waveform, so frames a cold receiver took 10/10 from the single modem could be lost from the bank at short TXDELAY. The bank now sends the identical fill, held to the identical bar (`MultiDecoderTests.The_Bank_Transmits_The_Training_Fill_A_Cold_Receiver_Acquires_From`: 10/10 into a cold single receiver at TXDELAY 0). **(2) The 2026-08-04 entry's standing carve-out is closed**: `Afsk1200MultiModem` was the last bank still reporting the first-finishing branch's comb position as `FrequencyOffsetHz`; it now ranks candidates per chunk on each demodulator's own `CarrierOffsetHz` residual and reports branch + residual, with one wrinkle the PSK banks never had - the +6/+12 dB/oct emphasis pre-filters tilt the spectrum by design, which skews the envelope midpoint the residual is read from (measured: an emphasised branch 30 Hz out reported a near-zero residual and stole the win from the matched flat branch), so flat branches outrank emphasised ones and the residual decides among equals. Honesty is coarser than the other banks': ~±12 Hz on steady content and ~±30 Hz worst-case from the trailing flags' mark duty (`AfskDemodulator` documents ±200 for Bell 202), against a deterministic −60/−90 comb lie before. **Caveat, deliberately recorded: the WA8LMF before/after that the 2026-08-04 entry asked for has NOT been run** - the TNC Test CD audio is not on this box; the in-suite AFSK coverage (loopback, Dire Wolf cross-validation, NinoTNC TXDELAY-0 parity, the multi-decoder and centre-frequency tests) passed unchanged, and the emission restructure is the same count-preserving shape the 300 baud and BPSK banks measured as decode-neutral, but the corpus re-score remains the outstanding follow-up before the 972/970 figure is re-asserted. **(3) `qpsk600`/`qpsk2400`/`qpsk3600` - the live NinoTNC network's primary modes - reported no frequency offset at all**: `QpskDemodulator` never received the measurement `BpskDemodulator` gained in #202. It has it now (coherent: the Costas NCO's tracked correction, DCD-gated; differential: the normalised conjugate product raised to the *fourth* power - one doubling more than BPSK's squaring, since QPSK's data steps are 90° not 180° - into the same decaying window), and `QpskModem` forwards it into `FrameQuality` exactly as `BpskModem` does (`QpskCarrierOffsetTests`: both detectors, shifted and on-frequency, the shift sized to the coherent loop's documented ~5 Hz pull-in). No mode changes matrix status; the campaign's remaining fixes (transmit-path lifecycle, TCP server robustness, startup contract, the descriptor-table refactor that turned per-mode facts into one row each, and the receive-path efficiency work including the mixer NCOs becoming rotating phasors) are recorded in the PR trail rather than here, since they change no mode's validation state - with one exception worth a sentence: the ARDOP TX centre shift was truncating the last ~5.3 ms of every shifted burst (the trailer tones) and leaking them into the next burst's leader, fixed per-burst-shifter-with-flush like every other TX shift (PR #232), which tightens the 2026-08-02 shifted-session evidence without invalidating it (those sessions decoded despite the truncation).

- **2026-08-06 (later³) - the differential BPSK receive chain rebuilt against measurement: ~1 dB more AWGN margin for `bpsk300`, ~1.5 dB for `bpsk1200`, and the 24 h miss-corpus scoreboard moves 8 → 5 (PR #236).** No mode changes status - both were ✅ - but the numbers behind the primary NinoTNC live mode moved enough to record. Three independent losses were isolated on the `sm-ota sim` ladder, each with its own experiment: the **band-pass fronting the decode path** cost ~1.5 dB (its passband is not flat across the signal, breaking the matched-filter condition; it now gates EnergyBusy only, the truncated-RRC matched filter's own −54 dB-at-one-baud stopband exceeding what it provided); the **⅔-baud low-pass** was not matched to the TX shaping (now a root-raised-cosine mirror of the modulator's roll-off, cascade ISI-free at symbol instants, ~0.5 dB); and **Dire Wolf's 0.74 DPLL inertia** carried ~1 dB of timing jitter at the RS threshold (0.92 on this path; the all-reversal IL2P preamble re-locks a cold clock within a normal TXDELAY, and 0.96+ measured better in steady state but failed the 24-bit minimum preamble - so 0.92 it is). On top of those the bit decision moved from the single-sample differential product to **decision-feedback differential detection**: the symbol-instant sample decided against a remodulated ~20-symbol exponential carrier reference, which buys back the classic ~1 dB differential-vs-coherent give-away with no loop to lose lock - carrier offset handled by a narrow always-running rotation tracker (two bank steps, which also keeps the neighbouring-branch selection diversity the wide-tolerance plain detector had provided for free) plus a one-shot per-burst seed from the offset window covering the ±baud/4 a lone modem always tolerated. Ladder, 9-branch bank, 300 bursts/point: **AWGN −4 dB 46 % → 82 %, −5 dB 4 % → 49 %, −3 dB 81 % → 94 %**; offset sweep 0-33 Hz at −3 dB flat 92-97 % including the mid-branch points a naive reference nulls (the first cut measured 0/60 at +3.75 Hz, which is what forced the frequency state); CCIR Good −4 dB 25 % → 33 %; CCIR Poor 22 % → 29 % at +12 dB - **Poor remains equaliser-bound, the known mode limit stands**. `bpsk1200` inherits the chain wholesale: +2 dB 20 % → 86 %. Real audio: the miss corpus scoreboard (`NINOTNC_ASPIRATION=1`) moves **8 still-failing → 5** of 37 (three genuinely weak GB7WEM-7/GB7OXF-2 frames the GB7RDG NinoTNC copied and we did not, now copied; 32 of 37 are graduation candidates per the corpus discipline - not done in this PR), and **every branch of the bank now CRC-verifies the committed GB7RDG capture's first burst** where the four branches nearest the carrier had managed only the plain reading. NinoTNC parity, QtSM interop and the off-air fixtures pass unchanged; the coherent path keeps its issue-#5 configuration untouched as the acquisition cross-check, and `CoherentDetectionTests` records the deliberate inversion (the BPSK rows now pin differential ≥ coherent). Cost: the bank measures 9.3 % of one core at 12 kHz on the dev box, against 4.2 % before. **Found on the way, fixed first**: `Il2pReceiver`'s late-reading guard - one maximum-length frame = 34 s of wall clock at 300 Bd - swallowed the plain reading of a byte-identical retransmission as a "late second reading" of the previous burst; the GB7RDG capture holds exactly that pair 12.5 s apart, and only ever surfaced its second burst because the branches that could not verify burst one's CRC never armed the guard. Cleared on `Reset()` now (skew cannot cross a burst boundary; retransmissions always do), with a regression test that fails against the old code. Measurement caveat, documented in the tests: a matched filter centred on the channel asymmetrically truncates a far-off signal's spectrum, biasing a **lone modem's** offset reading by ~10 % of the offset (24.3 read at 22 true); a bank's winning branch reads within half a step, where the bias is under 0.1 Hz, and the sub-Hz bank measurement pins of #202 pass unchanged. Simulation and archive audio only - nothing on air with the new chain yet; the obvious next legs are an on-air re-run of the Flex→RSP1 AWGN waterfall and the QPSK family, which still runs the old-style chain.

- **2026-08-06 (later³ b) - a grazed trailing CRC now corroborates a frame instead of silencing it: the miss-corpus delivery rate moves 3/37 → 22/37, and the mechanism behind 29 stranded frames is named (PR #238).** The day's honest correction first: the aspiration scoreboard counts `FrameDecoded`, which since the 2026-08-05 always-read-plain change includes withheld RS-only readings - so "32 of 37 copy" meant 32 demodulate **byte-exact**, while only **3 verified their CRC and reached a host**. Probing the other 29 measured their trailers sitting 2-10 wire bits from the trailer their (proven, byte-exact against the NinoTNC's own decodes) payloads imply, with the damage clustered in the last bit positions - the transmit pulse truncates at the end of the burst, the matched filter loses the final symbols, and IL2P+CRC parks its only FEC-unprotected bytes exactly there. The NinoTNC's shorter filters graze less, which is how it copied what we would not deliver. The fix is receive-side and format-clean: `Il2pReceiver` already holds a plain-only frame for exactly the 32 bits its trailer occupies, so it now collects those bits, recomputes the trailer the payload implies (`Il2pCodec.Encode`), and **delivers the frame when they differ by ≤ 4 bits**, whatever `acceptPlainIl2p` says - an uncorrelated trailer lands that close with probability ~1.1e-5, the same order as the CRC check's own false-accept, so a corroborated frame is CRC-grade evidence, badged with its measured distance (`FrameQuality.TrailerNearBits`; `crc_valid` stays null, which remains the truth). One bit of damage never reaches this path - the trailer's own Hamming layer corrects it and the CRC verifies outright - and a genuinely CRC-less station (GB7BWR-2, PD4R-11) is never promoted by it: nothing follows its frames but noise, which sits ~16/32 away. Measured on the corpus: host-delivered 3 → **22 of 37** (3 CRC + 19 corroborated), 10 byte-exact frames beyond the threshold, 5 with no demodulation at all. A companion surprise fell out of the same change: the "historical wide filters lose the frame to the neighbouring QSO" off-air AFSK300 pin was wrong about the mechanism - those filters recovered the payload all along and lost only the trailer, so the pinned test now records the true contrast (wide filters: corroborated-only; current defaults: CRC-verified). All modes inherit the delivery change through the `Il2pReceiver` seam. Simulation and archive audio only; the live 40 m capture campaign (see plan.md 2026-08-06 (later) and docs/dev/archive/rx-roadmap.md) will say what it does on fresh traffic.

- **2026-08-06 (later⁴) - Watterson performance masks land for the audio modems (rx-roadmap workstream 0, Tom's proposal).** No mode changes status; what changes is that every mode's measured floor is now held by a test instead of by ledger prose. `WattersonMaskTests` runs the same `SimBench` rig `sm-ota sim` drives - a mask number and a CLI number are the same deterministic measurement - in two tiers: a CI-blocking smoke tier (one to five anchor points per mode at N=40, floors 2-3 binomial sigma under the 2026-08-06 measured values, sized to catch a decibel and ignore wobble; ~21 s) and an `SM_MASK_GATE=1` full ladder (the flagship bpsk300's whole SNR x channel x CFO grid plus bpsk1200 at N=100; ~140 s), the receive-path A/B instrument. Masks are measured reality, never aspiration: bpsk300's rows come from the post-#236 + erasure receiver (AWGN -4 dB 37/40, the 3.75 Hz mid-branch CFO point 39/40, Good 0 dB 22/40, Poor +9 dB 7/40 - the equaliser-bound floor pinned so it cannot collapse unnoticed), and the QPSK family's rows sit at their current uncampaigned levels on purpose, so their eventual campaign is measurable. The accept discipline joins CLAUDE.md: receive-path PRs run the affected mode's full ladder A/B, and a mask moves only with an entry here saying what moved it. Both tiers green at landing. Known limit, stated where the masks live: green means no regression against the model - no static crashes, no SSB tilt, no AGC, our own TX shaping - and the 40 m capture campaign stays the truth about the band.

- **2026-08-06 (later⁵) - workstream 5's MLSE equaliser: built to parity, measured honestly, default unchanged, ceiling claim retired.** No mode changes status and no mask moves - the default bpsk detector stays differential, which is why this entry records an *instrument* rather than a transition. `PskDetector.Mlse` (opt-in; `sm-ota sim --detector mlse`; QPSK rejects it) puts a 4-state adaptive Viterbi equaliser behind the unchanged differential front end: 3 complex taps under decision-directed LMS, add-compare-select margins as the erasure ladder's confidence, 16-symbol traceback inside the DCD-reset window. Reaching parity took four measured corrections worth remembering (each cost dBs until found): no mid-stream trellis flush ever (a pipeline refill is a frame-killing bit slip); outer-tap adaptation skipped exactly on alternating decision triples, because the all-reversal IL2P preamble makes a 3-tap channel physically unidentifiable (only g0-g1+g2 is observable) and unconstrained LMS walks into the sync word with the main energy split three ways; outer taps enter the branch metrics only on sustained smoothed evidence during a seeded burst (unconditional exposure cost ~5 dB AWGN; engagement between bursts, where a tap-power ratio is meaningless, scored 0/50 at -5 dB); main tap and rotation tracker adapt undelayed (they are a per-symbol feedback loop, and delaying it measured 1-2 dB of phase jitter) while the outer taps adapt on traceback-matured depth-6 decisions. Endpoint, N=100/point seed 1 with Poor's upper rungs re-confirmed on seeds 101-200: AWGN -5..-2 and the 0-30 Hz CFO sweep at parity within two points; Good/Moderate parity with +3 at the top rungs; **Poor +6/+9 dB pooled 51->58 and 56->64 of 200 (~+4 points)**; the 37-frame miss corpus identical at 32/37 demodulated. The 29 %->50-60 % expectation is retired with its mechanism named: Poor's residue is flat outage, DPLL timing wander and near-antiphase composite nulls (no static two-path setting separates the detectors through the full chain - DF-DD+RS digests any static echo the trellis can equalise, and what kills DF-DD is timing, which no symbol-rate equaliser restores), and the preamble's unidentifiability keeps the outer taps unconverged until after the sync word, so header-limited frames are structurally out of reach. Successors: A/B `mlse` against the capture campaign's misses-v2 on real audio before any default flip is considered, and workstream 4's two-pass receiver, which is the honest route to converged-taps-from-symbol-0. `MlseEqualiserTests` + `MlseDetectionTests` pin the behaviour, including the recorded negative (the static two-path fixture asserts decode-through, deliberately not separation).

- **2026-08-07 - the QPSK campaign's first landing: matched filter + decode-path band-pass removal + DPLL timing move qpsk600 ~2.5 dB and qpsk2400 ~2 dB at the AWGN knee, and qpsk2400's CFO dip is gone.** The campaign (docs/dev/archive/qpsk/plan.md, Tom-directed, MS110D discipline) baselined both SSB QPSK modes under both detectors, autopsied the pre-#236 chain one loss at a time, and landed the three chain fixes as one change to `QpskDemodulator`'s differential path: an RRC matched to each mode's own TX roll-off replaces the 0.75-baud low-pass (~1-1.5 dB, the dominant fix - and the Q1 lesson worth keeping: a band-pass bypass alone measured a NEAR-NULL, because the bpsk300 prior's 1.5 dB was measured against a matched filter; against an unmatched low-pass the ripple drowns, so matched-filter-first is the autopsy order); raw audio feeds the decode chain with the band-pass gating EnergyBusy only (worth points only at qpsk600's deepest rung, where its +-300 Hz passband ripples across the signal); DPLL inertia 0.92 on the differential path (~1 dB, the PR #236 number reproduced). Full ladder N=100 seed 1: qpsk600 AWGN 0 dB 6->69 %, +2 80->99 %; CFO wall 10-15 -> 15-20 Hz; qpsk2400 +7 15->72 %, +9 80->97 %, CFO row 72/76/71/58 -> 95/96/94/84 across 3.75-30 Hz. Fading rows essentially unmoved on both modes - the Q0 loss surface's prediction that Moderate/Poor are equaliser-bound at these symbol rates stands, and stays with the campaign's hard end. Three pins moved with the landing, all improvements outrunning fixtures: the QPSK rows of `CoherentDetectionTests` joined the BPSK rows in the inverted differential-matches-coherent gate (the QPSK decision is still the one-symbol conjugate product - the decision-feedback reference is the campaign's open follow-up); `SsbLadderPassTests`' sub-cliff rung moved 0 -> -6 dB; the qpsk600/qpsk2400 AWGN smoke masks rose 30 -> 36 and 32 -> 36 of 40. Coherent detection untouched as the cross-check variant. Interop gates green at landing (NinoTNC parity, QtSM, off-air fixtures, full suite). Both modes stay wire-identical: nothing transmitted changed.

- **2026-08-07 (later) - the QPSK four-phase decision-feedback reference lands as a CFO-robustness change, with its AWGN null and its two hard-won findings on the record.** Fix-set 2 of the QPSK campaign (docs/dev/archive/qpsk/plan.md): the differential path decides absolute quadrants against a decision-remodulated ~20-symbol reference once a burst's rotation is seeded from the fourth-power offset window (quartered angle, unambiguous over +-baud/8), with a decision-directed tracker from the seed on. Measured: qpsk600 at +8 dB with a realistic 150 ms TXDELAY moves 15 Hz offset from 71 % to 97 % and 30 Hz from 0 % to 38 % - the first configuration in this repo's history to decode qpsk600 at 30 Hz - while AWGN, fading and knee-SNR CFO rows are identical to fix-set 1 within confidence intervals: the reference buys QPSK no noise margin (a measured null, unlike BPSK's ~1 dB, and unlike the campaign's own prior). Two findings worth their cost: the seed must gate the DECISION HANDOVER, not just the rotation (a reference deciding from cold scored 60 % at 8 Hz where the plain product scores ~99 % - QPSK has half the preamble symbols and half the phase margin BPSK's cold-deciding reference got away with; unseeded and weak bursts keep the plain product, so nothing is ever worse than fix-set 1); and qpsk3600 is deliberately scoped OUT after the same machinery measured a clean-signal regression on its FM chain at 6 2/3 samples per symbol (22/30 vs 30/30) - `decisionFeedback: false` keeps the mode on the detector it was validated with, recorded in the factory and the demodulator parameter. Full suite green (1574 tests); coherent path untouched; nothing transmitted changes.

- **2026-08-07 (later2) - the ensemble decode-any knob lands as an opt-in: differential + MLSE banks in union, +6-7 points on CCIR Poor for double the bank CPU.** rx-roadmap workstream 2's demoted idea, revived by the capture campaign's opening-evening measurement (symmetric ~1.5 % exclusive-frame exchange between the detectors, +1.1 % deliverable union on real traffic). `ModemOptions.SecondDetector` gives every bpsk diversity position a branch under each detector; the bank's existing content dedupe reduces the union to single deliveries; transmit is pinned byte-identical (a receiver arrangement, not a wire change); non-bpsk modes throw rather than silently supply the single bank. Sim ladder N=100 seed 1, single differential -> ensemble with mlse: AWGN 56/88/95 -> 58/88/95 across -5/-4/-3 dB, Moderate 50/55/63 -> 50/58/67 across 0/+2/+4, Poor 21/24 -> 28/30 across +6/+9 - workstream 5's fading catches banked without losing a differential frame, never worse anywhere measured. Deliberately NOT the default (the CPU doubling is an operator's call); masks continue to pin the single-detector default, so no mask moves. `EnsembleDetectionTests` pins the mechanism: union delivery, one-delivery dedupe, duplicate-detector collapse, byte-identical transmit, and the loud refusal on non-bpsk modes.

- **2026-08-08 - ARDOP off-air, the first wild-corpus campaign result: ConAck decode aligned to reference policy (M0LTE.Ardop 0.4.0), the monitor centre corrected, and the top-rung null confirmed at reference parity.** The ARDOP campaign (docs/dev/archive/ardop/plan.md) put the A0 wild baseline - 636 frames acquired, 551 bodies ok, RXO over ~45 h of the 40 m capture - to ardopcf 1.0.4.1.3 frame by frame, over bit-identical audio via the new `ardop-cut`/`--emit` instruments (#258). Three results belong here. **(1) ConAck500 was the reference's only clean win, 5-0, and it was policy, not demodulation**: ardopcf's `Decode4FSKConACK` (SoundInput.c:3059) initialises Timing to 0 and accepts on `Timing >= 0`, so a body whose three timing bytes have no 2-of-3 majority still passes, reported as "timing 0 ms" - all five disputes were exactly that case, and deployed ISS peers proceed on such ConAcks (our ARQ engine dropped them in `IssConReq`, stalling connections a reference peer completes). M0LTE.Ardop 0.4.0 (M0LTE.Ardop#3, v0.4.0 on merge 7616ce9) accepts a ConAck on successful type decode and reports `ConAckLeaderMs` null without a majority, refusing to fabricate the reference's 0 ms; a loopback test pins it and the package's ardopcf oracle legs stay green (368/368). Corpus A/B bounded to the chunks both scans processed: **exactly 5 verdict flips @1650 and 8 @1500, every one ConAck500 fail->ok, nothing else moved** (wild ConAck500 now 35/35 at the corrected centre). **(2) The monitor's wild-slot centre was ~150 Hz wrong**: the A1 sweep measured true session centres clustering 1500-1560 Hz audio (48 of 52 cuts; the survey estimator's ~1650 was biased), and the corrected-centre rescan acquired **749/605 against 636/551** - the acquisition loss A0 called invisible, measured at +18 %. `ardop-monitor` now defaults to 1500 (native, unshifted). Honest negatives: the 4FSK.500.100 body rate did NOT improve at the corrected centre (50->47 ok of 84->90; its failures are GB7BPQ's half-hourly FEC beacon at the decode threshold, which ardopcf also fails 31 of 34 times), and the 1680-1760 Hz outlier sessions fall outside ±200 of 1500 (8PSK 3->2, 16QAM.2000 1->0 acquisitions). **(3) The wild 16QAM/8PSK zero is a confirmed null at reference parity**: every top-rung body fails in ardopcf too, at every trial centre (4 of 11 never acquired; the corrected centre grew the sample to 17 16QAM bodies, still zero) - weak one-sided copies, not a decoder gap, exactly the design doc's §3.6 thin-margin prediction. Genuine per-frame disputes net 9-4 in our favour. **Not proven: any live ARQ session exercising the new ConAck acceptance** (the corpus is RXO; the majority-less ConAck path has bench and wild-replay evidence only), and nothing here touches transmit.

- **2026-08-08 (later) - ARDOP joins the mask discipline: the sim seam lands and workstream 0's recorded gap closes.** The campaign's A3 leg (docs/dev/archive/ardop/plan.md) gave ARDOP the maskable sim baseline every other non-FM mode already had. `ArdopFrameProbe` is the `DatacPacketProbe` pattern applied to the session TNC: the library's own modulator renders a full-capacity even data frame, the shared Watterson rig injects the channel (same `SimChannel.Apply`, same deterministic seeds), a fresh default-constructed demodulator (ardopcf-default squelch 5 / ±100 Hz) decodes, and the score is payload byte-exact - driven as `sm-ota sim --mode ardop:<FrameName>` through the same `SimBench.RunPoint` the mask rows call, so a mask number and a CLI number are one measurement. The wild-relevant six-frame ladder measured at N=100: AWGN knees at ~-5 (4FSK.200.50S), ~-2.5 (4PSK.200.100S), ~-1.5 (4FSK.500.100, CFO-flat to 50 Hz), ~+1.5 (4PSK.500.100), ~+10 (8PSK.500.100) and ~+11 dB (16QAM.500.100); fading anchors per type in the plan's table. Pinned: 8 blocking smoke rows (N=25, floors 2-3 binomial sigma under measured) and 23 SM_MASK_GATE full-tier rows in `WattersonMaskTests`; the suite's coverage-rule paragraph and rx-roadmap workstream 0 both record the closure. Two measured truths worth the ink: **the top rungs are unusable on fading channels** (8PSK/16QAM decode essentially nothing under CCIR Moderate/Poor at any plausible NVIS SNR - the wild zero-of-eleven was overdetermined: weak copies AND a channel class the waveform cannot carry), and **the single-shot ceiling is ~98 %, not 100 %, and it is the receiver** (certain noise realisations false-trigger the leader detector during the lead-in and the capture swallows the frame; seeds 18/27 fail at every AWGN SNR +10..+25 and decode at +40; ARQ retries absorb it in deployment; a leader re-arm improvement is a named future receiver leg). **Not proven: nothing here is on-air evidence** - the masks pin the receiver against the model, and the wild corpus (A0-A2) stays the truth about the band; Rung 5 remains outstanding.

- **2026-08-08 (later2) - bpsk300's symbol clock is measured to be at its own ceiling: perfect timing is worth nothing, and rx-roadmap workstream 4's ~0.5 dB timing claim is retired.** Sized before building, as workstream 3 was. The instrument is `sm-ota oracle` over a new bench seam (`BpskDemodulator.SymbolInstantObserver` / `SymbolInstantSchedule`, `TimingOracleSeamTests`): the Watterson rig draws its fades from the burst seed BEFORE its noise, so the same seed at infinite SNR replays the identical channel with the noise left off, and a pass over that noise-free copy records the sample index of every symbol instant for the measured arms to decode the NOISY copy at. The DPLL keeps running in every arm - transitions, `PacketDcd` and so the deframer's gating held constant - so the arms differ in one variable, when each symbol is read. Four arms: causal (deployed), oracle (noise-free instants), grid (a perfect constant-rate clock at the noise-free phase - the transmitted symbol grid, and the best a perfectly smoothed non-causal estimate could ever be), and a control slipped half a symbol which **decoded 0 of 200 at every rung of 5600 bursts**, which is what licenses reading a null off the rest. Measured single-branch bpsk300, N=200/rung, seed 1 (docs/dev/archive/bench/timing-oracle-2026-08-08.txt): pooled **causal 2774, grid 2763 (-0.2 points), oracle 2591 (-3.3 points)**; at the AWGN knees the perfect clock buys +3 and +6 frames against a ~70 frames/dB slope, i.e. **under 0.1 dB**. Two mechanisms recorded: at 40 samples per symbol behind an RRC matched filter the timing-error slope is gentle enough that DPLL jitter costs under a tenth of a dB, and on a fading channel there are two candidate true clocks (the transmitted grid, and the composite's instantaneous group delay as paths swap dominance) so sampling perfectly at either loses to the DPLL's inertia-limited compromise - the inertia IS the smoothing a second pass would be trying to discover. A prior suspicion is withdrawn with it: DPLL timing wander during dominance swaps is a symptom of the fade, not an independent cause of Poor's ceiling (a perfect clock rescues none of those frames). A lead is recorded rather than built: the hindsight arm (decode at all 40 phases, count if any delivers) reads 70/100 vs 34 at AWGN -5 dB, which is selectable diversity rather than an oracle since a frame passes IL2P RS+CRC or it does not - but the deployed 9-branch bank already scores 56/88/95 at -5/-4/-3 dB on the same seeds, so at most ~14 and ~7 points remain at the knees before overlap, against a proportional rise in false-accept exposure. **Nothing in the receive path changes behaviour**: the seam is inert unless a schedule is set, the bpsk300 sim rows reproduce main's recorded 56/88/95 exactly, and the full mask tier is green (65/65). An instrument fault found en route is on the record because it looked like physics: the first displacement profile read exactly 0/100 across its negative half, which was a schedule stalling on entries that fell before the first sample, not a receiver that could not decode; the matcher now steps over passed instants and a test pins it.

- **2026-08-08 (later3) - the FM modes get a channel at last: a physical FM link model, the first FM ladders, and five blocking FM masks.** rx-roadmap workstream 6 item 6. `FmChannel` (tests/Channel, linked into sm-ota like the Watterson rig) does not approximate FM with a curve: the modem's audio is frequency-modulated onto a carrier, complex noise is added *there* at a stated carrier-to-noise ratio, and a limiter and discriminator bring it back, through the radio's real audio paths (microphone pre-emphasis and 300-3000 Hz passband, or a flat data port) and its IF filter. Every FM impairment therefore emerges rather than being asserted, and `FmChannelTests` pins them as physics: the output falls away faster per dB below the threshold knee than above it; discriminator noise power rises with the square of audio frequency and de-emphasis flattens it; under-deviation costs output SNR; a microphone path will not pass a 4 kHz tone; flutter drags a link under threshold at a mean CNR a static link survives. The axis is **CNR in the receiver IF bandwidth**, deliberately NOT the SSB modes' SNR3k - the two are different quantities and a number moved between them would be wrong, so `--channel fm-mic|fm-data` says so in its help and the masks say so in their failure message. **First measured FM ladders** (N=50, seed 1, TXDELAY 150 ms, 50 % knees): `afsk1200-il2p` +8 dB through mic and speaker and +7.5 through a data port; `fsk9600-il2p` +10.5 (data port, 25 kHz); `c4fsk9600` +19.5 narrow and +17.7 wide; `c4fsk19200` +16.5 (25 kHz). Three findings worth the ink. **(1)** The model reproduces, unprompted, the reason every 9600 packet station needs a data port: `fsk9600-il2p` through a microphone path decodes **0 of 50 at +15, +21 and +27 dB** - not a threshold, a structural impossibility, and it is now a blocking test. **(2)** Channel spacing is worth 4.5 dB and is part of the mode: `fsk9600` first measured a ~15 dB knee on a narrow channel before being corrected to the wide channel it is deployed on (Tom's ground truth, 2026-08-08: C4FSK 9600 is the narrow-channel mode, C4FSK 19200 the wide one, per MMDVM-TNC and NinoTNC practice); mode-modulation-reference.md now carries a channel column, and `c4fsk9600` is measured to pay ~1.8 dB for fitting into narrow. **(3)** A noiseless FM link is not arbitrarily clean - full deviation on a low tone spreads sidebands past a narrow IF filter and symmetric truncation returns as odd-order distortion (third harmonic ~-25 dB on 8 kHz), which is the ceiling a dense constellation meets before it meets noise. **Masks**: five blocking FM rows plus the microphone-path negative; smoke tier still ~40 s; full tier green. One methodological correction paid for by CI: the first cut pinned `fsk9600` at +12 dB CNR, which is the steepest part of an FM cliff (22 % at +9, 90 % at +12, 100 % at +15). It passed locally and failed on the runner, because a mask row on a cliff measures the machine's floating point as much as the modem - FM curves are far steeper than the SSB ones these masks were designed around. Every FM row now sits above its knee on the flat, and the knees live in the ladder tables where they belong. **Not proven: none of this is calibrated against a real radio.** The numbers are the model's own, so an FM mask means "no regression against the model"; tying them to hardware is the `sm-ota ladder` FM route over the Flex/RSP1 rig, and that leg has not run. Nothing transmitted changed, and no receive path was touched.

- **2026-08-08 (later4) - NinoTNC v3/4.43's switch map corrects our catalogue, and in doing so exposes a real defect in the day-old FM channel model.** Tom supplied the v43 release notes; they are ground truth and we were wrong in three places (all now fixed in docs/dev/mode-modulation-reference.md, which gains the full v43 switch map with Nino's occupied-bandwidth figures). **(1) QPSK 3600 is switch 0101 alone, on a 1650 Hz carrier, and Nino groups it with the FM AFSK modes as a speaker/mic mode** - our table gave it 2079 Hz and "0001, 0101", conflating it with C4FSK 19200 (switch 0001 alone). `ModemCatalog` was already right at 1650 Hz; only the documentation was wrong. **(2) The shaped PSK modes (300/1200 BPSK, 600/2400 QPSK) are documented as suitable for SSB radios *or FM radios*** - we listed them SSB-only, and the FM channel model still refuses them for want of a deviation figure, now a recorded gap. **(3) Nino's OBW figures independently confirm the narrow/wide split**: 10 kHz for 9600 C4FSK and 4800 GFSK, 20 kHz for 9600 GFSK and 19200 C4FSK. **The defect the notes exposed:** the release notes predict 3600 AQPSK works through a microphone and speaker, and our model said it could not decode through one at any SNR - including 0/20 at +40 dB CNR, which is a path fault, not noise. A multitone probe found the microphone path tilted 15 dB across 500-2800 Hz: `FmChannel`'s pre-emphasis was a plausible-looking shelf with a separately-derived coefficient rather than the algebraic inverse of its one-pole de-emphasis, so the pair never cancelled. AFSK shrugged the tilt off, which is why every AFSK measurement looked healthy and nothing caught it; QPSK, having no equaliser, could not. Fixed by making the pair exactly inverse (same coefficient, de-emphasis applied at the modem's rate so it cancels precisely), and pinned by the test that was missing, `Emphasis_Round_Trips_Flat_Through_A_Matched_Pair` - measured with a multitone, because peak-normalised drive cancels a single tone's pre-emphasis and would measure the instrument rather than the channel. Consequences measured: **qpsk3600 now decodes through mic and speaker, 25/25 at +18 dB CNR** (NinoTNC's claim corroborated by our own decoder, and now mask-pinned on both fm-mic and fm-data), and every microphone-path number improved - `afsk1200-il2p` on fm-mic went 24/25 to 25/25 at +15, its mask floor rising with it. Also recorded from v43 for the capture campaign: pre-v43 firmware sometimes sent the first frame after an ID beacon **with no preamble**, which is structurally unacquirable and a candidate explanation for isolated corpus misses; v43 adds a **Morse ID for the HF modes**, so CW in an HF slot is now expected; and v43 widened the 300 AFSK transmit waveform and changed AFSK demodulator 1's correlator gain, so any NinoTNC interop A/B should record the firmware revision. **Open and not resolved: qpsk3600's 5.0 kHz deviation figure is disputed** - it appears inherited from the C4FSK 19200 row, a speaker/mic mode at 5.0 kHz on a narrow channel is over-deviated, and it measures that way (83-87 % plateau even at +40 dB CNR against 100 % at 3.0 kHz). The sim keeps the recorded 5.0 kHz because a calibration figure should not move on inference; it needs confirming with Nino before anyone transmits the mode.

- **2026-08-08 (later5) - a correction to the entry above, and a new open defect it uncovered: qpsk3600 decodes WORSE as the signal gets stronger.** The previous entry claimed qpsk3600's 5.0 kHz deviation figure "measures over-deviated", citing an 83-87 % plateau at +40 dB CNR against 100 % at 3.0 kHz and attributing it to IF truncation. Tom challenged it ("could just be your implementation?") and he was right: **the mechanism claim is falsified.** Controls: widening the IF does NOT fix the 5.0 kHz case (it gets worse - 90 % at +24 falling to 77 % at +40), and qpsk3600 degrades at high signal on a linear AWGN channel too, where no IF filter exists. The deviation figure remains doubtful **on provenance alone** (inherited from the C4FSK 19200 row it was wrongly sharing; 5.0 kHz is a wide-channel deviation for a mode Nino groups with the speaker/mic modes) and still needs Nino. **What the controls turned up instead is a real and previously unseen defect.** On the FM channel through a data port - flat audio, no emphasis, wide IF, so neither truncation nor the microphone path is involved - `qpsk3600` scores 30/30 at +18 dB CNR and then 73 %, 77 %, 70 % at +24, +30 and +40. On linear AWGN the same shape appears in miniature: 99/97/95/94/97 % across +15/+20/+25/+30/+40 at N=100. Four things ruled out by measurement: **not the channel model** (afsk1200-il2p and c4fsk9600 both 25/25 at +40 dB CNR on the same path); **not the shared QPSK code** (qpsk600 and qpsk2400 both 25/25 at +20/+30/+40 on AWGN); **not acquisition** (TXDELAY 150, 300 and 600 ms all give exactly 21/30, and the same frames fail each time); **not deviation or truncation** (wider IF is worse). That leaves the mode: qpsk3600 is the only catalogue mode at a non-integer 6 2/3 samples per symbol, and docs/dev/archive/qpsk/plan.md already recorded one clean-signal regression peculiar to it at that ratio. Hypothesis, untested: noise dithers a coarsely-quantised sampling instant and a clean signal does not. **Operationally this is the regime the mode is most used in** - a strong local signal is the normal case for FM packet - so it deserves its own leg, and the timing-oracle seam from workstream 4 is the instrument for it. Also settled this round: **afsk1200 is a narrow-channel mode**, justified rather than assumed - Bell 202's 1200/2200 Hz tones at 3.0 kHz deviation give a Carson bandwidth of 10.4 kHz, landing in Nino's own 10 kHz OBW bucket alongside 9600 C4FSK and 4800 GFSK, both of which fit 12.5 kHz. The sim already modelled it narrow; now it is on the record why.

- **2026-08-08 (later6) - the qpsk3600 high-signal anomaly is explained: a non-integer 6.667 samples per symbol, with QtSoundModem showing both the mechanism and the fix.** Tom pointed at the QtSoundModem source (GPLv3, the sanctioned cross-check per CLAUDE.md). Its `ax25_demod.c` settles it. **QtSoundModem never lets its symbol sampler see a fractional samples-per-symbol ratio**: `make_core_INTR` interpolates each PSK mode by `n_INTR = baud/300` - **6 for SPEED_Q3600** - and `decode_stream_QPSK` then hardcodes `baudrate = 300`, so every PSK mode reaches the decision point at exactly 40 samples per symbol. Its sampler is a different design from ours as well: an exponentially averaged envelope per position-within-symbol (`bit_buf[sample_cnt]`, 0.95/0.05) whose peak position selects the decision sample, an estimator that *improves* as noise falls - the opposite of what we measured. Our `qpsk3600` runs natively at 12 kHz, i.e. **6.667 samples per symbol**, and it is the only catalogue mode with a non-integer ratio; `qpsk600` (40), `qpsk2400` (10) and `bpsk300` (40) are integers and are exactly the modes that show no effect. **Confirmed by direct measurement** (AWGN +40 dB, N=30): 28/30 at 12 kHz (6.667 sps), 29/30 at 10.8 kHz (6.000), **30/30 at 36 kHz (20.000)**; and on the FM path where the effect is severe, 20 samples/symbol removes it entirely - **30/30 at both +24 and +40 dB CNR against 26/30 and 21/30 at 6.667**. **The fix must be internal interpolation in the receive path, not a catalogue rate change**: no divisor of the 48 kHz soundcard rate yields an integer samples-per-symbol at 1800 baud (48000/1800 = 26.667), which is presumably why the mode sits at 12 kHz. Interpolating by 3 (20 sps) or 6 (40 sps, matching QtSoundModem) inside `QpskDemodulator` is the change; **not built, and no mask moves until it is**. Recorded because it is operationally real: a strong local signal is the normal case for FM packet, so this is the regime the mode is most used in, and the mode was validated at 30/30 on clean audio in the QPSK campaign precisely because that campaign never measured it above +18 dB.

- **2026-08-08 (later7) - qpsk3600's high-signal anomaly is FIXED: the differential decode chain now runs on a whole number of samples per symbol, and the mode improves everywhere measured.** Tom: "land the full fix, nobody uses pdn-soundmodem yet". `QpskDemodulator` upsamples its differential decode chain by the smallest whole factor that makes samples-per-symbol an integer - **3 for both of qpsk3600's rates** (6.667 -> 20 at 12 kHz, 26.667 -> 80 at 48 kHz) - which is QtSoundModem's own answer arrived at from the other end (`make_core_INTR` interpolates by `n_INTR = baud/300`, 6 for SPEED_Q3600, and `decode_stream_QPSK` hardcodes 300 baud so its sampler always sees exactly 40 samples per symbol; ax25_demod.c, GPLv3, the CLAUDE.md cross-check). Band-pass and energy detection stay on the input grid - they answer "is something there" and need no sub-sample resolution - so upsampling costs the decode chain only; coherent detection is untouched as the cross-check variant. **Measured A/B**: AWGN +15/+20/+25/+30/+40 dB goes 99/97/95/94/97 -> **100/100/100/100/100** at N=100 (the droop eliminated); fm-data +24/+30/+40 goes 22/23/21 -> **30/29/30** of 30; and the knee improves too - **AWGN +8 dB 57/100 -> 73/100** - so the coarse grid was costing sensitivity everywhere, not only at high signal. fm-data and fm-mic at +15 and +18 all reach 50/50 from 48-49/50. Unchanged: the three real recordings decode in full (NinoTNC hardware 3/3, QtSoundModem 10/10, our own 10/10), and qpsk600 (40 sps) and qpsk2400 (10 sps) keep a bit-identical sample path. **Masks**: the qpsk3600 fm-data floor rises 20 -> 21 of 25, and a **high-signal row is added** (fm-data at +30 dB CNR, floor 21/25, measured 25/25 and 19/25 before the fix) - the suite had no high-signal rows at all, which is exactly why a mode that decodes worse as the signal improves went unseen: every mask sat near a knee, where such a defect looks healthy. Two process notes worth keeping: the first attempt at this change silently did nothing to two lines because a `str.replace` pattern had gone stale, giving a chain that ran its filters at 36 kHz and its one-symbol delay ring at 6 samples - 0/30 everywhere, which at least fails loudly; and an earlier partial fix (interpolating the decision instant, PR #268) is retained only as the fallback for a hypothetical mode where no small whole factor works.

- **2026-08-09 - a station can run a modem this repository does not contain, and OFDM-FM is the first: the plugin seam lands, and the OFDM core moves out.** Two things, one because of the other. **(1) Runtime modem binding** (`docs/dev/modem-plugins.md`, designed 2026-08-08, built now): `IModemPlugin` + a public `ModemDescriptor`, a `ModemPluginRegistry`, and a `ModemPluginLoader` that loads an assembly from an explicit path into its own collectible `AssemblyLoadContext`. `modemPlugins` in the daemon config names the paths; nothing is scanned for, because a file appearing on disk must never change what a station transmits. Plugin modes are `pluginId:mode` prefixed, so a plugin mode can never shadow a built-in and an uninstalled plugin says "no modem plugin registered for 'ofdm-fm'" instead of denying the mode exists. **Registered modes deliberately do not join `KnownModes`** - that set is what this repository is answerable for and what five whole-catalogue sweeps iterate, including the pinned count; `AllModes` is built-ins plus registered. Which is why **this ledger's matrix has no OFDM-FM row and should not grow one**: the matrix tracks `KnownModes`, and a mode whose waveform is not in this repository is not a mode this repository can vouch for. **(2) The OFDM core moved to the private `packet-net/pdn-ofdm-fm`** and became a real `IModem` there. Not a licence problem, a buildability one: a public GPL work has to stay buildable and distributable by anyone who clones it, so it cannot contain or build against something we are not free to ship, and at the time OFDM-FM's carrier layouts could not be published. (Superseded on 2026-09-20: the modem now ships in this repository on layouts of our own, and the entry below records the move.) **Verified rather than assumed, four ways.** A fresh clone of this repository, with no plugin and no geometry file present, builds and passes 1748 tests - which is the entire point of the exercise. The rule that makes the load context work is pinned by a test that walks the contract's public surface and fails if it grows an assembly the host does not provide (it already has one beyond the obvious: `FrameQuality.HeaderType` is an `M0LTE.Il2p` enum, and a plugin's own `.deps.json` would happily resolve a private copy from the NuGet cache); deleting the shared-contract rule fails eight tests, so they are measuring it. With the plugin staged in a directory outside both repositories the daemon loads it, logs its four modes, and comes up on one of them at 48 kHz; with it absent it prints one named failure and either starts anyway or refuses by name if a modem wanted one of its modes. And two regressions against what the OFDM campaign recorded, because a move is exactly when a waveform quietly stops being the same waveform: the coded FM ladder reproduces its table (uncoded 8/8/8/2/0/0 identical, rate 1/2 one frame better at +20 dB on different seeds, so the 5-6 dB coding gain holds), and all 32 transmitted sample bursts - four profiles by eight constellations - come out at exactly the durations the 2026-08-08 set had, which pins the geometry, the header sizing and the framing. **The real work in the plugin was the streaming adapter**, and it is worth naming because the obvious implementation is wrong: the burst codec is whole-buffer, `IModem.Process` is a stream, and accumulating the stream to re-run a whole-buffer search over it is quadratic in the search and unbounded in the buffer. It runs the same self-correlation metric incrementally, tracks the plateau to its peak, and calls the codec's own `DecodeAt`; two tests hold it honest - one asserts the streaming search times a burst at the *same sample* the whole-buffer search does, and one feeds twenty seconds of noise and asserts the buffer never grows (deleting the compaction fails it). **Not proven, and the reason there is no status here to upgrade: no OFDM-FM mode has masks in either tier, no ladder row blocks CI, and none of it has been on the air.** What landed is reachability - a station *can* run it - not a validated mode.

- **2026-08-09 (later) - the FM channel model had a rate-dependent defect: its filters are specified in hertz now, every FM mask was re-measured through the fix, and none of them moved.** `FmChannel` was extracted to the public `M0LTE.FmChannel` package (#274) so this repository and the private OFDM one share one definition rather than a copy; the extraction found the defect and 0.3.0 fixes it. **Every filter in the model used a fixed tap count.** A windowed-sinc's transition width is roughly `rate/taps`, so a fixed count makes a filter twice as sloppy each time the sample rate doubles - which is not how a radio behaves, its audio filters being analogue and no wider because a sound card samples faster. The consequence was that the same waveform measured at two sample rates went through two different channels, with the higher rate penalised, so no measurement at one rate could honestly be compared with one at another: a bad property in an instrument whose entire job is comparison. Filters are now specified by shape and the tap count derived at whatever rate the stage runs at, across all four rate-dependent stages (IF filter, audio band limit, and both resampler anti-alias filters; the flutter generator's own filter already ran at a fixed low rate of its own). For a 300-3000 Hz microphone path that is 119 taps at 12 kHz where it was 127, 239 at 24 kHz and 475 at 48 kHz - the 12 kHz case barely moving is the shape you want, the sharpness being the same in hertz everywhere instead of accidentally best at the lowest rate. **Measured on the audio-band OFDM waveform**, 16 seeds a rung: before, 24 kHz scored 16/16/16/16/14/4/0 against 48 kHz 16/16/16/16/13/4/0 across +28 down to +10 dB CNR; after, 24 kHz 16/16/16/16/14/2/0 against 48 kHz 16/16/16/16/13/2/0. The two rates now agree within the noise of 16 seeds, which is what a rate-independent model has to do. **Every mask re-measured: the full `SM_MASK_GATE=1` tier is 74/74 green through 0.3.0 and no row moved in either direction**, so the five FM rows and the qpsk3600 high-signal row keep the floors the entries above set them. That is the outcome worth recording rather than a set of new numbers: the modes most exposed to the change are the 48 kHz ones (`fsk9600-il2p`, `c4fsk9600`, `c4fsk19200`), and they were unaffected. The masks are still the model's own numbers, so nothing here moves any mode toward on-air validation. **One instrument sensitivity found and left alone, because it is faithful rather than wrong**: the model calibrates drive from the burst's own peak, the way a bench does, so a waveform's peak-to-average ratio feeds back into its effective deviation and two bursts of different length or content are not driven identically. It is real behaviour, but it means a code-rate A/B on a single burst length is not a clean single-variable comparison, and it explained a puzzling 8-of-8 acquisition failure in the OFDM ladder that had nothing to do with the receiver.

- **2026-08-09 (later2) - OFDM-FM's channel estimate is denoised, and two plausible improvements measured nothing and were reverted.** In `packet-net/pdn-ofdm-fm`, recorded here because the negative results are the durable part and the private repository's `docs/receiver-findings.md` carries the detail. **What landed**: the preamble-derived channel estimate is one noisy division per carrier and it is that receiver's dominant impairment, since equalisation divides by it and the waveform always has carriers 20 dB down on the edges of a voice passband. The estimate is transformed to a delay profile, each tap shrunk by how much of it is signal rather than noise, and transformed back - the channel being a handful of filters, its energy sits in a short run of delays, while noise is spread over all of them. Measured 8 seeds through the FM link, both legs through FmChannel 0.3.0: uncoded 8/8/7/2/0/0 -> 8/8/8/5/0/0, rate 2/3 8/8/8/8/8/3 -> 8/8/8/8/8/7, rate 3/4 8/8/8/8/3/0 -> 8/8/8/8/7/0 across +40 to +16 dB CNR, with no rung going backwards. Two details are load-bearing and both were got wrong first: it must transform the occupied bins only (inventing values for the unoccupied ones rings, and rang hard enough to break QAM-256 on a *noiseless* loopback while QPSK never noticed), and the bulk delay must be removed before the transform and restored after (the burst is not sampled where the channel's response peaks, so the estimate carries a phase ramp of non-integer slope, and a non-integer ramp does not transform to a spike - it transforms to a sinc smeared over every tap, at which point the shrinkage eats the channel; it showed as a loss at *high* CNR, where there is nothing to denoise and only the distortion is visible). **What did not land, both built and measured before being reverted.** *Decision-directed estimation*: when a payload fails its CRC, slice every equalised carrier of every symbol to its nearest constellation point, average the residual over all of them (pilots free, being known exactly), fold it into the estimate and decode again - in principle beating a single-symbol preamble estimate by roughly the square root of the number of symbols. It won **0 of about 30 attempts**, across the plain ladder and a flutter ladder at 2, 5, 10 and 20 Hz Doppler; by the time a payload fails it is not marginally wrong, so slicing gives a reference too corrupt to help, and the cases a better estimate would have rescued are already taken by the denoiser, which is cheaper and runs on every burst. *Lowering the sync threshold*: the whole rate 3/4 rung at +16 dB was failing acquisition outright (worst normalised correlation 0.695 against a 0.8 threshold), which makes the threshold look like the binding constraint - but sweeping it to 0.6, 0.45 and 0.3 changes the ladder in **no cell at all**, acquisition succeeding and the payload failing instead. It was masking the failure, not causing it. **The finding worth acting on next** is the failure budget under flutter, counted by the stage that lost each frame: at 20 Hz Doppler and +20 dB CNR, of 14 lost frames **7 are the header**, 4 sync and 3 payload. The header is the only part of the burst with no coding on it - 40 uncoded bits, hard sliced, behind their own CRC - while the payload underneath survives conditions it does not. That is a waveform change rather than a receiver one, and it is now the largest single lever. **Still not proven, unchanged from the entry above: no OFDM-FM mode has masks in either tier and none of it has been on the air.**

- **2026-08-09 (later3) - OFDM-FM's header is coded and spread over two symbols, and the fix was decided by measuring the header on its own rather than by reasoning about it.** In `packet-net/pdn-ofdm-fm`; recorded here because the method is the transferable part. The entry above named the uncoded header as the largest single cause of lost frames on a fading link and called it the next lever. It was, but **not for the reason the name suggests**, and the instrument that settled it is the point: a header ladder that hands the receiver the sync position and counts headers read correctly out of bursts actually acquired, so acquisition and payload cannot contaminate the number. Measured that way the old header read **64 of 64 at every carrier-to-noise ratio where the burst could be acquired at all with the flutter off**, and 18 of 40 at +20 dB with 20 Hz of Doppler. It was never noise limited; it was fade limited. The flutter is a flat fade on the RF carrier, so all carriers go down together, and a header living inside one symbol has no diversity of any kind to exploit when that symbol is in a fade - no code, however strong, changes that. What a fading channel pays for is diversity order, and the only axis with any left is time. **So the header is now tail-biting convolutional K=9 at rate 1/2 (40 bits in, 80 out), soft-decision Viterbi, laid across TWO header symbols** by a stride coprime to the coded length so every coded bit appears in both. The sizing was done before building: adjacent symbols are tens of milliseconds apart at this geometry and the model's Gaussian Doppler shaping puts the fade correlation between them at 0.13 at 20 Hz, 0.60 at 10 Hz and 0.88 at 5 Hz, so the diversity lands where the header was broken and is barely needed at 5 Hz where it already read 44 of 46. Two details are load-bearing: the soft metric weights each carrier by `|H|^2` as before **and now each symbol by its own pilot-measured amplitude** (two symbols carrying copies of a bit are two different fades, and without that the faded one gets equal say and most of the second copy's value is discarded), and the bit-to-slot mapping walks all header symbols as one run of slots rather than assuming exactly two - the first version left most of the code untransmitted on profiles narrow enough to need three. **Measured end to end**, flutter failure budget, same instrument and seeds both legs, every burst counted once in the stage that lost it: at 20 Hz Doppler, +20 dB CNR **2/16 recovered becomes 8/16** (header bucket 7 -> 3), +24 dB 8/16 -> 12/16, +28 dB 13/16 -> 16/16; at 10 Hz +20 dB 7/16 -> 9/16; at 2 Hz +20 dB 10/16 -> 13/16. Over all twelve cells **134 frames recovered becomes 155 and the header bucket falls from 16 to 6**. The payload bucket falls too, 25 to 16, which is not a payload change but the consequence of ordered buckets: bursts that used to die at the header now reach a payload that is coded and interleaved over ten symbols and was always the more robust half, so a fragile header had been discarding bursts that were perfectly decodable underneath it. **Costs**: one extra symbol on every burst, which on the plain non-fading ladder shows as the uncoded row losing two frames at +24 dB and rate 2/3 gaining one at +16 - the channel model calibrating drive from the burst's own peak the way a bench does, so a longer burst has a higher expected peak and marginally less average deviation. **Two tests were passing for the wrong reason and are fixed rather than deleted**: both claimed to wreck samples "past preamble and header, into the payload symbols" and both computed that offset as a hand-counted symbol index which was in fact the header symbol; they passed because wrecking the old header killed the burst, and the fault only became visible because the new header survives one wrecked symbol, which is exactly what it is for. **What this does not settle**: sync is now the largest bucket (15 lost frames against the header's 6) and the recorded threshold sweep warns that acquisition failures at the bottom rung were masking payload failures rather than causing them, so that is the next leg and it needs its own instrument first. **Still not proven, unchanged: no OFDM-FM mode has masks in either tier and none of it has been on the air.** Worth stating plainly for anyone reading the Doppler figures: the axis is Doppler *spread*, not Doppler *shift* - a carrier offset is irrelevant to an FM link, which turns it into a DC offset well below the waveform's lowest subcarrier. What is being modelled is mobile flutter, the rate at which a moving station's multipath pattern sweeps past, `v/lambda`: 6.3 Hz for 13 m/s at 145 MHz and 19 Hz at 433 MHz, 14.5 and 43 Hz at 30 m/s. So the rows that were broken are a station driving, and the 0 Hz row - fixed antennas, base to base - is precisely where the old header was already perfect.

- **2026-08-09 (later4) - a correction to the entry above: the header result holds at 64 seeds and is stronger than stated, but one of its claims was noise and is withdrawn.** The header change was landed on a 16-seed budget, which was enough to see the effect and not enough to quote it properly. Both legs were re-run at **64 seeds a cell**, in a git worktree of its own so that nothing else building in the checkout could contaminate them, with two-sided Fisher exact tests against every bucket. **What holds, and is now stated in the right place: the header bucket falls from 53 of 768 to 25, p = 0.0016.** That is the direct measure of the thing that was changed and the only strongly significant number in the set; frames recovered follow it, 580 of 768 to 616, p = 0.031. Per cell at 20 Hz Doppler: +28 dB 50/64 -> 56/64, +24 dB 37/64 -> 46/64, +20 dB 12/64 -> 23/64; at 10 Hz +20 dB 32/64 -> 40/64. **What is withdrawn: the claim that the payload bucket also fell.** The 16-seed run showed it going 25 to 16 and the entry above explained that as bursts which used to die at the header now reaching a payload that decodes. It was noise and the explanation was a story fitted to it - at 64 seeds the payload bucket is 82 against 87, unchanged (p = 0.74), and the ordering of the buckets in fact predicts a slight RISE, because bursts that newly survive the header at 20 Hz are precisely the ones most likely to fail the payload. The general lesson is worth the ink: a bucket that moves the way your story predicts is not evidence for the story until it moves further than noise would, and buckets that are ordered rather than independent will always tempt one into a narrative. **A second methodological point, made against my own write-up:** the headline quoted above and in PR #276 was a single cell, 2 of 16 against 8 of 16, and that cell did not clear significance at 16 seeds at all (p = 0.054). It does at 64 (p = 0.047), but it was the weakest evidence in the set and it was put at the front of the argument; the aggregate and the header bucket were always the stronger claims and should have led. **Also now visible with the seeds to see it, and honest about cost:** the extra symbol is paid for where the diversity is worth least. At 5 Hz Doppler the two header symbols are 0.88 correlated so there is little to gain, and that is the only column that goes backwards - 50 of 64 recovered becomes 47 at +20 dB and 60 becomes 58 at +28. Neither is significant alone (p = 0.68 for the first) but the sign is what the theory predicts, so it is probably real and small. **Unchanged: no OFDM-FM mode has masks in either tier, none of it has been on the air, and no mask in this repository moves.** The seed count is now an environment variable (OFDMFM_SEEDS) so the deep run reproduces without an edit.

- **2026-08-09 (later5) - a second and larger correction to the header entries above: the coding was the whole win and the time diversity was worth nothing, which two dated entries got backwards.** Tom, reading the write-up: "I highly doubt that this modem will ever be used for mobile operation tbh. Seems an odd thing to optimise for." He was right, and measuring it properly says so more strongly than the challenge did. **The change did two separable things and the entries credited the wrong one.** It CODED the header (tail-biting convolutional K=9 rate 1/2, 40 bits in, 80 out, soft Viterbi), which costs **no air time at all** because 80 coded bits fit inside the single symbol the uncoded header already used on every real profile (even on the narrowest). And it REPEATED the header into a second symbol for time diversity, which costs a whole symbol, about 8 % of a short burst. Three arrangements measured at 64 seeds a cell over fifteen cells from no flutter out to 20 Hz Doppler, 960 bursts each: **uncoded one symbol 772/960 recovered (53 lost to the header, 82 to the payload); coded one symbol 806/960 (32, 79); coded two symbols 808/960 (25, 87).** Uncoded to coded is +34 frames, p = 0.049, for nothing. **Coded-one-symbol to coded-two-symbols is +2 frames, p = 0.95** - indistinguishable from nothing, for 8 % more air time. Every bit of the gain was the free part; none of it was the part the entire design argument rested on. **The mechanism was not wrong, which is what makes it worth the ink.** A flat fade does take one symbol and usually not the next, and the second copy does exactly what it was built to do: header losses fall from 32 to 25. It gains nothing overall because a burst 8 % longer is 8 % more exposed to the next dropout, so the payload loses as many frames as the header saves (79 becomes 87). It trades a header failure for a payload failure, roughly one for one. Air time on a burst that has to survive the same channel attacks the same quantity the benefit does, and nothing but an end-to-end measurement across the whole burst was ever going to show it. **The general lesson, which is the transferable part: a mechanism being real does not make it worth its cost, and a stage-level measurement cannot see a cost that lands on a different stage.** The header ladder was the right instrument for choosing what to fix and the wrong one for deciding whether the fix was worth it; the failure budget across all stages was the one that could tell. **What is in the code now**: coded header, ONE symbol, `OfdmFmParameters.HeaderRepeats` defaulting to 1. A fixed station - which is what this modem is for - gets the coding free and pays nothing for a fade it never sees; measured with no flutter at all every arrangement reads 64 of 64 at every level tested, which is the other half of the challenge: **the header was never failing for a fixed station, so there was nothing there to rescue**. The setting is kept rather than deleted only because the trade depends on payload length (at 64 bytes the extra symbol is 8 % of the burst, at 256 bytes nearer 2 %, and nobody has measured whether the header saving survives at that ratio), with a doc comment warning against raising it on the strength of the mechanism sounding right. **An instrument fault en route is on the record because it produced a table that looked like a result**: a three-legged comparison reverted a source file the tests still referenced, so the third leg's build failed with six errors and the runner silently re-used the previous binary, giving numbers byte-identical to the leg before it. Identical columns gave it away. Build error counts are checked now rather than glanced at. **Unchanged: no OFDM-FM mode has masks in either tier, none of it has been on the air, and no mask in this repository moves.**

- **2026-08-09 (later6) - a self-inflicted channel-model error, caught by the mask suite within the hour, and the genuinely useful finding underneath it: C4FSK 19200 cannot pass a Tait TM8100's IF at all.** Tom supplied a full TM8100/TM8200 documentation set (20 manuals, 1083 pages, at M0LTE/tait-tm8100-tm8200-docs) with the instruction not to trust its index but to go back to the markdown, which is what every figure below was read from. **The error first.** The service manual measures Tait's own total IF 3 dB bandwidths at **12.6 kHz wide, 12.0 kHz medium, 7.8 kHz narrow** (MMA-00005-05 issue 5, p.73, Table 3.1; 12.0/9.0/7.6 on the K5 band), against `FmChannel.IfBandwidthForSpacing`'s generic 16 kHz and 8 kHz. Since the carrier-to-noise axis is *defined* in that bandwidth, that looked like a citable 1.1 dB error in the instrument, and M0LTE.FmChannel 0.4.0 duly replaced the generic figures with Tait's. **That was wrong**: one manufacturer's measurements do not belong in a function whose argument is a channel spacing, and substituting them made the library less accurate for every radio that is not a Tait while looking more authoritative - the exact failure mode this ledger has recorded before, and the one the research brief for this very extraction warned agents against. **The mask suite caught it on the first full run**: `c4fsk19200` at fm-data +24 dB CNR went from its pinned 25/25 to **0 of 25**, a collapse rather than a degradation. 0.4.1 restores the generic figures and all 74 mask rows are green again with no row moved, so nothing in this repository's pinned numbers changed in either direction. **The finding underneath is worth more than the error cost.** The collapse was physically correct and is now measured rather than inferred: C4FSK 19200 occupies about 20 kHz (Nino's own OBW figure, recorded in the 2026-08-08 later4 entry), and a 12.6 kHz IF does not attenuate that, it truncates it. **So C4FSK 19200 will not work through a TM8100 on a 25 kHz channel at all, and by the same arithmetic neither will 9600 GFSK**, whose OBW is also 20 kHz. That is a property of the radio, not a defect in either mode, and it is an operational fact for anyone building a station on TM8100s - the fast NinoTNC modes are off the table on that hardware whatever is done to the modem. It also sharpens the case for the radio-model work Tom asked for: a caller who wants a Tait should ask for a Tait and get all its figures together, including the ones that say a mode does not fit, rather than a generic helper quietly carrying one radio's IF filter and nobody else's. **Also extracted from the set and recorded in docs/hardware/tait-tm8100-cm108.md (#279)**, all cited: 100% deviation is +/-2.5 kHz NB, +/-4 kHz MB, +/-5.0 kHz WB (MMA-00072-03 p.6), and Tait's own internal 1200 baud modem defaults to 60% of the class ceiling; the audio tap out is a 600 ohm source on a +2.4 V pedestal behind a 12 kHz Butterworth (MMA-00005-05 p.84, board values from MMAB12-B1-00-814); the tap in is 4k7 series on a +1.5 V internal bias with 10 nF at the connector; RSSI is specified over **-115 to -50 dBm** (MMA-00072-03 p.14) with the firmware carrying values to -119 dBm, and 12 dB SINAD is measured at -121 dBm for B1 narrowband, which together answer what a low RSSI reading can and cannot mean. **Two things the documents do NOT contain**, searched exhaustively and stated so that an empty search reads as an answer: no input-level-to-deviation figure at any tap in either direction, so drive must be bench-calibrated per station (the note gives a Bessel-null method needing only an SDR); and **no occurrence of "T13" anywhere in the 1083 pages** - the only tap-in points Tait document are r2, r5, t1 and t5, the only transmit tap-in ever configured in a programming form is T5, and Tait qualify their own transmitter response as "below limiting" for a tap-injected signal (p.480), so **the hard limiter is downstream of every documented transmit tap**. If a mode is to be injected at T5 it meets the 300 Hz high pass, pre-emphasis, that limiter and the 3 kHz low pass, which matters to any waveform whose lowest carrier sits near that corner. **Unchanged: no mask moved, and none of this is on-air evidence** - it is documentation, and the bench tests that would settle the tap question are named in the interface note.

- **2026-08-09 (later7) - the claim in the entry above that C4FSK 19200 and 9600 GFSK cannot pass a TM8100's IF is WITHDRAWN. It was wrong twice over, and Tait's own product disproves it.** Tom: "Nonsense. The radio channel needs setting to wide, that's it. Think you need to read the manual again." He is right. **The disproof, from the same documentation set** (MMA-00038-06 p.9): "The over-air data rate is 1200 or 2400 bit/s for FFSK data, **12 kbit/s for Tait High Speed Data (THSD) narrow band and wide band, and can be set to 19200 bit/s for THSD wide band**." So 19200 bit/s over the air fits the 12.6 kHz wide-channel IF, and 12 kbit/s fits the **7.8 kHz narrow-channel** IF, in that same radio, as a shipped Tait feature. A 12.5 kHz channel carrying 12 kbit/s is 1.54 bit/s per hertz of IF, which is unremarkable for a well-designed mode and far beyond what the withdrawn claim said was possible. **Error one: the 9600 GFSK half was pure extrapolation, and the measurement disproving it was in the file being quoted from.** The claim was extended from C4FSK 19200 to 9600 GFSK "by the same arithmetic" with nothing measured. The 0.4.0 mask run had `fsk9600-il2p` at fm-data **passing 25 of 25 through the same 12.6 kHz IF**, one line above the failure that prompted the claim. **Error two, and the transferable one: comparing a 99 % occupied bandwidth against an IF 3 dB bandwidth and concluding a mode does not fit is not sound reasoning.** Occupied bandwidth includes low-energy tails, an IF filter has skirts rather than walls, and `FmChannel`'s IF filter is a windowed sinc with a transition of 15 % of its bandwidth, which is sharper than a crystal-plus-FPGA cascade. **So the `c4fsk19200` collapse at 12.6 kHz (0 of 25, against 25 of 25 at 16 kHz) is now a defect to investigate rather than a finding to report**, and the prime suspect is already on this ledger: the 2026-08-08 (later5) entry flags that mode's **5.0 kHz deviation figure as doubtful on provenance**, inherited from a row qpsk3600 was also wrongly sharing. At 5.0 kHz deviation and 9600 symbols per second Carson's rule gives 19.6 kHz, which is exactly the too-wide signal that dies in a 12.6 kHz filter, and Tait's own 19200 bit/s mode evidently does not occupy anything like that. **Nothing in the code changed and no mask moved**: 0.4.1 restored the generic bandwidths before any of this, all 74 rows are green, and the correction is to the conclusion rather than to any number. What the round leaves behind is a genuine open item - **verify c4fsk19200's deviation figure against NinoTNC before trusting any measurement of that mode**, since a wrong deviation makes every simulated bandwidth result about it meaningless - and one reusable warning, which is that a claim of the form "this mode cannot work on this hardware" contradicts whatever the manufacturer ships and should be checked against that before it is written down."

- **2026-08-10 - a deviation sweep across every FM mode: two modes are leaving 3 to 4 dB on the table, two are already right, one is not established, and qpsk3600 drops to 2500 Hz which is free and reduces its emission.** Fourteen agents, six modes swept on the FM channel model and each independently re-run by a second agent; verifier numbers are the ones quoted. **Measured, mode by mode**: `fsk9600-il2p` 2400 -> 5000 Hz is **+4.1 dB** (knees +9.89 to +5.76 dB CNR, N=400, reproduced bit-for-bit on an independent build and seam, +4.12 on an independent seed block, and it GROWS to +5.4 dB on a narrower 11 kHz IF); `fsk4800-il2p` 1200 -> 2500 Hz is **+3.4 dB** pooled over two 200-burst blocks, with the instrument calibrating against theory below stock (1000 -> 1200 Hz measures +1.62 dB against +1.58 predicted by the deviation-squared law); `c4fsk9600` and `c4fsk19200` are **already at their class ceilings and gain 0 dB in class**, every deviation below being strictly worse, which makes them the controls that prove the seam; `afsk1200-il2p` is **not established** because the recommendation's sign flips with the modelled IF bandwidth, +1.2 dB at 8 kHz against -0.6 dB at 10 to 12 kHz, and its own mask channel was never swept; `qpsk3600` 5000 -> 2500 Hz is **+0.4 dB at the knee and takes Carson bandwidth from 15.4 to 10.4 kHz on a 12.5 kHz channel**. **Only the last is applied.** It is a level on phase modulation of a 1650 Hz audio carrier so the far end normalises it, it REDUCES emission rather than increasing it, it brings a 200 %-of-ceiling mode onto its channel class for the first time, and the 5.0 kHz it replaces is the one figure in the table already doubted on provenance. All 74 mask rows stay green at the new deviation, including all three qpsk3600 rows. **The divergence is expressed as a divergence, not an edit**: `FmModeProfile` gains `RecommendedPeakDeviationHz` alongside `TargetPeakDeviationHz`, because the latter is a record of what NinoTNC publishes and overwriting it would falsify that record and leave nobody able to see that a disagreement had happened; a test refuses any divergence without a stated reason. The Bessel-null tone consequence is recorded with it: an operator following the documented procedure needs 1039 Hz, not 2079. **The two big wins are NOT applied and must not be until they have been on the air.** Both `fsk9600` and `fsk4800` sit at deviation = baud/4, which is minimum shift keying by design, and raising them moves the modulation index from 0.5 to 1.04 - a change to the signal's geometry rather than its level. Our receiver is a discriminator with an envelope-midpoint slicer and gains nothing from MSK orthogonality, which is exactly why we measure a gain; a NinoTNC's receiver is probably the same, but probably is not ground truth and the live network is. Nino's own position (#ninotnc-help, 11 July 2026) is permission to test rather than permission to ship: "more deviation will be more effective until you exceed the capability of the transmitter or receiver (IF filter)". **Four findings worth more than the numbers.** (1) **Carson's rule must not be used to pick deviation**: turnovers land at 1.0 to 1.9 times the modelled IF, never consistently at 1.0, and fsk9600's naive Carson limit of about 2800 Hz would have left the entire 4.1 dB unclaimed. (2) **AFSK 1200 fails by discriminator clicks, not truncation**: past its optimum the post-detection SNR keeps IMPROVING, 6.2 dB at 3000 Hz against 3.8 dB at 1750, while frame success falls, because error kurtosis rises from 9 to 19 and IL2P's Reed-Solomon is worst against runs - anyone reading only an SNR column picks the wrong deviation for this mode. (3) **The model has no adjacent-channel victim anywhere in it**, so it can only ever reward deviation until the wanted receiver truncates, and every out-of-class optimum found here (c4fsk9600 at 4500, c4fsk19200 at 9000, qpsk3600 at 3500) is uncosted for everybody else on the band and is not an available setting. (4) **On the two modes that have both levers, crest factor is the bigger one**: fsk9600 carries 3.2 dB of transmit pulse-filter overshoot, a defect rather than a property, on top of its 4.1 dB of deviation headroom and at constant Carson bandwidth - while AFSK 1200 on a data port measures 3.38 dB against a sine's 3.01 dB floor, so the OFDM crest precedent does not transfer to it at all. **An operational hazard for anyone calibrating**: a NinoTNC sets deviation with a Bessel-null sine at 3.01 dB crest, so if the tone and the data are emitted at equal RMS rather than equal peak, the data over-deviates by the difference, 4.1 dB on qpsk3600 and 2.9 dB on fsk9600. **Corrections to this ledger's own bookkeeping**: there are eight FM mask rows across five modes, not five rows, and `fsk4800-il2p` has no FM mask row at all, which is why its sweep had no external pin to check its seam against. **Still not proven: none of this is on-air evidence**, the model has no adjacent-channel term, and the two largest gains are explicitly held pending a NinoTNC A/B.

- **2026-08-11 - OFDM-FM negotiates its own rate, and the measurement campaign behind it says eleven of the 24 rates a header can name should never be sent.** In `packet-net/pdn-ofdm-fm`; here because the method and the negative results are the transferable part, and the "no matrix row for OFDM-FM" rule from 2026-08-09 stands. **What the wire gained.** The header already named the constellation and a receiver already used it, so one end could follow another from BPSK to QAM-256; the *coding* was not named, so the receiver decoded with whatever its own configuration said and both ends had to be set alike in advance, a mismatch looking exactly like a bad path. The coding index cost nothing: the length field was 20 bits and validated at 65535, so four of them could never be set. A recommendation for what a station would like to **hear back** cost 8 more, and the header is still one symbol on every real profile, so the whole of negotiation costs no air time at all. The change that mattered was not the bits but the burst sizing: a streaming receiver works out how long a burst is from its header before it has the burst, and sized from its own configuration rather than the signalled one it stops collecting part way through and hunts for sync inside the remainder. **The ladder, measured not derived** (`RateLadderGridProbe`, R1/T13 on a TM8100 narrow at 2500 Hz, 256-byte payload, K=9, coarse at 16 seeds to bracket each cliff then 64 on 1 dB steps): of the 24 constellation-by-coding combinations, **thirteen survive and eleven are dominated** - for each of those there is another rate at least as fast that also needs less signal, so a scheme able to select one eventually will and will then be slower AND less robust at once. Two results nobody would have guessed. **Rate 2/3 owns the top of the ladder**: every rung above QAM-16 3/4 is a 2/3 code, so the obvious design of one code rate with the constellation varying misses most of the range. **QAM-128 is dominated at all three code rates**, because a burst is a whole number of symbols and seven bits per carrier usually rounds up to the same symbol count as eight - it pays QAM-256's noise penalty for QAM-64's air time. Payload size is not a detail either: at 64 bytes the same rounding merges whole groups of rungs and the ordering does not hold. The surviving ladder spans 17.1 dB for 5.2x the throughput. **The margin measure**: re-encode what the Viterbi decoder decided, compare against the demodulator's own hard decisions, and the difference is how much of the code's correcting power that burst spent. It rises smoothly as signal falls, where "the CRC passed" is one bit that arrives too late, and **the value at which a rate gives out is a property of the code rate and not the constellation** - about 0.09 at rate 1/2, 0.05 at 2/3, 0.03 at 3/4 - so a station needs three numbers rather than thirteen. It also has a measurement floor that shaped the policy: an error rate is a count over a few thousand coded bits, good to about 8 % at the cliff and to about 45 % at 3 dB of margin, so it is sharp exactly where a station is in trouble and vague exactly where it has room. **The policy, and the two wrong diagnoses on the way to it.** Closed against an oracle - every fixed rate over the same link and seeds, best taken afterwards, air time counted for failed bursts too - the first version reached 72 to 91 %.
