I blamed the step-up rule for wanting a *run* of consecutive clean bursts (real, worth about 3 points) and then blamed using one slope for how fast the error rate falls with signal (also real - 0.36 per dB on BPSK against 0.81 on QAM-256, so one fitted 0.7 reads anything from 2.4 to 11.3 dB where the truth is 4.0 - and also worth about 3 points). Neither explained the gap. **Counting the retreats instead of theorising about them found it in ten minutes**: at +16 dB, zero retreats on failures and fourteen on bursts that had decoded perfectly. Stepping down whenever one successful burst looked expensive was the whole fault. A burst that decoded is proof the rate works, whatever it spent getting there. What replaced it is the durable part: **choose the rung with the best expected THROUGHPUT, not the best margin** - translate the current rung's margin to every other rung through the difference in their cliffs and each rate's own slope, turn the implied spending into an expected frame success, multiply by goodput. A rate losing one frame in seven at 4107 bit/s is well ahead of one losing none at 3227, and deciding on margin alone always picks the safest rate. Final: **87 to 93 % of an oracle that knew the answer in advance**, settling on the right rung at three of five signal levels. **Wired into the modem**, where three more things were needed that the component did not have: a burst reported once rather than once per retry attempt (a failed payload is retried a sample on, so one wrecked burst otherwise reads as a run of failures); opening at the configured rate rather than the bottom of the ladder (the first burst of any link goes out before anything is heard back); and expiring a recommendation after two minutes, which took a `TimeProvider` and whose fake clock immediately found a real bug - the "have I heard anything" sentinel was a zero timestamp, which a real clock never returns and a test clock always starts at. **Off by default, and not for compatibility.** Two of the three situations it can be in are ones it makes worse: a beacon or one-way link never hears anything back and sits where it started, and on a shared channel several correspondents' reports average into one nonsense recommendation, because the payload here is an opaque AX.25 frame and this layer never reads an address. It is right for a point-to-point link. **Still not proven, unchanged: no OFDM-FM mode has masks in either tier, and none of this has been on the air.** Everything above is one geometry, one deviation, one tap, one payload size, a static link, in simulation.

- **2026-08-11 (later) - a Tait manual arrived that contradicts two negative findings recorded on 2026-08-09, and both hardware notes are rebuilt on it. No mode changed, no mask moved.** Tom added `MMA-00011-01`, the **3DK Hardware Developer's Kit Application Manual** (issue 1, March 2006, 156 pages) to `M0LTE/tait-tm8100-tm8200-docs`. It is the document Tait wrote for people building hardware that plugs into a TM8100, and it answers the two questions the 2026-08-09 (later6) entry above recorded as unanswerable. **That entry stands as the record of what the 20-manual set contained; it is superseded, not wrong.** What the new manual says: **input level to deviation is published for every tap in both directions** (Table 2.7, p.22), specifically **0.87 Vp-p at T13 for 3 kHz deviation** and **0.60 Vp-p at R1 for the same into 600 ohm**, with full scale 2.0 Vp-p either way; and **T13 is a fully documented tap-in point** (Figure 3.14, p.93) with its own level, group delay and frequency response, so the earlier "no occurrence of T13 in 1083 pages" was a property of the set, not of the radio. The service manual's CCTM tap list is a test command's subset in an older numbering. **Three findings that change what a station should do**, none of them measurable here and all of them cited in `docs/dev/hardware/`: Tait's own external-modem configuration specifies **R1 tap out, type D-Split, unmute Except on PTT** and **T13 tap in, type A-Bypass In** with **EPTT1 as Data at highest priority** (p.112), which is the tap pair both notes had already argued for from the block diagram; **PTT to full carrier power with valid modulation is 14.8 +/- 0.5 ms** via T13 (Table 5.2, p.113), which is the floor for TXDELAY and is eight times the 1.8 ms group delay a reader would otherwise size on; and the **T13 level carries a +/-10% spread**, so a divider computed from the typical figure can over-deviate by 9% on a radio at the sensitive end, which is the argument for keeping the Bessel-null check per radio rather than per model. **Two errors found in our own notes while cross-checking, both fixed.** The transmit divider in the CM108 note had its shunt leg on the radio side of the DC block, which would have pulled the tap's 1.5 V bias to about 15 mV through its 100 kohm bias network and clipped half of every waveform; and the internal-board note specified Rt as 2k9 in prose and 3k3 in its schematic, now 3k0 throughout, the nearest E24 value that lands under the deviation ceiling rather than over it. **Unchanged: this is documentation, not measurement.** No mask row was touched, no mode was re-run, and nothing here has been on the air.

- **2026-08-13 - the CM108 interface note splits: build instructions for the board actually deployed, with the reasoning kept as extended notes.** Tom's review of the note: he wanted simple instructions for wiring one specific board to one specific radio, and the note, however correct, was a treatise. The board is [tomwardill/cm108radiowidget](https://github.com/tomwardill/cm108radiowidget), and reading its KiCad netlist changed real values, which is the substantive point beyond presentation. It has **no microphone bias network** (VBIAS unconnected, MICIN reached through 1 uF), so the stock-dongle 1k || 2k2 shunt arithmetic does not transfer and the receive divider computes against Rp alone, about 3 dB different. Its **PTT is already an open-drain BSS138 on GPIO3**, so it wires straight to AUX_GPI1's internal pull-up, and the generic add-a-MOSFET advice applied to this board would have inverted the logic. And it carries a **Schottky-into-volume-down SQUELCH input**, which maps onto Busy Status programmed active Low, with a hazard worth recording: the signal arrives as a HID consumer key, so a host that maps those to a mixer will wind the calibrated capture gain down whenever the channel is busy. `tm8100-cm108-interface.md` is now the five-wire build note with computed values (Rb 1k with Rt from a measured-full-scale table and C4 on the radio side of the divider for transmit; Rs 6k8 with Rp 1k receive at nominal full scale; all passives in the D-sub backshell); `tm8100-cm108-interface-notes.md` keeps the full 3DK treatment from the entry above plus the widget's deltas. This leg and the rebuild above found the same 3DK figures and the same bias trap independently, which is a cheap cross-check of both. **Unchanged: documentation only, no mask moved, nothing on air.**

- **2026-08-15 - `freedv-datac1` and `freedv-datac3` cross a real ionospheric path, out of the production node's own transmit chain. Neither mode's status changes; what changes is the class of evidence behind it.** The 2026-07-28 campaign that turned all six datac modes green transmitted through the `sm-ota` harness into an RSP1 on the same bench, across a 125 dB attenuator chain (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-07-28-freedv-ofdm-oncar/`). Everything between "our modulator" and "our demodulator" was ours and was in the room. This run replaced all of that: the **production GB7RDG daemon** on 40 m at 7053.600 MHz, ANT1, 50 W, sharing one Flex slice with three modems carrying live traffic, received by a **second pdn-soundmodem instance on somebody else's receiver** (`ubersdr:wessex.zapto.org`, terminated delta loop, SW England) with an ordinary band-planned config and nothing but the device string different. Frames went in over KISS (`scripts/kiss-send.py`) and came out as AX.25 at the far end. **`freedv-datac3`: 6 of 6, 100 %. `freedv-datac1`: 11 of 14, 79 %.** Every decode `crc ok`, `fec 0` - not one Reed-Solomon correction spent across 17 frames - at a measured **+2 Hz** offset, which is what a GPSDO-locked Flex and a disciplined receiver are worth. The gap between the two modes is the expected ~5 dB: datac1 needs +4.6 dB on a fading channel against datac3's -0.7 dB (the rows above), and this path did not have 5 dB to spare. **The path measurement is the other half of the result.** A first attempt into `m9psy-1` (Dalgety Bay, ~550 km) decoded nothing at all, and the survey was *empty* - heard nothing, rather than heard-and-failed. An IQ capture taken through the transmissions settled it rather than leaving it to guesswork: `sm-iqcapture` at 7053.600 plus a time-resolved in-band-minus-reference analysis showed our bursts arriving at the right instants at only **+6 to +10 dB** of in-band excess, about +5 dB SNR3k - right on datac1's fading threshold, which is exactly consistent with no copy. So the mode was never the problem; a 550 km hop on 40 m at 09:10 UTC in August was. Moving to a receiver ~120 km from the transmitter turned 0 % into 100 % on the same waveform, same power, same config. **New instrument:** `scripts/iq-occupancy.py`, the pre-transmission clear-channel check (Welch over an sm-iqcapture WAV, mean and peak per bin against a median floor); it validated itself on the first run by finding GB7RDG's own bpsk300 at +19 dB exactly where it sits, and found a narrow persistent carrier at 7055.15-7055.25 that moved the test slot from 7054.000 down to 7053.600 for guard. Also proven on air, and not previously proven anywhere but a mock: the **per-modem Morse identification** shipped in v0.35.0 (PR #303) - `id[3] M0LTE in CW` at 09:18:50 and again at 09:28:56, an interval of **10 min 06 s** against a configured 10 minutes, with the 6 s being the 5 s poll granularity plus channel access. Campaign plan, placement reasoning and the band plan constraint (7053-7060 is *not* the segment designated for unattended data stations, so this ran attended and came down afterwards) in [freedv-ota-plan.md](archive/freedv-ota-plan.md). The node was restored to its exact pre-campaign config and filters and verified back in service working EI0RSI-1.

- **2026-08-15 (later) - the `freedv-datac1` misses are the channel, not the receiver, and the measurement that says so also found a bug that takes a transmitting node off the air.** Tom asked what happened to the 3 frames of 14 that did not decode in the morning's run. Answer, from 30 more frames at 25 s pacing with an IQ capture running through the whole run and each burst's received level correlated against the receiver's own verdict: **the misses are the weak bursts, with no overlap at all.** Decoded bursts (n=10) sat at **+17.2 dB mean in-band excess** (range +15.1 to +20.0); missed bursts (n=13) at **+5.7 dB** (+3.5 to +13.7) - a **separation of 11.5 dB and not one inversion**. Everything at or above +15.1 dB decoded; everything at or below +13.7 dB did not. That is a threshold device behaving exactly as one should, and it means there is **no "strong but undecoded" population** - nothing here indicts the demodulator. Two further details fall out of the same data. The path changed state sharply at **10:54:25**: before it, bursts arrived weak *and short* (0.3-1.8 s of a 4.4 s transmission poking above the detector), which is within-burst fading rather than a shortened transmission; after it, full 4.4 s bursts at +13 to +20 dB. And bursts 20 and 21 (mean +13.4 and +13.7, full length) are the knee itself - the only two full-strength misses in the set. **Do not read the absolute figure as an SNR3k**: it is a power-spectral-density ratio of the occupied band against a 400 Hz reference just above it, so it is a clean *relative* instrument and an uncalibrated absolute one. The relative result is what the question needed. **The bug:** pacing frames every 8 s to gather this data took the production GB7RDG daemon off the air at 10:28:50 with `receive feed dead: 30 s of unbroken digital silence from the radio`. A keyed Flex is not receiving and its DAX stream delivers exact zeros, which is byte-for-byte identical to the dead feed `DeadFeedWatch` exists to catch, and nothing told the two apart - so a station that transmits enough restarts itself. Pre-existing since the watch landed on 2026-08-07, and invisible until now because nothing had ever driven this station's transmit duty cycle that hard. Fixed by giving `Observe` the station's keyed state and re-arming rather than accumulating (PR #305); 25 s pacing produced 30 transmissions and zero dead-feed events afterwards. The trade-off is recorded in the class: a station transmitting more often than the threshold can no longer detect a dead receive feed, which is the right way round, and the receive-only capture station the watch was built for is unaffected.

- **2026-08-15 (later2) - chase decoding lands after all, and the 2026-08-06 null it reverses is explained: the null was the confidence metric, not the idea. bpsk300 gains ~0.5 dB at the AWGN knee, ~1 dB on short frames, and reads 18 of the week's real missed bursts.** rx-roadmap workstream 1's named remainder, CRC-arbitrated chase decoding, had already been built once (M0LTE.Il2p branch `chase-decoding`, archived 2026-08-06 as a measured system-level null with the condition "re-open only with a demonstrably better per-bit soft metric"). That condition is now met. The mechanism: a differentially-decoded bit compares symbol k against symbol k-1, so one hit symbol flips TWO consecutive bits - and the demodulator's per-bit confidence measured only the current symbol's projection magnitude, leaving the second bit of every such pair looking confident. A chase hunting the weakest bits could never assemble the true flip set: instrumented at the AWGN -4 dB knee, **0 chase hits in 50 header attempts** with the old metric, **40 of 70** once `BpskDemodulator` emits the weaker of the two symbols' magnitudes per bit - and the accepted flip sets come back as adjacent pairs, the mechanism's own signature. Each factor alone is a measured null (pair-min without chase moved nothing; chase without pair-min was the 2026-08-06 null, reproduced exactly); together they compose. M0LTE.Il2p 0.3.0 (PR M0LTE/M0LTE.Il2p#4) carries the chase: per-bit confidence through the deframer, header rescue as any combination of the 12 weakest bits accepted only as an exact codeword (the 2-parity header affords nothing speculative - one random word in 17 sits within a byte of a codeword), payload rescue as up to 3 flips with at most 7 located errors on top, every attempt keeping the erasure ladder's own two parity symbols in reserve; `Il2pDecodeInfo.ChasedBits` and a `chased_bits` frame-log column keep the honest account, the `erased_bytes` precedent. Full ladder A/B (N=300, seed 1, `sm-ota sim`, this PR #307): AWGN -5 dB **55 -> 67 %**, -4 dB **88 -> 93 %**, -3/-2 within a point; the CFO span at -3 dB **92-97 -> 98-99 %** (chase rescues the header bits a residual rotation grazes); Good -4/0/+4 39/62/79 -> 40/64/80; Moderate 40/52/60 -> 41/53/60; Poor 24/28/32 -> 24/30/32 (the outage ceiling, exactly as workstream 5 said). Short 15-byte frames - the live slot's modal supervisory traffic, where the header is most of the frame - move hardest: AWGN -5 dB **62 -> 76 %**, -4 dB 88 -> 93 %. Wild evidence, not just the model: against the new **misses-v2 corpus** (573 full-length undecoded bursts cut from the 40 m capture's whole 166.9 h raw archive, built this evening - see the campaign section of rx-roadmap.md) the isolated-decode score moves **14 -> 32 bursts, zero lost**, 11 of the 18 new reads CRC-verified end to end and every one of them a known station of the week's traffic (GB7WEM-7, EI0RSI-1/-7, GB7BPQ>SA6DAZ-3, GB7BEX-15, PD4R-12); 17 of 18 leaned on chase, and the flip counts are again even pairs. The GB7RDG 37-frame corpus is unmoved at 32/37 demodulated - measured before and after, its residue is genuinely not RS-limited, as the roadmap has said since 2026-08-06. Full-tier mask rows raised with this entry per the measured values: AWGN -5 35 -> 47, -4 68 -> 73, the four CFO rows 75 -> 80; smoke floors unchanged (still 2-3 sigma under the new measurements). Nothing on the wire changed: every candidate the chase accepts decodes to the same bytes a clean receive would have produced, and NinoTNC interop stays pinned by the parity suites.
- **2026-08-15 (later3) - every decoded frame now records the strength of the burst it arrived on: `snr_db` in the frame log, `snr x.x dB` on the journal line, `snrDb` in the KISS quality frame, one figure across all of them.** No mode changes status - filed on the acceptPlainIl2p precedent: this changes what a station writes down, not what it decodes. rx-roadmap workstream 9, the cheapest item on the list and the one that makes every other workstream measurable in the wild: the daemon has computed a per-burst SNR since the waterfall's `BandActivityTracker` landed and threw it away unless a browser was watching - the 2026-08-15 FreeDV investigation had to stand up a parallel IQ capture to recover a number the daemon had already measured. The measurement now lives in the channel (`BurstSnrMonitor`), bands probed by the same `ModemBandProbe` as the waterfall overlay and RF planner, fed only non-transmit audio, and enriches `FrameQuality.SnrDb` at the single point every modem's quality passes through - so every consumer records the identical figure and the waterfall panel prefers it over its own tracker for the same reason. **The convention is stated everywhere the number appears** (the roadmap's warning, honoured): mean in-band power over the burst against a rolling minimum noise floor, in dB - NOT the 3 kHz-referenced SNR of the sim ladder and the Watterson masks; comparing the two without converting is wrong. A quiet band yields null, never zero; a frame log from an earlier daemon gains the column by additive migration with old rows honestly null. Pinned by `BurstSnrTests` (a strong burst carries a figure; a cold-start burst that cannot be attributed carries none), `FrameLogTests`, `ActivityLogTests`, and the KISS quality suite. PR #308.
- **2026-08-16 - the chase landing's continuous-replay verdict: +78 frames over the whole 166.9 h archive, 41 CRC-verified, zero content lost - and it outreads the MLSE detector swap with the deployed default.** Overnight addendum to the 2026-08-15 chase entry, run on the merged main (M0LTE.Il2p 0.3.0 from nuget): the full raw archive replayed continuously - not the isolated per-burst rig - reads **4394 decoded / 3914 deliverable** against the pre-chase baseline's 4318 / 3872 (+76 / +42, +1.8 % / +1.1 %), 4442 DCD bursts of which 3481 now carry a decode (was 3456), and 102 of the week's decodes leaned on chase (flip histogram again pair-dominated: 40x4, 23x2, 21x6). The diff's two apparent baseline-only frames dissolve under autopsy: both are copies inside retry chains whose payload the chase arm also read seconds away, so no content the old receiver heard is missing from the new one. For workstream 2's ledger: a same-window `--detector mlse` pass (old receiver generation) reads 4343 / 3889 - the chase landing gains more on the deployed default detector than the MLSE swap offers - and the mlse-vs-chase exchange stays roughly the known shape (75 frames only mlse caught vs 127 only chase-differential caught), so the ensemble opt-in remains sized at about +1.7 % union, an upper bound until an mlse arm carries pair-min + chase too. CSVs beside the others in `/home/tf/capture-40m/replays/`.

- **2026-08-16 (later) - a survey capture now really carries its trailing margin: the WAV waits for the audio instead of shipping at verdict time.** No mode changes status - a record-quality fix, found the first time somebody wanted to listen to the misses. Tom asked for the day's 18 full-length missed bursts with a second of context either side for by-ear characterisation; the files had their full 1 s lead-in and ~0.2 s of tail, because `SignalSurvey` wrote the capture the moment the detector closed the burst - one 0.2 s grace period after the last hot line - and clamped the trailing margin to the audio that existed. The reference's `marginSeconds` ("audio kept either side of the burst") was the promise; the code kept half of it. A triaged capture now waits in a pending queue until the ring holds the whole trailing margin and is written then; a keyup or shutdown flushes it early and honestly short, because splicing post-transmission audio onto a pre-transmission burst would manufacture context that never happened. Pinned both ways in `SignalSurveyTests` (a 2 s burst's WAV measures 4.0 s with a 1 s margin; a keyup flush stays under it). PR #310.
- **2026-08-16 (later2) - Tom characterised the day's misses by ear, and the instruments turned his "unknown modem at ~2150 Hz" into three findings: it is our own network's bpsk300, most of the "misses" were fade-split fragments of transmissions the station DECODED, and the survey now lets a decode claim its fragments.** Tom listened to the 19 full-length missed-burst WAVs of the v0.37.0 rollout morning and reported an unrecognised modem around 2150 Hz on most of them (plus VARA near the ARDOP slot, static, and fragments of HF misc). Spectral fingerprinting answered him: every strong burst carries a razor 300.0 Hz envelope baud line and a 31-41 dB carrier line at exactly twice its centre under squaring (none under 4th power) - 300 Bd binary PSK, our own mode, RRC-shaped, in our own slot. The 2fc line measures each burst's true carrier to a fraction of a Hz, and the offsets cluster into exactly three station fingerprints the frame log already knows: -18 Hz (GB7BPQ and PD4R-12), -8.4 Hz (GB7BEX-15), +1.6 Hz (EI0RSI-7). Then the timing correlation: **9 of the 11 strong-BPSK "misses" have a same-fingerprint decode landing 0.6-3.2 s after the burst closed.** Frame timestamps are decode-completion times and a 118-byte beacon is ~4.3 s of wire, so these bursts are leading fragments of transmissions the station went on to read: a fade outlasting the burst detector's 0.2 s grace splits the transmission, and Triage's attribution window (one second past the burst) closes before the decode exists. The fix, built on v0.37.1's pending-capture queue: a Missed capture waits `decodeClaimSeconds` (default 5 s) and is re-checked against the decodes at release - a decode inside the window claims it and it is never written, counted in `ClaimedByLaterDecode`; a real miss releases unchanged when the window passes. Pinned both ways in `SignalSurveyTests`. Two honest riders: the same fragment class colours **misses-v2** (a share of that corpus's 573 "full-length undecoded bursts" are fragments of decoded transmissions, which the retry-sibling attachment partially marks and the isolated re-decode score already reflects - fragments do not decode alone); and of the day's 11 in-slot bursts only 2 (a GB7BPQ-fingerprint at 08:43 and an EI0RSI-fingerprint at 09:34) were genuine unclaimed misses. Tom's "sounds overdriven" stays open: what a fragment of a QSB-mangled BPSK burst sounds like and what an overdriven transmitter sounds like are not separable by ear on this evidence, and the drive question belongs to the stations, not this receiver. PR #311.
- **2026-08-20 - MS110D: the Poor-gate successor program is registered, the measured-only counts become a bank the suite asserts, and the record is reconciled with the evidence.** No mode changes status. Three weeks after the WN8 program closed, the documents still told three different stories: `docs/dev/ms110d/README.md` and `roadmap.md` ended at Phase B or called the WN8 program live; `phase-b-closeout.md` §4/§5 still said WN8's two walls stood (both fell on 2026-07-31, now recorded there as dated amendments, the Phase B text left as written); the OTA planning documents still called §E2 and §E4 unrun and the RSP1's AGC undefeatable, when the 2026-07-27 full campaign ran eighteen live ladders through the pad-chain rig with the AGC off and §E4 ran from a real antenna on 2026-08-03. The rows above for WN2/WN6/WN13 drop their retired caveats (#102 closed 2026-07-28; the confirmation evidence has been committed since the same day). The substantive gap was in the suite: the W6 closeout's obligation that `MS110D_POOR_GATED`'s expected-red values re-bank to the measured figures had been discharged in prose only - no test referenced 2.90E-4 / 1.75E-2 or 2.56E-5 / 1.48E-5, so a regression from either was invisible. `Ms110dMaskTests.MeasuredOnlyBank` now pins WN7's and WN8's closing counts (both seed families, exact, in the battery configuration) as assertions; `MS110D_POOR_GATED=1` keeps its meaning and is how a chase leg gets past the bank. The program of record is `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/poor-gate-successor-plan.md` (issue #312): G0 closes the books and re-baselines (registration and battery under `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-20-poorgate-g0/`); G1 puts the MFB-form receiver on WN7, which nobody has tried and which the W1 truth lane licenses (WN7's oracle-residual corpse decodes to zero on all 11 blocks under per-symbol truth); G2 takes WN8's residual, now localised to three first-block non-convergences; G3 covers the production path through `FrequencyShiftedModem`; H1/H2 are Tom's hardware legs, H2 being the pad-chain ceiling measurement that is the only route by which the +19/+23 dB points ever run through a real transmitter. Both Poor points stay sim-only by physics until H2 says otherwise.
- **2026-08-20 (later) - G0 PASS: the MS110D re-baseline reproduces every digit, and the measured-only bank holds.** No mode changes status. The successor program's entry battery (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-20-poorgate-g0/`) on current main: all five Phase B closeout §6 guard pins exact; every Poor error count identical to the W0/W6 baselines on both seed families (WN7 83/3,243,776 and 48/3,243,776, WN8 1,254/4,325,120 and 75,713/4,325,120, now asserted in code by `Ms110dMaskTests.MeasuredOnlyBank` and passing); AWGN 10/10, static WID2 and the three Doppler checks zero errors; 120 of 120 census CSVs byte-identical. Run on the Release test exe directly rather than `dotnet test` on Debug: 40 minutes wall for the 33 legs where the W0 and W6 batteries took 3.5 hours, with identical digits, so that is the battery form from here on. G1 (WN7 under the MFB-form receiver) is next.
- **2026-08-20 (later2) - MS110D WN7 Poor: the MFB-form receiver alone does not beat the DFE-chain receiver, but the two fail on disjoint blocks and a label-free selector picks right every time.** No mode changes status yet. Leg G1 of `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/poor-gate-successor-plan.md` (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-20-poorgate-g1/`): the W5 MFB-form prototype generalised to 8PSK (calibration: byte-identical on the WN8 specimens, so the QAM16 numbers are untouched) leaves 238 errors pooled over the seven bursts that carry all 131 of WN7's battery errors, zeroing three of them and losing on the rest; the pre-committed rule kills it as a standalone receiver and the Phase B B3.9 verdict stands under the MFB class. The measured lever is the disjointness: on all 88 blocks of the eight specimens the two receivers are never wrong together, and the MFB reconstruction residual - re-encode each candidate decode, reconstruct through the estimated trajectory, keep the lower residual - selects a zero-error decode on every block (18 contested; the wrong decode prices 0.17 to 22 % above the right one, about 4 sigma at the thinnest). Per-block ensemble ceiling on these specimens: 0. G1d (the shipped build and its battery) decides whether the WN7 row moves; nothing ships from this entry.
- **2026-08-20 (later3) - MS110D WN7 (8PSK r3/4) Poor: measured-only -> sim hard-gated. The 8PSK per-block ensemble takes the point from 83/48 to 0/0.** The broken-to-working transition for `ms110d-wn7` on the D.6.1 Poor channel at +19 dB. Leg G1d of `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/poor-gate-successor-plan.md` (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-20-poorgate-g1d/`, issue #312): the shipped `Ms110dMfbBlockDecoder` speaks 8PSK as well as QAM16 and prices any block decode by its reconstruction residual; on every 8PSK block the demodulator runs it beside the DFE-chain path and keeps the decode with the larger evidence - the MFB's Gaussian log-likelihood gain (rows x ln of the residual ratio) against the chain's summed |LLR| at the disagreeing wire bits. No constant is tuned. Two things were found on the way and are part of the record: the residual alone is blind to an MFB maximum-likelihood error (the first battery took AWGN WN7 from 0 to 8 on one nearest-neighbour codeword block; the chain's |LLR| of 4.5 on those five bits against 1-3 where the chain is wrong is what separates them, and the two-sided rule separates all 19 contested blocks with an 8x margin at the thinnest), and the MFB's block-0 probe geometry was wrong for one-frame interleavers (the preamble's closing probe is unshifted), caught by the hermetic WN7 UltraShort loopback and fixed for QAM16 too. Battery: **Poor WN7 0 / 3,243,776 both families** (97.5 % bound 1.13E-6), AWGN WN7 0, every one of the other 114 censuses byte-identical to G0 (WN8 1,254 / 75,713 untouched), guard pins exact (WN7 w1/b0 now 0 coded, selected 2). `Poor_Channel_Mask_Gate` arms WN7 by default; `MeasuredOnlyBank` drops its WN7 rows. Sim-only by rig physics: the +19 dB mask region is above both rigs' ceilings, so the on-air column does not forward and the row stays partial (successor plan §6; H2 is the route). Latent hole in the shipped MFB filed as #316. The 9 of 10 Poor points are now hard-gated; WN8 remains the one measured-only point.
- **2026-08-20 (later4) - the Watterson rig learns to fade about a moved centre; every MS110D waveform passes the shift gate at 3750 and 5000 Hz on AWGN and WN6 on Poor.** No mode changes status. Leg G3 of `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/poor-gate-successor-plan.md` (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-20-poorgate-g3/`). The first Poor arm of the new shifted-modem smoke read native 10/12 against 4/12 at 3750 Hz and 0/12 at 5000 Hz, the same at 14, 20 and 26 dB, with AWGN at parity everywhere - the signature of a band-limited instrument, not a receiver. `WattersonChannel` forms its complex envelope about the 1800 Hz sub-carrier with a 2.2 kHz low-pass; it had never been asked to fade a signal centred anywhere else. It now takes `CentreHz` and scales its low-pass taps with the sample rate (129 at 9600 Hz exactly as before, so the five guard pins and four corpses reproduce their banked digits). Corrected: Poor at 14/20/26 dB, native 10/11/11 of 12 against 10/11/11 at 3750 and 10/11/11 at 5000. The shift ladder now runs all ten `ms110d-wn*` (50 frames per cell, native / 3750 / 5000, rungs re-centring on each knee) and every waveform passes per rung and in aggregate; the smoke joins the hermetic suite; the daemon prints WN7's and WN8's Poor standing at start-up and on SETHW. The #221 gate's AWGN-only verdict stands; what it could not see, it now can.
- **2026-08-20 (later5) - MS110D WN8 (16QAM r3/4) Poor: 2.90E-4 / 1.75E-2 -> 7.17E-6 canonical / 1.78E-5 disjoint, the canonical family at the mask; still measured-only.** Leg G2 of `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/poor-gate-successor-plan.md` (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-20-poorgate-g2/`). The whole disjoint 1.75E-2 was three blocks, mid-burst not first, each a coin-flip the shipped schedule never descended from, and the matched-filter bound with nothing but label-free probe anchors decoded all three to zero, so the model was never the deficiency: the cold rung was. `Ms110dMfbBlockDecoder` now starts every QAM16 block with a per-symbol linear MMSE estimate on its anchored trajectory (known probes subtracted, neighbouring data chips as Es-power unknowns, the anchor-fit residual as the noise estimate) instead of an ISI-inclusive matched projection; every block begins at 4-13k errors instead of 24k and converges in four to seven rungs where it took ten to forty or never. Battery: **Poor WN8 31 / 4,325,120 canonical (7.17E-6, direct BER under the mask) and 77 / 4,325,120 disjoint (1.78E-5, 1.8x over on one block at 62 and one burst at 15)**, AWGN WN8 0, all 113 other censuses byte-identical (WN7's ensemble untouched by the QAM16 scope). `MeasuredOnlyBank` re-banks to 31 / 77. The row stays partial and measured-only: one family clears, one does not, and both are sim-only by rig physics. Successor levers recorded: the stability-driven soft handover (measured to convert exactly these stragglers before the cold rung existed), schedule diversity priced by residual, and the cold rung on 8PSK (unmeasured, its own registration).
- **2026-08-20 (later6) - MS110D WN8 (16QAM r3/4) Poor: measured-only -> sim hard-gated at 12 / 18 per 4,325,120 bits. All ten MS110D Poor points are now hard-gated.** The broken-to-working transition for `ms110d-wn8` on the D.6.1 Poor channel at +23 dB, completing the program. Leg G2's second half (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-20-poorgate-g2/`, G2e/G2f): with the MMSE cold rung in, four blocks still settled on nearly-right decodes (62 / 15 / 12 / 19 errors), every one of them a soft phase that ran to its cap without settling and then a period-2 oscillation accepted as converged at the first hard rung - the #316 hole, measured. The QAM16 schedule now has a soft-phase floor, accepts a cycle only between hard-phase decodes, runs the hard-first schedule after any cycle-accept (an exact fixed point wins; two cycles are priced through the same probe-anchor model, because residuals through different re-fit models are not comparable - banked), and hands the soft phase over when its residual stops falling rather than at the cap. Battery: **Poor WN8 12 / 4,325,120 canonical (bound 4.85E-6) and 18 / 4,325,120 disjoint (bound 6.58E-6)**, from 31 / 77 this afternoon and 1,254 / 75,713 at the WN8 program's close; AWGN 0; every other census byte-identical, WN7's ensemble untouched (8PSK keeps the W5b2 schedule; #316 stays open for it). `Poor_Channel_Mask_Gate` arms WN8; `MeasuredOnlyBank` is empty. Sim-only by rig physics: the row forwards to "Poor sim hard-gated" and stays partial until the pad-chain ceiling (H2) lets +23 dB through a real transmitter.
- **2026-08-21 - MS110D 8PSK: the MFB decoder's MMSE cold rung and reworked schedule reach the WN7 ensemble; nothing the ensemble emits changes.** No mode changes status. Leg G4 of `docs/dev/archive/ms110d/poor-gate-successor-plan.md` (`docs/dev/archive/ms110d/evidence/2026-08-21-poorgate-g4/`): the QAM16-only scope of G2d/G2f lifted, so WN7's second receiver starts each block with the per-symbol MMSE estimate and stops on the same rules as WN8's. Battery: Poor WN7 0 / 3,243,776 both families, AWGN 0, WN8 12 / 18, all 120 censuses byte-identical to their baselines; the 8PSK MFB terminates every block in 11 rungs where it took 10-40 with a soft oscillation accepted on one; wall-clock flat. #316 (a cycle accepted between soft-phase decodes) is closed on both branches.
- **2026-08-21 (later) - `bpsk1200` read off a real FM receiver, and the FM/SSB split in this document turns out not to be a split the receiver cares about.** No mode changes status: `bpsk1200` was already working (on-air AWGN validated 2026-07-28 over the Flex->RSP1 **SSB** rig). What is new is the carrier. Tom recorded five noisy captures off an **FM** radio on 2026-08-21 with the mode not written down, as the first real material for the new `pdn-decode` sweep tool, and all five turned out to be one `bpsk1200` IL2P+CRC QSO (N2IRZ-2 to WA2M-2, a BPQChatServer-6.0.21.40 session). The sweep recovers **7 distinct frames from 3 of the 5 files** on the shipping differential bank with no tuning - CRC-clean, 0-8 bytes FEC-corrected, one frame rescued by 6 chased bits, measured offsets -4 to +1 Hz. The corpus and its analysis are committed at [`samples/offair/2026-08-21/`](../../samples/offair/2026-08-21/README.md). **The finding worth carrying forward** is the one that cost the tool its first default: `FmModeProfiles` and `docs/dev/mode-modulation-reference.md` classify a mode by how it reaches the air, which is a fact about modulators, and `pdn-decode` first read that as the set of modes an FM capture can contain. It is not. Nino's own switch map groups the shaped-PSK modes "Shaped PSK - SSB radios, **or FM radios**" and puts `qpsk3600` with the speaker-and-mic modes; a shaped-PSK signal through an FM radio is an ordinary thing on the air, and the FM-native sweep read none of this corpus. Nothing in the modem was wrong - the same `bpsk1200` bank that reads the GB7RDG SSB capture reads this FM one - but any future tool or document that treats the FM column as "what an FM radio can carry" will be wrong the same way. The four hard files are hard for an ordinary reason and not a mode limit: they are open-squelch recordings whose noise floor never drops (in-band and above-4 kHz energy within 1 dB, nothing clipped, -7 dBFS peak), against the one squelched file whose bursts sit 63 dB over its floor and which yields five of the seven frames.
- **2026-08-21 (later2) - `qpsk600`: a real off-air burst at +1.7 dB that the shipping receiver cannot copy, and the measured reason is the QPSK family never got PR #236's chain or a diversity bank.** No mode changes status; what changes is that the QPSK receive chain now has a named, committed, real-signal failure instead of a roadmap note. Tom said `packet-24738.wav` "definitely contains one of the slower modes"; it does. Finding it needs the right polarity - the file is loud almost throughout and goes **quiet** from 3.06 to 5.31 s, which is an open-squelch FM receiver quieting on a carrier, so the transmission is in the quiet window rather than the loud one. Measured in it: a two-tone preamble 3.03-3.63 s at **1350.6 / 1649.4 Hz** (centre **1500.0**, shift **298.8**), symbol rate **300.00 Hz** off the `abs(z)^2` cyclostationary line, and a **4th-order** constellation - the `z^4` line lands at 0.00 Hz, 20 dB above `z^2`, which has no DC line at all, and against both NinoTNC references degraded to matching noise the burst looks *more* 4th-order than the `qpsk600` reference does. 300 sym/s, 1500 Hz carrier, 4th order is **`qpsk600`** (switch 1001). **SNR is not the excuse**: +4.1 dB on the preamble and **+1.7 dB on the data**, 3 kHz-referenced, against a published `bpsk300` AWGN threshold near -4 dB; the noise is ordinary Gaussian (kurtosis 3.0, nothing beyond 4 sigma, so not FM click noise), the carrier offset is ~0, and nothing is clipped. Tried and failed: the whole 46-entry sweep; the burst isolated to 2.9-5.5 s so the surrounding full-scale hiss could not disturb AGC or deframer; a manual diversity sweep at 17 offsets from -30 to +30 Hz; and the coherent detector on every PSK mode. **Structural cause, already predicted here**: `bpsk300`/`bpsk1200` run `BpskMultiModem` (nine-branch bank) with the #236 rebuilt differential chain worth ~1.5 dB, and `afsk300` runs an eleven-branch bank, while **the entire QPSK family is a single `QpskModem` with no bank and the pre-#236 chain** - exactly the leg the #236 entry above lists as undone ("the obvious next legs are ... the QPSK family, which still runs the old-style chain"). Degrading the clean references to matched noise puts numbers on it: `qpsk600` 3/6 at +2 dB, 2/6 at 0 dB, **0/6 at -2 dB**, where `bpsk300` holds 3/6 throughout - a gap of roughly 4 dB, and this burst sits in it. Absolute calibration of that ladder is rough and the rows mean nothing except against each other. The corpus is committed at [`samples/offair/2026-08-21/`](../../samples/offair/2026-08-21/README.md) with the full measurement, so the QPSK campaign has a real-signal fixture with a known answer to aim at rather than only a simulation ladder. Registered as **issue #326**, which also notes that #11 and #116 (`qpsk600`'s measured +-5 Hz CFO wall and its 0-1/3 copy rate on real NinoTNC transmissions over a clean audio loop) are very likely the same root cause.
- **2026-08-21 (later3) - the QPSK family gets BPSK's diversity bank and the rest of PR #236's chain, plus two timing fixes of its own: qpsk600's AWGN knee moves ~0.8 dB, qpsk2400's ~1 dB, and both modes' carrier-offset tolerance goes from a wall to the bank's comb (issue #326).** No mode changes status. What moved, measured on the `sm-ota sim` rig (N=100, seed 1, 60-byte frames, single modem before -> nine-branch bank after): **qpsk600 AWGN** -2/-1/0/+1/+2 dB 1/26/69/86/99 % -> 9/45/82/94/98 % (zero TXDELAY; at 150 ms TXDELAY, N=200, -1/0 dB 23/66 % -> 58/90 %); **qpsk600 CFO at +4 dB, 150 ms TXDELAY** 0/3.75/7.5/15/30 Hz 99/93/96/75/**3** % -> 100/99/100/100/**100** %, and at zero TXDELAY 3.75/7.5/15 Hz 100/99/71 % -> 100/100/100 %; **qpsk2400 AWGN** +5/+7/+9/+11 dB 3/72/97/99 % -> **35/90**/99/100 %, **CFO at +9 dB** 3.75/7.5/15/30 Hz 98/94/95/89 % -> 100/100/100/100 % (and 39-40 of 40 at 60 and 120 Hz, the comb's edge at 1200 Bd, bpsk1200's RSP1 figure from #116); fading rows up a few points on both modes (qpsk600 Good +4/+8/+12 57/74/80 -> 63/78/88, Moderate 27/40/49 -> 38/50/56, Poor 6/6/10 -> 6/8/11; qpsk2400 Good 24/34/42 -> 37/49/55, Moderate 8/13/17 -> 7/17/17, Poor 2/2/2 -> 2/3/5) - still equaliser-bound, the known limit stands; **qpsk3600** (one-branch bank by default, FM, its detector unchanged) AWGN +8 dB 66 -> 81 %, fm-mic +8 dB CNR 62 -> 78 %, fm-data 54 -> 80 % (N=50), the 15-30 dB FM rows 100 % as before. Four things did it, each isolated on the ladder and on the real burst below. (1) **The decision-feedback reference decides from cold, as BPSK's does.** The 2026-08-07 campaign gated the handover on the fourth-power offset window turning coherent and recorded an AWGN null for the reference; on the real burst that window is coherent through the all-reversal preamble and incoherent for the entire payload (the fourth power of a noisy phasor is far noisier than BPSK's square), so the reference never decided the part that matters, and the gate even handed the payload back to the plain product mid-frame. (2) **The rotation tracker's gain drops 0.05 -> 0.01 once DCD asserts.** At 0.05 the tracker's own jitter left the reference no better than the product at the 0 dB knee (18 degrees rms either way - the mechanism of that null); at 0.01 it reaches the 14 degrees a tracker-free reference reaches, and 12 against the product's 16 on the off-air burst. The acquisition gain stays 0.05 until DCD because a low gain cannot pull a half-step residual in from cold, and the tracker now runs from the first symbol (clamped to one bank step, leaking toward zero between bursts), which is what the campaign's "60 % at 8 Hz from cold" lacked. (3) **Sub-sample crossing interpolation into the DPLL**, which BPSK's demodulator always had and QPSK passed as zero: the single biggest lever, worth ~1.5 dB on qpsk2400 at 10 samples per symbol (N=200, +6/+7 dB: 97/163 -> 156/187 at zero TXDELAY, 111/171 -> 182/193 at 150 ms). With the truth of a synthetic 0 dB burst in hand, a fixed clock at the right phase decoded it with zero wrong bytes where the DPLL left six: the knee was timing-bound, not detector-bound. Inertia 0.94 on top (0.96 loses the 16-symbol zero-TXDELAY acquisition, as #236 found at its 24-bit minimum). (4) **The product the DPLL and DCD see is de-rotated by the seed-plus-tracker estimate**, and **the seed re-arms on the window's rising edge and is released on the DCD falling edge** (with a three-second backstop decay), never on window collapse alone: releasing it mid-payload had handed an 18-degree-per-symbol burst to a 9-degree tracker on whichever bank branch's window emptied first. A lone branch now copies +-30 Hz at +4 dB where the campaign measured 3 %. **`QpskMultiModem`** is `BpskMultiModem` on the QPSK factories (best-residual branch choice, honest null offset, the coherent second detector as the ensemble knob; `ModemOptions.SecondDetector` now accepts the qpsk banks), wired into the `qpsk600`/`qpsk2400` catalogue arms at 4 pairs x baud/40 and into `qpsk3600` at 0 pairs (FM tones arrive on frequency and its chain runs threefold upsampled; `OffsetPairs` widens it on request). QPSK frames now carry per-dibit confidence into `Il2pReceiver`, so the erasure ladder and chase decoding that were BPSK-only reach the QPSK family. **Masks moved, all upward:** `WattersonMaskTests` gains qpsk600 awgn +2 (39/40, floor 34), 0 dB (33/40, floor 26), +4 dB at 15 and 30 Hz CFO (40/40, floor 36), qpsk2400 awgn +9 (39/40, floor 34), +7 (38/40, floor 32), +9 dB at 60 and 120 Hz CFO (39 and 40 of 40, floor 34); the existing +4 and +11 dB rows stand at 40/40. **The fixture does not copy, and its README now says exactly why.** A fixed-clock bench receiver swept through all 40 clock phases recovered the frame (a TARPN ID beacon, N2IRZ-2 > ID, 103 bytes; the hex is in the README): hard Reed-Solomon decodes it at one phase with exactly 8 corrected bytes, the code's limit. Against that truth the deployed chain syncs with 0 bit errors, decodes the header with 0 corrections and leaves **10 wrong bytes** in the single 103-byte payload block, six of them consecutive in the first 70 ms of payload and wrong at every clock phase - damaged in the signal, with high decision margins, so no erasure ranking tried (amplitude-scaled or phase-only, pair-min or windowed) can flag them inside the 16 the code affords. The bank's best branches leave 9. "+1.7 dB" was an average over a burst whose payload start is not there; the fixture keeps its value as a burst that can now be scored in wrong bytes against an 8-byte floor. **Found on the way:** QPSK's `PacketDcd` was already marginal on main - it asserts only with the carrier on frequency and noise present (never under a 7.5 Hz offset, never on a noise-free burst, never with the 16-symbol zero-TXDELAY preamble), because the product's quadrant flickers at every phase-change null and only about half its transitions land inside the eighth-symbol window; the de-rotation above adds the +7.5 Hz case and the bank tests pin the counters and the offset measurement rather than DCD. The tracker gear and the seed release therefore only engage on bursts with a real preamble, which is every real one. Also: `sm-ota sim` aborted with "Internal CLR error" in 5 of roughly 100 runs on the dev box during this work (a minidump is kept under the job's scratch directory); not reproduced in 25 isolated runs, not seen in the suite, not understood. **#11 and #116:** qpsk600's +-5 Hz wall is gone in simulation (a lone branch at +-30 Hz, the bank at its comb, the seed to +-37.5 Hz beyond it) and the QtSM fixture still passes, but neither is closed here - #11 needs its live QtSM retest and #116 still covers afsk300. On-air for the QPSK family remains, as the campaign left it, without a slot to monitor.
- **2026-08-21 (later4) - the off-air `qpsk600` fixture copies, and every PSK mode gains ~1 dB at the knee: seven timing phases per symbol and a clock that holds once a burst is established.** No mode changes status; `qpsk600` has its first real-signal copy of a burst the shipping receiver could not read. Tom's question after the #326 PR was why a receiver could not do what the bench had done to the fixture, which was to try the clock phases and keep the one that passed, and the first answer ("it copies at the luckiest clock phase, not at the clock the receiver recovers") was wrong in the way that matters: the receiver's clock was the problem, not the clock phase. Two things, both measured on the sim rig (N=200, seed 1, deterministic, so every difference is real) and on the fixture. **(1) Timing diversity.** `TimingDiversity` gives `QpskDemodulator` and `BpskDemodulator` seven decision phases per symbol - the recovered clock instant and 2.5, 5 and 7.5 % of a symbol either side, interpolated from a short ring of baseband samples (the late phases wait for samples that arrive after the instant, so decisions are emitted a few samples late, which nothing downstream notices); the matched filter, mixer, clock and offset window are shared, each phase keeps its own reference, tracker, polarity and confidence state, and `QpskModem`/`BpskModem` run one `Il2pReceiver` per phase behind a symbol-clocked content dedupe shorter than any frame, so a frame any phase reads is delivered once. Diversity off -> three phases at 5 % -> seven at 2.5 %: **qpsk600** -1/0 dB 100/166 -> 131/185 -> 146/187 (zero TXDELAY), 112/180 -> 145/193 -> 157/198 (150 ms); **qpsk2400** +6/+7 dB 155/180 -> 184/192 -> 190/195; **bpsk300** -5/-4 dB 133/184 -> 164/195 -> 174/197 (zero TXDELAY), 131/182 -> 160/192 -> 173/194 (150 ms); **bpsk1200** +1/+2 dB 131/186 -> 160/197 -> 173/198; CFO rows hold or improve (qpsk2400 at +9 dB, 15 Hz: 84 -> 98 %; bpsk1200 at +2 dB, 30/60/120 Hz: 84/85/89 -> 97/98/99 %); qpsk3600, detector unchanged, AWGN +8 dB 81 -> 95 %, fm-mic +8 dB CNR 78 -> 85 %. **(2) The clock holds once a burst is established.** A rigid clock copies the fixture at one phase two samples from where the DPLL acquires; the DPLL at 0.94 then wandered 2-10 samples through the payload, which is why the three phases around it landed on 10-12 wrong bytes, and inertia 0.96-0.99 from cold loses the 16-symbol zero-TXDELAY acquisition (#236's finding). So the clock acquires at 0.94 (BPSK 0.92) and holds at 0.995 from the moment the offset window seeds or DCD asserts. With that, `pdn-decode samples/offair/2026-08-21/packet-24738.wav` **copies the frame** - N2IRZ-2 > ID, the TARPN beacon the README records as the truth - with 6 bytes corrected against a limit of 8 (8 with the three-phase set; the hold alone, without the wider phases, is what crosses the line). On the sim knee rows the hold is exactly neutral, because nothing establishes a burst there at zero TXDELAY (the numbers above are identical with it on or off); on BPSK, where the gate is DCD itself (its square-law seed fires before the clock has converged, and a clock frozen then measured the QtSM bpsk300 fixture 10 -> 8 frames and DCD itself no longer asserting), it is neutral on the sim rows too; a 0.5 % correction per transition still follows a 500 ppm symbol-rate error with a tenth of a symbol of lag. **Two faults the measuring found and fixed.** The first cut of the phases measured a null on every row and the fixture: a static-initialiser ordering slip had them at [0, -0, 0], and it was the per-phase fixture scoring (byte-identical errors on all three) that caught it, not the ladder. With the phases live, `Qpsk2400_Differential_Acquires_A_Short_Preamble_After_Idle_With_Noise` lost a burst, and the probe showed the QPSK rotation tracker free-running on noise between bursts at the acquisition gain and parking at its clamp (30 Hz at 1200 Bd) more often than not, where a 24-symbol preamble cannot unlearn it; the #328 chain carried the same walk and a two-sample change to its path exposed it. `QpskDemodulator`'s trackers now integrate only while DCD, a coherent window or in-band energy says a signal is there, and restart from zero at a burst's first energy; the scenario is 10/10 at every noise level from 0.03 to 0.16 and qpsk600's knee gained a few frames on its own (94/162 -> 100/166 with diversity off). **BPSK's tracker stays free-running**, as #236 measured: gating it the same way cost bpsk1200 131/186 -> 120/170, the selection diversity its 90° margin can afford and QPSK's 45° cannot. **Masks:** four rows added a rung below the existing knee rows, floors 2-3 binomial sigma under the N=40 smoke measurement (bpsk300 awgn -5 dB floor 30, measured 37/40; bpsk1200 awgn +1 dB floor 22, measured 34/40; qpsk600 awgn -1 dB floor 22, measured 32/40; qpsk2400 awgn +6 dB floor 26, measured 37/40); every existing row measures at or above its recorded value (bpsk300 -4 dB 40/40 against 37, good 0 dB 24/40 against 22, moderate +2 dB 25/40 against 21, poor +9 dB 7/40 against 7; qpsk600 0 dB 37/40 against 33; qpsk2400 +7 dB 38/40, +9 dB 39/40; every CFO row 40/40). Cost: the decision stage and six extra deframers per branch; the front end is untouched. The fixture README carries the outcome beside its truth; `samples/offair/2026-08-21/` is now a real-signal regression test that passes, pinned in `OffAirQpskTests`.
- **2026-08-21 (later5) - the two C4FSK modes get seven timing phases and the clock hold, and every knee moves 1 to 2 dB.** No mode changes status; `c4fsk9600` and `c4fsk19200` were both working and are now working at materially lower signal. PR #330's technique applied to `C4fskModem` per issue #331: the decision stage runs at the recovered clock instant and 5, 10 and 15 % of a symbol either side, each phase interpolated from a ring of the slicer's normalised input, each with its own 5-tap equalizer, its own freeze hysteresis and its own `Il2pReceiver`, and the modem delivers whichever phase reads a frame once, behind a 32-symbol content dedupe; the filter, energy gate, envelope and clock are shared, and the decision is emitted three points (a third of a symbol) after the instant so the late phases have their samples. The clock acquires at Dire Wolf's 0.74 and holds at 0.995 from the moment DCD asserts, DCD here being the 30-of-32-well-timed-transitions kind, which is the clock having converged. **Measured** on the sim rig, N=200, seed 1, deterministic in (mode, channel, SNR, seed, bursts), before -> after of 200: **c4fsk9600 AWGN** TXDELAY 0, 16/17/18 dB 0/32/104 -> 10/106/182; 150 ms, 20/21/22/23 dB 4/15/36/69 -> 16/39/83/138. **c4fsk19200 AWGN** TXDELAY 0, 19/20/21 dB 3/35/107 -> 23/118/180; 150 ms, 23/24/25/26 dB 1/9/39/89 -> 21/59/123/171. **c4fsk9600 fm-data** (the deployment channel, carrier-to-noise in the receiver IF) TXDELAY 0, 13/14/15/16 dB 1/8/33/73 -> 16/54/110/159; 150 ms, 15/16/17/18 dB 2/12/43/76 -> 8/43/123/176. **c4fsk19200 fm-data** TXDELAY 0, 13/14/15/16 dB 3/10/45/93 -> 22/74/133/174; 150 ms, 14/15/16/17 dB 1/9/33/70 -> 6/26/84/159. As 50 % knees that is c4fsk9600 fm-data 16.6 -> 14.8 dB CNR at TXDELAY 0 and 18.9 -> 16.8 at 150 ms, c4fsk19200 fm-data 16.2 -> 14.4 and 17.9 -> 16.3, c4fsk9600 AWGN 18.0 -> 16.9 and 23.9 -> 22.3, c4fsk19200 AWGN 20.9 -> 19.9 and 26.2 -> 24.6. **The step is twice the PSK modes'**, and that was measured, not assumed: both modes decide at 10 points per symbol at 48 kHz, so the PSK set's 2.5 % is a quarter of a point and c4fsk19200's underlying audio is only 5 samples per symbol. Summed over the eight knee rows (of 1600): no diversity 681, 2.5 % x 3 pairs 1223, **5 % x 3 pairs 1339**, 7.5 % x 3 pairs 1341, 10 % x 2 pairs 1292, 5 % x 4 pairs (nine phases) 1366. Reaching past ~15 % buys nothing and a ninth phase buys 27 frames of 1600, inside the noise, so the mode keeps seven phases and the PSK family's phase count. **Attribution**, same eight rows: the hold alone (one phase) 721, the phases alone (no hold) 1311, both 1339. The hold is exactly neutral on AWGN and worth 4 to 8 frames of 200 on each fm-data row, which is where these modes actually live; it is kept for that and because it is what the phases need to be able to reach anything. **Cost**: the decision stage and six extra deframers, no change to the front end. A fixed 100-burst point at one worker measures 2.68 -> 2.51 s (c4fsk9600) and 2.13 -> 2.29 s (c4fsk19200) of wall clock, so the sim rig is unchanged within its own scatter. **Masks**: nothing moved. Both C4FSK rows in the FM tier sit at 24 dB CNR, where both modes measured 25/25 before and after; the FM tier's rule is that rows sit on the flat, above the knee, and the knee is where this change shows. **Two honest negatives.** `fm-mic` is 0/25 at 30 and 40 dB CNR for both modes, before and after: a 4800 or 9600 symbol-a-second baseband does not pass a 300-3000 Hz emphasised voice path at any signal level, which is a mode limit and the reason these modes are data-port-only, not something a decision stage can move. And **TXDELAY costs these modes several decibels**, before this change and after it: c4fsk9600's AWGN knee is 16.9 dB at TXDELAY 0 and 22.3 dB at 150 ms, c4fsk19200's 19.9 and 24.6, with fm-data showing 2 dB of the same. A longer run-in making a receiver worse is backwards, it is the rank-deficient preamble training the equalizer's freeze hysteresis only partly cures (see the freeze comment in `C4fskModem`), and it is worth more than this change was. **The instrument.** Per-phase scoring of a known frame (`C4fskTimingDiversityTests`) - each phase's decided bits aligned on the clock instant's own sync word and compared byte by byte with what was transmitted - is asserted rather than assumed, after PR #330's [0, -0, 0] slip read a clean null on the ladder. It also found something the ladder cannot see: on a **clean** burst the +15 % phase misreads one symbol of the sync word while -15 % is byte-perfect, because the clock's wrap can only land on a sample, so the decision instant is already up to a tenth of a symbol late and the phases are not centred on the eye. Reading the decision back to the instant the clock asked for, as `QpskDemodulator` does with its wrap overshoot, is the obvious next thing to measure here.
- **2026-08-21 (later6) - the AFSK family gets the PSK chain's timing diversity and clock hold; every AFSK knee moves about a third of a decibel, and the measurement says plainly which half of the change earned it.** No mode changes status. `AfskDemodulator` now keeps its slicer's input in a short ring and decides every bit at the seven `TimingDiversity` phases (the recovered clock instant and 2.5, 5 and 7.5 % of a bit either side, interpolated, the late phases waiting for samples that arrive after the instant so a bit's decisions are emitted a few samples late); the front end, the clock, the envelope trackers and DCD are shared, phase 0 is byte-identical to the bit the plain sink has always been given, and each wrapper runs one deframer per phase behind a content dedupe clocked in bits - an `Il2pReceiver` per phase in `Afsk300Modem` and `Afsk1200Il2pModem`, an `HdlcDeframer` (plus the `Fx25Deframer` where there is one) per phase in `Afsk1200Modem`, and per phase per branch in `Afsk1200MultiModem`, whose own bank dedupe already merged copies. The clock acquires at the DPLL's usual 0.74 and holds at 0.995 once `PacketDcd` asserts, gated exactly as BPSK gates it. **Sim ladder** (N=200, seed 1, deterministic, before -> after): `afsk300-il2pc` awgn -3/-2/-1/0 dB 2/61/177/197 -> 4/92/187/198, and at 150 ms TXDELAY 1/80/174/199 -> 3/97/187/200; CFO 35/70 Hz at -1 dB 183/183 -> 191/190 and at 0 dB 200/199 -> 200/200. `afsk300` (plain HDLC, no FEC, so the benefit is any phase whose FCS checks) awgn -1/0/+1/+2 dB 8/70/161/195 -> 13/86/175/197. `afsk1200` awgn +4/+5/+6/+7 dB 3/27/107/152 -> 6/49/129/173; on the FM channels at +10/+11/+12 dB CNR, fm-mic 80/143/189 -> 85/147/189 and fm-data 80/148/185 -> 87/149/185. `afsk1200-il2p` at +7/+8/+9 dB CNR, fm-mic 8/93/168 -> 11/98/170 and fm-data 50/143/185 -> 60/151/188. **Where it buys nothing, said plainly.** On the fading channels `afsk300-il2pc` is flat (N=60 at 0-12 dB: good +12 dB 49 -> 50, moderate +12 dB 27 -> 30, poor +12 dB 3 -> 2, every other rung within a frame); a fade is not a timing problem and no number of decision phases makes it one. On the FM channels the gain is a fraction of the AWGN gain, because a limiter-discriminator link fails in bursts of carrier noise rather than at a mistimed sample - `afsk1200` at +12 dB CNR is identical either way. **The two halves, measured apart.** Diversity alone (hold disabled) reproduces the whole result to within a frame on every row tried (`afsk300-il2pc` -2 dB 92 and 97 with TXDELAY, `afsk1200` +6 dB 128 against 129, `afsk300` 0 dB 85 against 86), so the phases are the entire sim gain. The hold alone (phase 0 only) is exactly neutral on `afsk300-il2pc` (-2/-1 dB 61/177 and 80/174 with TXDELAY, the before numbers to the frame) and slightly positive on `afsk1200` (+5/+6/+7 dB 27/107/152 -> 28/110/157). It is kept for the reason `QpskDemodulator` measured on the off-air fixture rather than for a sim row: nothing establishes a burst at zero TXDELAY, and a clock that no longer wanders is the one the phases can reach past on a real capture. **Why 300 baud gains less than the PSK modes did.** Sweeping the decision instant plus or minus 15 samples over a known bit stream (`AfskEyeSweepProbe`) draws the eye: at 300 baud, 40 samples per bit, the error count is flat across a nine-sample plateau (offsets -6 to +2 all within 34/30 errors of each other), so the phases at 1 to 3 samples out usually decide the same bit and only the ones near the plateau's edge differ; at 1200 baud, 10 samples per bit, the useful window is about [-2, 0] samples and one sample late nearly doubles the bit errors (21 -> 39), which is why fractions of a sample matter more there than their size suggests. **The instrument.** `AfskTimingDiversityTests` scores a known 1088-bit stream at every phase against what was sent and requires every phase to disagree with the clock instant somewhere, at a noise level that costs bits; it is the guard against the [0, -0, 0] slip that made the first PSK cut read a clean null, and it also pins phase 0 byte-identical to the plain bit sink. **Masks:** one row added, `afsk300-il2pc` awgn -1 dB floor 30, measured 37/40 (N=40, seed 1); the existing 0 dB row measures 40/40 against its floor of 30 and its own recorded 39/40. The rung where the change actually shows, -2 dB, measures 16/40 -> 25/40 and is deliberately not pinned: at 62 % it sits on the cliff, where a mask measures the machine's floating point as much as the modem. **Cost:** user CPU +6 % on `afsk300-il2pc` (an 11-branch bank, so 77 IL2P receivers running) and +12 % on `afsk1200-multi` (15 branches); the front end is untouched and it dominates. Full suite green, and the real-audio fixtures are unchanged: the off-air `samples/offair/m9psy-40m-afsk300-il2pc-qrm.wav` QRM capture still decodes three ways, the NinoTNC AFSK parity captures and the QtSM `afsk1200` corpus still decode exactly. Issue #331, PR #334.
- **2026-08-21 (later7) - the direct-FSK modes take the timing phases and gain 1.5 to 2 dB at every knee; the clock hold that came with them on PSK is measured, rejected and explained.** No mode changes status. Issue #331's second item, rx-roadmap workstream 10. **(1) Timing diversity, which is the whole gain.** `FskModem` decides every symbol at seven phases - the recovered clock instant and 10, 20 and 30 % of a symbol either side, interpolated from a short ring of the slicer's own input, the late phases waiting for points that arrive after the instant so decisions come out a few points late - and gives each phase its own deframer, plus its own G3RUH descrambler and NRZI state on the classic leg, since both are bit-serial and a phase that reads a different bit must carry the difference forward. A frame any phase reads is delivered once, behind a symbol-clocked content dedupe 48 symbols wide. Phase 0 is the clock's own instant and reads exactly the level the DPLL sliced, so the long-standing behaviour is preserved as the first branch. Measured on the sim rig (N=200, seed 1, deterministic), before -> after: **fsk9600-il2p** AWGN +9/+10/+11 dB 45/128/177 -> 135/183/197 at TXDELAY 150 and 0/26/98 -> 10/92/164 at TXDELAY 0, fm-data +10/+11/+12 dB CNR 106/156/183 -> 171/188/199 and 108/164/186 -> 175/196/200; **fsk4800-il2p** AWGN +6/+7/+8 dB 16/74/133 -> 51/147/180 and 0/4/43 -> 1/26/112, fm-data +9/+10/+11 dB CNR 78/138/185 -> 137/181/193 and 81/146/184 -> 137/183/195; **fsk9600 classic HDLC**, where the only FEC is "some phase's FCS checks", AWGN +14/+15/+16 dB 63/119/159 -> 167/195/199 and fm-data +12/+13/+14 dB CNR 27/83/135 -> 136/188/198. Every rung above the knee stays at 100 % (AWGN and fm-data at +16, +20 and +30 dB), so nothing was bought by decoding worse when the signal is good. Two negatives that stay negative: **the classic leg still cannot acquire at TXDELAY 0** (0/200 before and after, at every rung to +20 dB - two opening flags is not a run-in and no number of decision phases invents one), and **9600 GFSK still does not pass a microphone path** (0/50 at +15, +21 and +27 dB CNR, so `Fast_Fsk_Cannot_Pass_Through_A_Microphone_Path` holds unchanged). One small positive nobody asked for: **4800 GFSK now gets its first frames ever through a microphone path**, 0/50 -> 4/50 at +27 dB CNR and 8/50 at +33, which is a trickle and not a usable mode, recorded because it was 0 at every level before. **The step is this mode's own, and was measured rather than inherited.** At 48 kHz these modes decide on ten points per symbol (9600 is interpolated x2, 4800 already has ten), so the PSK set's 2.5 % is a quarter of one point. At fsk9600-il2p AWGN +10 dB, N=200, TXDELAY 150: 128 of 200 with no diversity, 172 at 2.5 %, 178 at 5 %, 183 at 10 %, 183 again at 15 % - the union saturates by 10 %, which reaches 30 % of a symbol either side, so that is the step and going wider only spends deframers. Per-phase scoring of a known burst (the instrument #331 requires, and `The_Timing_Phases_Read_The_Same_Burst_Differently` as a pinned test) shows the phases are real and, interestingly, asymmetric: on one fsk9600 burst at the edge the instant got 21 bits wrong of 1360 while -10 % got 13 and +10 % got 62, because the clock wraps on the sample that overshoots and this chain does not interpolate its crossings, so phase 0 reads systematically late. Recentring the whole set 15 % early was tried and is worth nothing the symmetric set does not already cover (138/183/197 against 135/183/196). **(2) The clock hold: built, measured, rejected, and this is the honest negative of the workstream.** On the sim rows a DCD-gated hold at 0.995 is neutral, exactly as it is on BPSK: with diversity on, hold on against hold off, fsk9600-il2p AWGN +9/+10/+11 dB 135/183/196 against 135/183/197, fsk4800-il2p AWGN +6/+7/+8 dB 51/147/180 both ways, fsk9600 fm-data +12/+13/+14 dB CNR 136/188/198 both ways, and fsk9600-il2p fm-data at N=600 (+10/+11 dB CNR) 520/564 with it against 524/569 without, about one sigma down; with diversity off it is neutral to a frame (45/128/176 against the 45/128/177 this mode has always measured). It was verified live rather than assumed neutral - forced to 0.0 the same rows read 45/127/166, so the gate does fire and does change the clock. **What settled it was the cost.** A test this repository did not have, `The_Clock_Tracks_A_Mistuned_Transmitter`, resamples the burst as heard from a transmitter whose crystal is out by a given number of parts per million: as the mode stands it copies at +-2000 ppm at both bauds and both signs, and with the hold in it fails from +-500 ppm (both bauds, both signs, clean signal, 200 ppm the last rung that passes). The mechanism is this chain's one deliberate difference from the PSK chains, and it is in its own source comment: it does not interpolate its zero crossings, so a nudge is quantised to a tenth of a symbol and the loop's entire correction is (1 - inertia) times the measured phase. At 0.74 that is 26 % per transition and follows any real crystal; at 0.995 it is 0.5 %, the loop bandwidth falls by twenty, and a mistuned transmitter walks the clock past DCD's own window - DCD drops, the clock re-acquires, DCD asserts, the hold re-engages, and the frame dies in the limit cycle. A stiff clock and an unresolved crossing do not go together, so the hold is not shipped for these modes; `FskModem`'s inertia constant carries the whole measurement so the next person does not repeat it, and the ppm test is the guard. Worth revisiting only if this chain ever gets matched-filter timing, which is a standing note in `Process` from long before this work. **Masks:** six rows added, one per mode per channel, a rung below where each old knee sat, floors 2-3 binomial sigma under an N=25 seed-1 TXDELAY-150 measurement - fsk9600-il2p awgn +11 dB floor 21 (measured 25/25, was 22/25), fsk4800-il2p awgn +8 dB floor 17 (22/25, was 17/25), fsk9600 awgn +16 dB floor 21 (25/25, was 19/25), fsk9600-il2p fm-data +12 dB floor 21 (25/25, was 23/25), fsk4800-il2p fm-data +11 dB floor 21 (25/25, was 22/25), fsk9600 fm-data +14 dB floor 21 (25/25, was 15/25). fsk4800-il2p had no mask of any kind before this and now has two. No existing floor moves in either direction and the whole suite is green, including the Dire Wolf cross-validation, the NinoTNC parity set, the QtSM RUH-4800 interop pin and the `samples/pdn/` corpus QC. Cost: the decision stage and six extra deframers per modem; the filter, the envelope-tracking slicer, the clock and DCD are all shared and untouched.
- **2026-08-22 - QPSK packet DCD is scored on the symbol decisions rather than on slicer transitions: it now asserts on every decodable burst of the differential family (clean, off-frequency, minimum preamble), never on noise, and the tracker gear and clock hold it drives engage as designed (issue #329).** No mode changes status. Before this the differential QPSK path fed Dire Wolf's transition-timing `PacketDcd` with the quadrant changes of the one-symbol conjugate product, and the issue's measurement stood: DCD asserted for a qpsk600 burst only with the carrier on frequency and some noise present. The reason is worse than the issue put it. The product's angle is the phase CHANGE between consecutive symbols, so it changes quadrant only when consecutive dibits differ; at every phase change the product's magnitude nulls and its angle there is whatever noise makes it, so a noisy product flickers through the other quadrants around each null, and on the all-reversal IL2P preamble (a 180-degree change every symbol) a clean product never changes quadrant at all. The transition phases the DPLL reported were 40-60 % inside the eighth-symbol window in every condition, so 30 of 32 was never reachable by that route, and the de-rotation of #328 could only move the hump, not narrow it. **The envelope-null timing source the issue suggested first was built, measured and rejected** (an honest negative): feeding the DPLL one event per phase change at the matched-filter output's null, parabolic sub-sample interpolation, half-symbol hold-off, gave 39-54 % in window and a clean +60 dB burst still spread its nulls over half a symbol, because at qpsk600's 0.20 roll-off the neighbouring symbols move each null by up to a third of a symbol (data-dependent ISI self-noise), and the 0.995 clock hold then froze the clock at whatever offset acquisition had left (the hump's centre moved between the 40 and 60 dB rows). No per-transition scorer can reach 30 of 32 from those nulls. **What ships instead is `QpskDecisionDcd`**: at every clock instant the detector already has the symbol-instant sample relative to its reference phasor (the decision-feedback chain) or the de-rotated conjugate product (qpsk3600's plain-product chain); the fourth power of that unit phasor strips the data and leaves cos(4e), e the angle error from the nearer axis, +1 on a clean decision and zero-mean on noise; an exponential average over 32 symbols estimates exp(-8 sigma^2) of the decision's angle error, asserts DCD at 0.55 (about 16 degrees rms, a dB or so below every QPSK knee) and releases at 0.10 (about 31 degrees rms). On noise the average is 0.09 wide: ten minutes of idle noise at 300 Bd and at 1800 Bd produced no assert and a maximum of 0.36 and 0.37; the 10 s idle rows at three noise levels are 0 of 100 blocks. `PacketDcd` and `BitDpll` are untouched, the coherent QPSK path keeps both and its deframer-reset rule exactly as they were, and BPSK is not involved. **The public-API gate** (`QpskModem.Qpsk600`, differential, one 60-byte burst in 2.5 s of noise, DCD polled per 100 ms block, seed 1), before -> after in DCD blocks of 25: +8 dB at 150 ms TXDELAY, 0 Hz 14 -> 17; -7.5/+7.5/-15/+15 Hz 0/0/0/0 -> 15/15/14/14; +8 dB at zero TXDELAY (the 16-symbol preamble) 0 -> 16; +40 dB clean 0 Hz 0 -> 18 and 11.25 Hz 0 -> 12 (and that lone-modem burst now copies, see below); +60 dB 0 -> 18; 0 dB 0 -> 16 (150 ms) and 0 -> 14 (zero TXDELAY); -1 dB 0 -> 14. DCD asserts 24-54 symbols into a burst on frequency (inside a 150 ms preamble, 45 symbols) and 86-124 symbols in for a lone modem two bank steps out. qpsk2400 at +8 dB asserted 171 symbols into its 180-symbol preamble before and asserts at 46 now, covers the whole burst at zero TXDELAY (0 -> 3 of 3 blocks) and at +-7.5/+-15 Hz; qpsk3600 (plain product, FM chain) asserts at +8 dB on and off frequency too. The frame decodes in every one of those rows except the single -1 dB realisation, which decoded before and does not now while the N=200 row below gains six; its cost is the noise. **Release** is the cost: 60 to 130 symbols after the burst's end (200-430 ms at qpsk600, 50-110 ms at qpsk2400), where the old DCD released in 31 on the one row it had asserted on, because the average has to decay through the noise floor's own excursions; a release level of 0.25 (about 45-75 symbols) was measured first and rejected below. **What DCD drives, measured one at a time on the knee rows (N=200, seed 1, deterministic):** the tracker gear (acquire 0.05 -> track 0.01 on DCD) is worth 40-50 frames: with it disabled qpsk600 at -1 dB falls 149 -> 101 and qpsk2400 at +6 dB 188 -> 101; the clock hold's inertia is immaterial on the sim rows (0.995, 0.98 and 0.94 give 140, 142 and 141 on qpsk3600 at +8 dB) and the hold now gates on DCD alone rather than on the offset window's seed as well, which cures a #330 side effect the probe found: a lone modem at +40 dB and 11.25 Hz (beyond the tracker's one-step clamp, so the seed has to do it) had its clock frozen by the seed while the pre-seed rotation residual was still biasing it, and the burst did not copy; with the hold waiting for good decisions it copies. **The one real regression found and fixed on the way:** with DCD now asserting at the knee, `QpskModem`'s reset of its deframers on the DCD falling edge fired mid-burst whenever the average dipped, and on qpsk3600 at +8 dB (where the plain product's coherence sits near 0.5, inside the first cut's 0.55/0.25 band) that cost 43 of 200 frames on AWGN and 30 on fm-mic: 16 of 50 probe bursts had an in-burst DCD drop and every one of those lost its frame, and disabling the reset alone gave every row back. The release level went to 0.10 (183/175 of 200 back from 140/144) and the differential path's reset now fires when the channel goes quiet, DCD and in-band energy both down, rather than on a DCD dip alone, which gave back the last one or two frames on two rows. **Ladder, before -> after (N=200, seed 1, 60-byte frames):** zero TXDELAY: qpsk600 AWGN -1/0 dB 146/187 -> 152/188; qpsk2400 +6/+7 dB 190/195 -> 190/194; qpsk600 +4 dB at 3.75/15/30 Hz 200/200/200 -> 200/200/200; qpsk2400 +9 dB at 15/60/120 Hz 192/199/199 -> 192/199/199; qpsk3600 +8 dB AWGN/fm-mic 183/174 -> 183/175. 150 ms TXDELAY: qpsk600 -1/0 dB 157/198 -> 163/199; qpsk2400 +6/+7 dB 195/199 -> 198/199; qpsk600 +4 dB at 3.75/15/30 Hz 200/200/200 -> 200/200/200; qpsk2400 +9 dB at 15/60/120 Hz 200/200/200 -> 200/200/200; qpsk3600 +8 dB AWGN/fm-mic 180/163 -> 189/175. Fading (N=100): qpsk600 Good +4/+8/+12 dB 69/81/88 -> 68/82/88, Moderate 44/54/62 -> 46/56/64, Poor 5/13/15 -> 6/13/14; qpsk2400 Good 12/34/48 -> 13/34/48, Moderate 3/9/19 -> 3/10/21, Poor 1/2/4 -> 1/2/4. The one row down is qpsk2400 +7 dB at zero TXDELAY, 195 -> 194, one seed; every other row holds or gains, the gains being the gear and the hold engaging on bursts they previously could not. No mask moves. **Fixtures:** `pdn-decode samples/offair/2026-08-21/packet-24738.wav` copies the N2IRZ-2 beacon with 6 bytes corrected of 8 as before and `OffAirQpskTests` passes; `NinoTncParityTests` (the idle-with-noise qpsk2400 scenario 10/10), `QtsmInteropTests`, the NinoTNC captures and the full suite are green; `QpskMultiModemTests` regains the two DCD assertions its BPSK twins have (the damaged burst asserts DCD, the offset is read while DCD holds). New: `QpskDecisionDcdTests` (unit, including a million-symbol noise run) and `QpskDcdTests` (the public-API gate as a test: nine burst rows, the noise rows, reset).
- **2026-08-22 (later) - a longer TXDELAY no longer costs the C4FSK modes anything on the data port and costs them under a decibel on AWGN, and the instrument that found the cause says it was not the equalizer.** No mode changes status; `c4fsk9600` and `c4fsk19200` were working and now work at lower signal after any run-in. Issue #336, from the 2026-08-21 (later5) negative: c4fsk9600's AWGN knee was 16.9 dB at TXDELAY 0 and 22.3 dB at 150 ms, c4fsk19200's 19.9 and 24.6, with 2 dB of the same on fm-data, and the named suspect was the 5-tap equalizer training on the rank-deficient 0x77 alternation. **The instrument** (`C4fskTxDelayProbe`, env-gated `C4FSK_TXDELAY_PROBE=1`, on the new `C4fskModem.DecisionObserver` seam, over the sim rig's own bursts, N=40 seeds) reads the receiver at the moment the sync word arrives, after a 20 ms run-in and a 150 and 300 ms one, at 18 and 22 dB (9600) and 21 and 25 dB (19200). **What it showed, before:** the equalizer's taps were within 0.03 of identity and frozen at the sync in every long-run-in case (dev 0.023 to 0.036), so the suspect is cleared; the envelope tracker was the cause. It was a per-point peak follower (attack 0.08, decay 1e-5), which on a noisy waveform is a max-hold that climbs to the largest noise excursion of the whole run-in: at the sync after 150 ms the half-swing sat 1.20 to 1.26 times the clean burst's at 22 to 25 dB and 1.34 to 1.38 at 18 to 21 dB, against 1.00 to 1.07 after 20 ms; the sync word's own outer symbols read 0.70 to 0.75 normalised against 0.89 to 0.94, a hair over the 2/3 slice; 32 of 36 front-of-frame errors at 22 dB were outer symbols demoted to inner; and at 18 dB the clock instant's stream never carried the sync word at all after 150 ms (0 of 40, against 40 of 40 after 20 ms). **The fix:** the follower keeps the job it is good at, reaching the signal from nothing inside the 20 ms run-in floor, for the first 32 symbols after the energy gate opens, and then hands over to a decision-directed envelope: one reading per symbol at the clock instant, outer decisions only, moving each peak 0.05 of the way toward its reading up or down alike, so noise averages out of the reference instead of being held at its maximum, and inner decisions hold it, which is the hold-through-inner-heavy-stretches behaviour the slow decay bought on the real corpus. After: half-swing at the sync 1.03 to 1.08 of clean at every TXDELAY, the sync outer symbols at 0.86 to 0.89, the sync found 29 to 40 of 40, c4fsk9600 at 18 dB decoding 32 of 40 after 150 ms where it decoded 0. **Measured** on the sim rig, N=200, seed 1, deterministic, before -> after of 200 (50 % knees in brackets). **c4fsk9600 AWGN** TXDELAY 0, 16/17/18 dB 10/106/182 -> 49/147/189 [16.9 -> 16.5]; 150 ms, 17/18/19/20 dB 0/0/3/16 -> 83/153/191/196 [22.3 -> 17.2]; 300 ms, 0/0/0/5 -> 55/148/181/197 [22.4 -> 17.5]. **c4fsk19200 AWGN** TXDELAY 0, 19/20/21 dB 23/118/180 -> 64/156/193 [19.8 -> 19.4]; 150 ms, 19/20/21/22 dB 0/0/0/3 -> 29/127/188/196 [24.6 -> 19.7]; 300 ms, 0/0/0/3 -> 18/120/184/198 [24.7 -> 19.8]. **c4fsk9600 fm-data** (CNR) TXDELAY 0, 12/13/14/15 dB 1/16/54/110 -> 23/88/161/186 [14.8 -> 13.2]; 150 ms, 0/0/0/8 -> 29/116/168/190 [16.7 -> 12.8]; 300 ms, 0/0/1/4 -> 29/101/164/188 [17.1 -> 13.0]. **c4fsk19200 fm-data** TXDELAY 0, 3/22/74/133 -> 54/126/176/195 [14.4 -> 12.6]; 150 ms, 0/0/6/26 -> 72/147/186/199 [16.2 -> 12.4]; 300 ms, 0/0/4/25 -> 73/150/181/196 [16.0 -> 12.4].
