On the data port, which is where these modes live, the 150 and 300 ms knees now sit at or below the 0 ms knee on both modes, and every knee moved 1.6 to 4.7 dB. On AWGN the long run-ins finish 0.3 to 0.7 dB above the 0 ms knee, and that remainder is the rig rather than the receiver: the sim calibrates its noise against the mean power of the whole active burst, and the 0x77 alternation through our 1.0x shaping is a tone 2.15 dB hotter than the 4-PAM frame (measured, `C4fskPreamblePowerProbe`), so 150 ms of it hands the frame 1.0 dB less SNR than the 20 ms floor does and 300 ms 1.25 dB less; the FM axis is carrier-to-noise and has no such term. Recorded as a rig property, not changed. **Two measured negatives, kept out.** A running-mean envelope that restarts at the hand-over and again when the alternation ends, so the frame's own level replaces the preamble's within the sync word (the alternation reads 14 to 16 % above a symbol's level through the shaping, a clean-burst fact the probe also shows), lands the envelope prettily by the header and costs 138 frames over the six AWGN knee rows against the plain 0.05 tracker, neutral on fm-data. And reading the 4-PAM decision back to the instant the clock asked for (the wrap's point overshoot, as `QpskDemodulator` does) cures the +15 % phase's clean-burst sync misread that the later5 entry noted, and is a wash on the ladder: +11 to +22 frames of 600 at TXDELAY 0 and on fm-data, -15 to -34 at AWGN 150 ms, +4 net over the seven rows it was run on. Neither is in. **Fixtures**: `NinoTncParityTests` (TXDELAY-0 acquisition, both modes), `C4fskEqualizerTests` (the ISI-closed eye and the 20 s idle-then-burst), the MultiDecode round trips and the catalogue tests unchanged and green; the full suite 1785 passed, 0 failed. `C4fskTimingDiversityTests` still proves the phases differ, but its c4fsk19200 burst had to get noisier (sigma 0.20 -> 0.36): after the fix six of its seven phases read the old burst byte-perfect, which is the receiver improving, not the instrument weakening. The NinoTNC corpus probes (`C4fskContentProbeTests`) are env-gated on a corpus that is not on this box (`/tmp/nino-corpus` is empty here), so they were not run; the change is argued safe for them (a NinoTNC's 2-up-2-down preamble never alternates, the hand-over happens 32 symbols in, and a decision-directed outer reference sits below the peak, which moves ISI-squeezed outer symbols away from the slice, not toward it) but not proven, and that is the one thing a corpus run should check. **Masks**: the two C4FSK fm-data rows at 24 dB CNR are untouched (25/25 before and after); with both data-port knees down about 2 dB, a row a rung below is added for each at 18 dB CNR, TXDELAY 150, floor 36 of 40 against a measured 40/40 (seed 1) and 200/200 on the ladder. Regression cover: `C4fskTxDelayTests` pins the envelope at the sync after 300 ms to within 10 % of the 20 ms one and the sync outer symbols above 0.8, and a 300 ms run-in decoding at least what a 0 ms one does (c4fsk9600 22 dB, c4fsk19200 25 dB, 20 seeds each; 15 and 22 of 40 before).
- **2026-08-22 (later2) - a run of identical scrambled bits no longer reads as silence: the four ordinary frames a noiseless bench could never decode now copy, and the same latent fault is closed on every mode that shared it (issue #339).** pdn-qso's hermetic rig (its PR #21) found `bpsk300` deterministically failing 4 of 256 otherwise identical 56-byte UI frames - differing in one payload byte - over a perfectly silent channel, `RsFailures` and `CrcFailures` both zero, at every TXDELAY tried, while noise at even 120 dB SNR made every one decode first time. **The mechanism, traced to the line.** The demodulated bit stream was measured bit-perfect even on the failing frames; what killed them was carrier detect. Each failing frame's IL2P-scrambled stream holds a 48-bit run of one bit value starting at bit 270 (the other 252 values of that byte top out at 25), and a run of reversals is a full-strength alternating carrier whose differential product never changes sign: no slicer transitions. `PacketDcd.OnSymbol`'s quiet-symbol path - built so a squelched-silent channel cannot latch DCD on for ever - counted 24 transition-free symbols as carrier-gone and dropped DCD in mid-frame (measured at sample 12401 of a 29200-sample burst on all four frames), and `BpskModem`'s falling-edge deframer reset, the continuous-decode robustness fix, abandoned the collection with the sync word already consumed. Nothing was retried because nothing was left to retry: zero RS failures, zero CRC failures, exactly as reported. **Why noise "fixed" it.** During a reversal run the product grazes zero once per symbol at the envelope nulls, and which side of zero a graze lands is a float residue (measured decaying 3e-8 -> 3e-11 -> 2e-14 over successive grazes) that dies within a few symbols on a bit-exact input but chatters - as well-timed transitions - under any noise at all. Acquisition conditioned on noise being present, at its most fragile exactly where the signal is strongest, as the issue predicted. **The fix, in the shared class.** `PacketDcd.OnSymbol` now takes the demodulator's own per-symbol decision magnitude, keeps a ~32-symbol mean of it, and only counts a symbol toward the quiet drop when its magnitude has collapsed below a quarter of that mean: silence means no transitions AND no signal. All five users hand over their own evidence - BPSK the differential product (or coherent amplitude), AFSK and FSK their slicer excess, C4FSK its equalized reading, QPSK-coherent its baseband power. The assert path, transition scoring, and every falling-edge deframer reset are untouched, and release into real silence is measured landing on exactly the same sample as before the fix (bpsk300: release at sample 30601 for a burst ending at 29200, both sides). **Scope, measured not assumed - every one of the catalogue's 38 modes swept noiselessly, pre-fix and post-fix builds, over two 2048-frame families: the pdn-qso perf shape (256 session values x 8 sequence numbers, TXDELAY 300) and 2048 random-content 40-byte-payload UI frames (payload seeded by frame index).** Seven modes lose frames pre-fix on the perf family, all with the same frames and the same mechanism: `bpsk300`, `bpsk300-multi` and `bpsk300-nocrc` lose the 4 48-bit-run frames; `afsk1200-il2p` and both direct-FSK IL2P legs (`fsk4800-il2p`, `fsk9600-il2p`) lose 6, the four plus the two whose runs are 25 bits, because a held tone or a held NRZ level has no grazes at all, so those modes need no float coincidence and even noise does not save them (the issue's 300-arbitrary-frame afsk sampling had simply missed a 1-in-341 event; the pre-fix DCD traces drop inside the run, `fsk4800-il2p` at sample 16483 of a 20848-sample burst, `fsk9600-il2p` at 15465 of 17648); `afsk1200-il2p-nocrc` loses the 4. The random family corroborates independently: frame r1699 is lost pre-fix by `afsk1200-il2p`, `fsk4800-il2p` and `fsk9600-il2p` alike. Single `Afsk300Modem` and single `Afsk1200Il2pModem` lose the session-194 frame outright in direct traces, and the deployed `afsk300-il2pc` bank's escape at TXDELAY 300 was luck, not immunity: swept again at TXDELAY 150 it loses all six. The affected modes were also swept at TXDELAY 0 and 150 (pre-fix: the same frames fail; post-fix: none), consistent with the issue's every-TXDELAY finding. Clean pre-fix, with the structural reason stated: `bpsk1200` (the graze chatter happens to survive its 10-sample symbols - the pre-fix DCD trace holds through the whole burst), `qpsk600`/`qpsk2400`/`qpsk3600` (V.26A rotates at least 45 degrees every symbol by construction, and the differential family's decision DCD took #329's fix), classic `fsk9600` and the AFSK HDLC/FX.25 arms (bit stuffing bounds a run at six), `c4fsk9600`/`c4fsk19200` (bits are gated on in-band energy, so a run cannot read as silence), and the `freedv-datac*` and `ms110d-wn*` families (their own sync machinery, no `PacketDcd`). Post-fix: 0/2048 on every mode in both families. After: 0/2048 on every mode above. **Gates:** the new `NoiselessRunTests` pin the four frames through the single modem and the deployed bank plus the `PacketDcd` semantics both ways (a full-strength transition-free run holds DCD; 24 signal-free symbols still drop it), and all five fail without the fix; the full suite is green (1812 passed / 0 failed); the full `SM_MASK_GATE=1` Watterson ladder was run pre- and post-fix on the same seeds and is identical point for point (95 rows green both sides; the 81 per-point successes/trials readings match exactly) - the change is inert wherever noise exists, which is everywhere but a bench. Fix and evidence in PR #341.
- **2026-08-22 (later3) - bpsk300's factory and the deployed bank now transmit and match at the same 0.35 roll-off; the factory's 0.20 rested on a mis-measured NinoTNC reference, and the daemon never ran it.** No mode changes status, and no deployed behaviour changes at all: the fix moves the bench path to the air's value, not the air. Issue #340, found during #339 when a reproducer built against `BpskModem.Bpsk300` (roll-off 0.20) would not reproduce what pdn-qso saw through the catalogue's `BpskMultiModem` bank, whose branches take the constructor's 0.35 default. The history: 0.20 went into the factory on 2026-07-15 to match "the 328 Hz a NinoTNC's own mode-8 transmission measures"; the bank arrived three days later building its branches through the constructor and bypassing it; the QPSK bank (2026-08-21) routes branches through the per-mode factories precisely so per-mode configuration travels, but the BPSK bank never got that treatment, and `OccupiedBandwidthTests` certified the factory, so the divergence was invisible. Measured before deciding anything (`RollOffDiscrepancyProbe`, `ROLLOFF_PROBE=1`, deterministic seeds): **(1) the 328 Hz was the pre-issue-#2 highest-energy-window mis-measurement** - whole-burst and like-for-like, exactly as the shipping never-wider test measures, the same `samples/ninotnc/bpsk300.wav` reads **398 Hz**, its spectrum flat to about plus or minus 150 Hz with steep skirts (-27 dB at 250 Hz out, -44 dB at 300), wider than either of our candidate shapes (0.20 = 328 Hz, 0.35 = 352 Hz by the same meter), so the never-wider rule never forced 0.20. **(2) Loopback cost of the roll-off: none.** At the -5 dB AWGN knee (N=400) matched 0.35 copies 264 and matched 0.20 copies 268, statistically identical; the cross terms (246 and 258) put the worst filter-mismatch penalty near 0.2 dB. **(3) The interop direction favours 0.35, measurably.** The real NinoTNC burst under calibrated AWGN: rx 0.35 copies 248/400 against rx 0.20's 210/400 at -5 dB (about 4 sigma, roughly 0.4 dB), 36 vs 14 at -6 dB, 185 vs 174 and 199 vs 195 per 200 at -4 and -3. And the real-TNC bench interop was only ever validated at 0.35: nino-bench has always built bpsk300 through the constructor default, including the "all 13 modes re-benched, 6/6 both ways" run inside the very commit that chose 0.20, so the factory's 0.20 was never once measured against the real TNC. So the factory aligns to the bank, not the other way: `BpskModem.Bpsk300` now passes the 0.35 default, `OccupiedBandwidthTests` builds every mode through `ModemCatalog.Create` so the certified transmitter is the one the daemon keys (bpsk300's certified OBW becomes the bank's 352 Hz, 12 % inside the reference recording), and `BpskTxShapingParityTests` pins factory, bank and catalogue arm to sample-identical transmit audio for both bpsk modes so this class of divergence fails instead of lurking. The deployed receive path is untouched by construction and the mask smoke tier confirms it: all 48 rows green, the bpsk rows point for point at or above their recorded values (bpsk300 awgn -5 dB 37/40, -4 dB 40/40, good 24/40, moderate 25/40, poor 7/40; bpsk1200 34/40 and 40/40). Deferred, named: `qpsk600` keeps its 0.20 - its factory comment cites the same superseded 328 Hz figure, but its bank routes through the factory so both paths agree, its masks were measured at 0.20, and moving it is a separate campaign with its own A/B (issue #344). docs/dev/bench/ninotnc-loop.md carries the dated correction to the July table. Fix and evidence in PR #345.
- **2026-08-22 (later4) - the diversity banks and FX.25 variants stop swallowing byte-identical ARQ retransmissions: the content-dedupe window shrinks from a flat 3 s to a measured 200 ms and clears on carrier re-acquisition (issue #342).** No mode changes status; this is delivery-layer, no DSP moved. The defect: every bank (`QpskMultiModem`, `BpskMultiModem`, `Afsk300MultiModem`, `Afsk1200MultiModem`) and `Afsk1200Modem`'s FX.25 route merge deduped on frame content across `3L * sampleRate`, wider than any ARQ retry interval, so a stop-and-wait retransmission - byte-identical by definition, and AX.25's entire repair grammar is byte-identical repeats - was silently discarded. Measured with the issue's repro shape (same frame twice through `ModemCatalog.Create`, 600 ms gap): **14 modes delivered 1 of 2 before, 2 of 2 after** - `afsk1200-multi`, `afsk1200-fx25`, `afsk1200-fx25rx` (the fx25 pair measured for the first time; the issue had them by source census), the `afsk300` trio, the five `bpsk` names, `qpsk600`/`qpsk2400`/`qpsk3600`, plus the four PSK ensemble configurations. The window is now sized from what it actually compensates for, measured by the new `BankDedupeSkewProbe` (`DEDUPE_SKEW_PROBE=1`): copies of one transmission spread at most 130 samples at 12 kHz across the widest bank the catalogue builds (all of it the coherent-vs-differential detector gap; 50 same-detector, 0-10 on the AFSK banks, invariant from 25 to 200-byte frames), plus at most one feed slice of emission-clock quantisation - two dedupe chunks (200 ms) covers the worst case about twice over and sits below every bank mode's burst-time at any realistic TXDELAY, so two distinct transmissions deliver outside it (the one sub-window regime, a minimal frame behind an aggressively short preamble on the fastest mode, is exactly what the acquisition layer's test pins). Second, semantic layer: `FrameDeduper.CarrierAcquired()` clears the window when the modem's carrier detect rises after a drop - a re-acquired carrier is positive evidence of a new transmission however fast the timers run, proven by a test that fits two whole qpsk3600 bursts inside the residual window (50 ms gap) and still receives both. The FX.25 route window stays codeblock-wide (2040 bits, because a small frame's embedded-HDLC and Reed-Solomon readings really are up to a whole block of padding apart) but becomes direction-aware via `FrameDeduper.RecordDelivery()`: only the FX.25 reading is ever suppressed, the embedded-HDLC reading - which nothing remembered can be a copy of, since it always precedes its own block's FX.25 reading - is recorded and always delivered. **Gates:** `RetransmissionDeliveryTests` pins both properties permanently (all 14 retransmission cases fail against the pre-fix library; one-transmission-one-delivery holds at catalogue defaults and the 18-branch ensemble); full suite 1856/0 on the tree rebased over #345, with the bpsk skew re-measured there and identical to the sample (the probe's branches and the bank's transmit both always ran the constructor's 0.35 roll-off, which is the value #345 aligned the factory to); Watterson smoke tier 55/0, with the full ladder provably inert to the change (`SimBench` gives every burst its own payload, and content dedupe compares byte-identical frames only); consumer merge gates run against a local `0.40.1-dev342` pack: packet.net `Quality_Counters_Accumulate_Across_Frames` passes, pdn-qso's whole suite passes 444/0 (the held bump is unblocked), and packet.net's one remaining failure is the `qpsk2400-il2pc-multi9` mode string, issue #343, a separate defect. Fix and evidence in PR #346.
- **2026-08-22 (later5) - qpsk600 transmits and matches at the 0.35 default roll-off; the 0.20 rested on the same mis-measured NinoTNC reference #340 corrected for bpsk300, and this time the deployed value moves (issue #344).** No mode changes status; qpsk600 was working and now copies real NinoTNCs better at the margin. Unlike #340 there was no factory/bank split to fix - the QPSK bank routes its branches through the factory, so the deployed arm really did transmit and match 0.20, every mask row was measured at 0.20, and moving the value moves what goes on air, which is why the issue demanded its own A/B campaign rather than a repeat of #340's fix. The campaign (`Qpsk600RollOffProbe`, `ROLLOFF_PROBE=1`, deterministic seeds, decisive rows at N=400 via `ROLLOFF_PROBE_N`): **(1) the reference figure was wrong here too.** Whole-burst and like-for-like with the shipping never-wider test, `samples/ninotnc/qpsk600.wav` measures **398 Hz** - the same 398 as the bpsk300 recording, from the same modulator at the same 300 sym/s - against the 328 Hz the July decision cited from the pre-issue-#2 highest-energy-window method; our own shapes meter 328 Hz (0.20) and 352 Hz (0.35), so the never-wider rule never forced 0.20 for this mode either. **(2) The filter-mismatch penalty on the deployed differential path is unmeasurable.** Loopback at N=400, all four tx/rx combinations statistically identical at every SNR tried (-1 dB: matched 0.35 = 307, matched 0.20 = 305, cross terms 296 and 300), so a mixed 0.20/0.35 fleet during rollout loses nothing in either direction; QPSK's fourth-power/differential detection did not change the sign of BPSK's answer, it flattened the loopback difference entirely. On the coherent cross-check detector the TX shape alone decides (its receive low-pass is fixed at 0.75 baud - both rx columns are bit-identical) and it decisively prefers the wider transmission: tx 0.35 copies 120/219/290 of 400 at -1/0/+1 dB against tx 0.20's 69/176/249. **(3) The decisive instrument, as in #340, is the real capture.** The NinoTNC mode-9 burst under calibrated AWGN through the deployed differential receiver: rx 0.35 copies **131 vs 81 of 400 at -2 dB** (about 4 sigma, 1.6x), identical from -1 dB up (305 vs 302, 383 vs 380, 398 vs 397), both dead at -3 (6 vs 7). **(4) The TNC-validation record repeats #340 verbatim:** nino-bench passes `--qpsk-rolloff`'s 0.35 default to the factory explicitly, so the "all 13 modes re-benched: still 6/6 both ways" run inside commit 027a7ed - the commit that chose 0.20 - and the TXDELAY survey's 10/10 both transmitted 0.35; the 0.20 shape was never once validated against a real NinoTNC. So the factory takes the 0.35 default, the on-air-compatible direction being the shape the reference hardware was actually bench-proven to decode. **The masks moved, upward or not at all** - the deployed ladder A/B (sm-ota sim, N=200, seed 1, same seeds both sides, 0.20 -> 0.35): -2 dB 50 -> 64, -1 dB 152 -> 157, 0 dB 188 -> 196, +1/+2/+4 dB and +4 at 15/30 Hz CFO all 200 -> 200. Smoke tier N=40 seed 1 at 0.35: -1 dB 34/40 (was 32), 0 dB 38/40 (was 33), +2 dB 40/40 (was 39), +4 dB and both CFO rows 40/40 (unchanged); the whole 95-row tier green, floors untouched (no row regressed, so no floor moves; the new measured values are recorded beside the rows with this entry as provenance). The off-air 2026-08-21 fixture still copies through the 0.35 matched filter (`OffAirQpskTests` green), `QpskTxShapingParityTests` now pins factory, bank and catalogue arm to sample-identical TX audio for all three QPSK modes (the guarantee was architectural before, held only by the bank's routing), and `samples/pdn/` modes 08 and 09 are regenerated so the checked-in set is again byte-for-byte what `sm-samples` renders (mode 08 had been stale since #340; a full regeneration diff confirmed exactly those two files changed). Fix and evidence in the #344 PR.

- **2026-09-04 - RS-only frames stay in the decoded frames list and out of the links pane.** No mode changes status and no decode changes: a frame only the plain reading produced (`MonitorOnly`, badged **`RS ONLY`**) is still listed on the page, still written to the frame log with `crc_valid` null, still in the journal and still withheld from the host, but it no longer feeds the waterfall's AX.25 link observer - neither live nor on the start-up backfill out of the frame log, where a `monitor_only` row is now skipped. At the operator's request, and for the reason the 2026-08-05 (later) entry above gives for withholding such a frame in the first place: the callsign pair in a CRC-less reading is as unverified as the rest of it, so it should not mint a heard station or a link on a pane read as a statement about the channel. `WaterfallPlainIl2pTests` pins both halves over the real GB7BPQ capture and `BackfillLinksTests` the replay; PR #394.

- **2026-09-04 (later) - the RS ONLY badge survives a restart: `plain_il2p` is written down and replayed.** No mode changes status and no decode changes. The frame log had no column for what the entry above badges: `FrameQuality.PlainIl2p` was dropped at the point of writing, so the decoded-frames panel's opening backlog - the last 50 rows, sent to every browser on connect - could not say that a frame had been read with no CRC behind it, and every replayed RS-only row arrived unbadged. That reads as a frame something checked, which is the opposite of what the badge is for, and it is what an operator sees for the whole of the quiet spell after a daemon restart. A `plain_il2p` column is added on open the way `monitor_only` was, `LoggedFrame` carries it, and the `history` message now sends `plain` and `monitorOnly` under the names the live `frame` message already used, so the page needed no change at all. `FrameLogTests` pins the round trip and the migration, `WaterfallPlainIl2pTests` the GB7BPQ capture written to a real log and replayed to a browser, and `WaterfallPageTests` the badge on a backlog row; issue #403.

- **2026-09-07 - the WA8LMF corpus benchmark becomes a tool in the tree, and `afsk1200` goes 959 -> 1007 and 972 -> 1011 on its two traffic tracks, past the recorded reference on both.** No mode changes status: both AFSK 1200 rows were already ✅ working off this corpus. What changes is that the measurement is now repeatable by anyone with the discs rather than reconstructible from a plan.md bullet. `tools/Packet.SoundModem.TncTest` (`sm-tnctest`, [tnc-test-cd.md](bench/tnc-test-cd.md)) plays a track through any `ModemCatalog` mode and reports the score with the counts that a bare total hides - distinct frame contents, AX.25-shaped, source callsigns reached, and the winning branch's measured station offset and emphasis. Two design points are the whole reason it exists in this form. It **reads the corpus FLAC itself**, verified frame by frame against each frame's CRC-16 and end to end against the MD5 the encoder wrote into STREAMINFO, because a benchmark with a manual conversion step is a benchmark run through a different converter every time and "which resampler did you use" is exactly the question that makes two scores incomparable. And it **shares pdn-decode's resampler by reference rather than by copy**, so a score and a forensic decode of the same file cannot disagree about what the modem heard. `--json` writes every frame rather than the total and `--baseline` diffs the frame sets, because a receive-path change that leaves the count alone and swaps which frames it got has done something a count cannot see.
  **Scores, the 144.39 MHz LA rush hour recording, 12 kHz, Release, multi+emphasis bank vs single decoder:** Track 1 (flat) **1007 / 968** (845 distinct contents, 119 callsigns, 106 s wall for 1549 s of audio), Track 2 (the same recording de-emphasised) **1011 / 534**. Against the 999 and 970 recorded for Dire Wolf `atest` on the same corpus (2026-07-15, plan.md Phase 1), that puts the AFSK bank ahead of the reference on both, having been at 959 and 972 when that comparison was made; the intervening levers are the ones already in this ledger and in plan.md § 17 (per-mode discriminator clamp, sub-sample DPLL crossing interpolation, seven timing phases, clock hold). **The atest side of that comparison is a recorded number, not a re-run** - Dire Wolf is not built on the machine this was measured on, and a fresh A/B is what would settle it. The 100-flat-Mic-E-burst track, the only one in the set whose true count is known, scores **100 of 100**, which is the harness's own evidence that it is not losing frames between the file and the modem.
  **The resampling costs nothing, which had to be checked because every figure above depends on it.** A CD is 44100 Hz and 12000 does not divide it, so each of those scores passed through a polyphase interpolator. Run at the file's own 44100 Hz with no resampler in the path at all, Track 1 scores **1006** against the resampled 1007 - one frame in a thousand, the same 845 distinct contents, the same 119 callsigns. The 12 kHz figures are the ones to quote anyway: that is the rate the daemon runs this mode at, and at 44100 the same bank manages **1.3x real time** against 14.7x, which is a receiver with no margin rather than a faster one.
  **What the two tracks together say about the bank, which neither says alone.** On the flat recording it is worth +4 % over one centred decoder, for twenty times the CPU: a Bell 202 discriminator is not narrow, so a station 60 Hz off is not a station a centred decoder misses, and the bank is buying the tail. On the de-emphasised recording - the same stations, through the audio path most operators actually have - it is worth **+89 %**, 1011 against 534, with nearly 400 frames decoded on a pre-emphasis branch rather than the flat one. **Twist, not frequency, is what the bank is for**, and a benchmark run only on the flat track would have concluded the opposite.
  Three things about the corpus that were not written down anywhere and cost time to establish. **The tags on this rip do not all describe the audio**: the file tagged "100 Mic-E Bursts DE-Emphasized" is 25:49 long, decodes 1011 frames, and yields the same 845 distinct contents from the same 119 callsigns as Track 1, so it is the de-emphasised copy of the traffic recording and not a hundred bursts of one beacon (which is what the flat Mic-E track really is: 100 frames, one distinct content). Go by length and by what decodes. **This copy of Track 1 is 25:49** while its own tag says "40 Mins Traffic" - not a truncated read, since the decode produces exactly the 68,325,012 sample instants STREAMINFO declares and hashes to the declared MD5 - so a score off it cannot be compared with a published figure taken from a 40 minute copy. And **the measured station offset moves with the emphasis rather than with the transmitters**: centred near -30 Hz on the flat file, on 0 with a tail past +150 on the de-emphasised one, the same stations both times. That is the discriminator's DC level following the twist, and it is worth knowing before reading an offset histogram as a statement about anybody's crystal.
  The FLAC decoder is pinned by `FlacReaderTests`, which round-trips every subframe shape the format allows - constant, verbatim, all five fixed orders, LPC, both Rice partition methods, the escape to raw residuals, wasted bits and all four stereo decorrelations - through a small encoder written for the purpose, and refuses a stream with a flipped bit rather than decoding it. The harness itself is pinned by `ScorerTests` over modulated audio whose frame count is known, which is the only place it can be: a corpus score has no denominator, so nothing in a real run can tell you the counter is right.

- **2026-09-08 - the AX.25 receive path learns to repair its own near-misses: `afsk1200` single goes 963 -> 980 and the bank 1008 -> 1021 on WA8LMF Track 1, with zero fabricated deliveries on the corpus's canary track.** No mode changes status; both rows were ✅ working off this corpus and stay there. Two changes, both measured end to end with `sm-tnctest --baseline` on the tnc-test.m0lte.uk re-cut ([tnc-test-cd.md](bench/tnc-test-cd.md) carries the full tables and the false-pass record). **(1) The bank's branch filters doubled** - 512-tap band-pass, 256-tap I/Q low-pass - putting the Bell 202 tones inside the passbands instead of on the 128-tap low-pass's skirt and sharpening the skirts against adjacent-channel junk: +7 on Track 1, +2 Track 2, +1 Track 4. The lone decoder keeps 256/128 *because of* the same measurement: a single demodulator under the long filters loses Track 3 65 -> 3 and Track 2 547 -> 418 (21 ms of LPF memory smears a preamble's two-bit blips into a marginal eye; only a bank's diversity covers it), which is now written down where the next person to try it will find it. **(2) `HdlcDeframer` grew a chase engine** (`HdlcRepairPolicy`, opt-in, both afsk1200 receivers): confidence-ordered single/pair bit flips on FCS failure and delete/insert realignment on bit slips, fed by a new soft-decision sink on `AfskDemodulator`, delivering through `FrameRepaired` with `FrameQuality.ChasedBits` set. Repairs clear three gates before delivery - strict AX.25 structure (UI control only: a search never fabricates link state), payload-protocol cross-checks (NMEA's own XOR checksum, the APRS position/weather/Mic-E character skeletons), and `RepairEchoGate`, which drops repaired copies landing within 350 ms and ten bytes of a clean decode: the dominant false-delivery class measured during development (a damaged read of a burst another branch copied cleanly, invisible to content dedupe). Track 3 - one hundred transmissions of one known frame - is the canary that pins the false-pass rate at zero delivered fabrications on budgets up to twice the shipped one; Track 4 lands on 110, the count the recording is documented to carry, two of them repaired WA8LMF Mic-E frames filling real beacon-sequence gaps. Every Track 1 repaired delivery was cross-checked against the run's clean population before the gates were finalised (each one's source is a station decoded cleanly elsewhere in the same run). `HdlcRepairTests` + `RepairEchoGateTests` pin the engine, the gates and the rule that a clean FCS pass is never gated; the full suite (2593 tests) is green with the change.

- **2026-09-20 - OFDM-FM moves out of a runtime plugin and into this repository as eight built-in modes, and the plugin seam loses its worked example in the process.** The modem was the first thing to use the plugin seam, in 2026-08-09's entry above, and it lived out of tree while its carrier layouts were still settling. It now ships eight presets of our own in `OfdmFmPresets`, built through `ModemCatalog` like any other mode, and nothing about the waveform changed on the wire. **What is proven is the 2026-09-19 bench campaign**, two Tait TM8110s on a 25 kHz channel through a coax bench: `ofdm-fm-8k` delivered every frame at 1024 and 1900 byte payloads in both directions and 19 of 20 at 3000, for 17.5 to 24.7 kbit/s of application goodput scored from both stations' frame logs, and the rate and follow-on variants are in the matrix above with their own numbers. **Three things are deliberately not shipped and each is a real loss, recorded so nobody goes looking for them.** The presets carry no geometry id, so each one acquires on its own layout and both ends must be on the same mode: the geometry-table machinery is built and tested and lets one receiver hear several layouts, but it needs an agreed acquisition layout as entry 0 that every span contains, and the only such layout that has ever been on air is one we are not publishing. Shipping an unmeasured replacement would put the sync, preamble and header of every burst on a layout nobody has keyed, which is the least forgiving place in the waveform to guess. `ofdm-fm-narrow` is designed rather than measured, sized so the coded header still fits one symbol, and it is here only because a voice-bandwidth audio path cannot carry the wider presets at all. And the radio-derived carrier sense, which took DCD and RSSI from the radio's own control port rather than inferring a busy channel from audio, is gone with its serial dependency; the `IChannelBusySource` seam and the audio-derived detector remain, and a radio source can be added behind it. **The plugin documentation moved off this mode and onto the `sample` plugin in `tests/`**, which is both more honest, since OFDM-FM is no longer a plugin, and better, since the documented example is now one the repository can actually run.
- **2026-09-21 - C4FSK found to decode nothing over a real FM link; `fsk9600` proven on air for the first time (issue #518).** Two independent faults, both measured on the radio1/radio2 Tait bench. **First**, `C4fskModem` is the only modem where `EnergyBusyDetector.Busy` hard-gates the bit path, and it asserts 6 dB ABOVE a tracked floor: an FM receiver with the squelch open is louder idle than under a carrier, measured at -17.0 dBFS idle against -18.1 dBFS for a transmission at full transmit level (and -36.1 dBFS at -18 dB), through the modem's own 7.2 kHz receive filter. Over a 100 s recording containing 29 s of transmissions the gate opened once, on the return of the hiss. That alone accounts for 0 of 20 at every frame size from 8 to 256 bytes, both directions. **Second**, with the gate forced open the eye is still shut (rms distance to the nearest 4-PAM level 0.193 off air against 0.019 to 0.032 straight out of the modulator, identical at every transmit level from 0 to -18 dB), because the interface's audio coupling network high-passes the band: the measured end-to-end response is flat from 280 Hz to 4.5 kHz and 3 dB down at 70 Hz, and `C4fskBaselineWanderProbe` (40 seeds, 60 dB AWGN) puts `c4fsk9600`'s tolerance at a 30 Hz corner, `c4fsk19200`'s at 70 Hz and `fsk9600`'s at 200 Hz. The measured response's low end alone delivers 0 of 40 for both C4FSK modes; its top end alone delivers 40 of 40 for every mode. **`fsk9600` over the same path is the first real-RF decode of any baseband mode here**: 100 % at 32 bytes falling to 5-10 % at 512, in both directions, the falling curve being the same wander. A prior report blaming a rolloff at the TOP of the band (#517) is superseded: its -23 dB at 4 kHz was the transmitted waveform's own spectrum, which by construction is 17.6 dB down at 4 kHz and has a null at 4.8 kHz, not a property of the path. Full evidence and the traps in [c4fsk-on-air-handover.md](c4fsk-on-air-handover.md). Two modulator-side defects found in the same pass, neither of which needs a radio to see: both C4FSK modes emit above full scale (`c4fsk9600` peaks at 1.0562 with 5.9 % of its samples clipped by `Pcm16.FromFloat`, `c4fsk19200` at 1.0261), because `HeadroomFraction` 0.8 does not cover the pulse shaper's 32 % overshoot; and #516's per-mode deviation scaling is a no-op for these two, since it normalises each mode against its own declared channel class and both sit at 100 % of theirs, so both still come out at 0.800 and the intended 1:2 ratio is still 1:1.
- **2026-09-21 - a NinoTNC reference transmitter separates the two C4FSK faults (issue #518).** A NinoTNC in C4FSK 19k2 replaced radio2's CM108 and transmitted through the same Tait, captured on the same SDR and received by the same station, which is the control our own transmissions could never be. Scored on one instrument (symbol-instant values fitted to four levels by k-means, within-cluster rms as a fraction of the half-swing; 0.02 is healthy, the decision margin is about 0.11): our modulator's own float samples **0.021**, our station as radiated **0.191**, the NinoTNC as radiated **0.080**, the NinoTNC as received by radio1 **0.151**, our station as received by radio1 **0.19**. So BOTH halves of our chain are at fault and the transmit half is the worse of the two: a reference TNC through the same radio radiates a clean eye with textbook levels and even occupancy where we radiate a shut one from samples that are clean when they leave the modem, and our receive chain then roughly doubles the spread again on the reference's clean signal. radio1 decoded **0 of 15** NinoTNC frames, and still read nothing with the energy gate forced open, so the gate fix alone would not have read a reference peer. Deviation as radiated: the NinoTNC at its pot's midpoint is 4.85 to 4.89 kHz peak against `c4fsk19200`'s published 5.0 kHz, and its bursts carry about 5 ms of preamble, which is the acquisition figure a real peer gives us. Two instrument bugs of mine nearly cost this diagnosis and are written up with it: no anti-alias filter before decimating a 1 Msps discriminator output, and labelling a 50 kHz stream as 48 kHz. Both are caught by checking that the preamble tone reads exactly half the symbol rate before trusting anything downstream. See [c4fsk-on-air-handover.md](c4fsk-on-air-handover.md).
- **2026-09-21 - FM carrier sense becomes a station service and is proved on air, for every mode rather than for `ofdm-fm` alone (issue #522).** The radio-derived source is back and is no longer scoped to one mode family: `Packet.Radio.Tait` is a package reference again, the stack lives in `Packet.SoundModem.CarrierSense`, and `SoundModemChannel` asks it once for the station rather than each modem asking separately. **What that fixes, measured on the radio1/radio2 Tait bench on 2026-09-21**: with the `afsk1200` sub-channel deliberately put back alongside `ofdm-fm-8k` on radio2, the configuration that silenced a station in the 2026-09-19 campaign, time from KISS to air two seconds after hearing one burst was **9.73, 9.92 and 10.23 s** with the radio ignored and **0.50, 1.19 and 1.31 s** with it consulted, against 0.5 to 1.6 s on a quiet channel either way. The cause is that every in-band energy detector here assumes a signal ADDS power and an open-squelch FM receiver does the reverse, so they fire after a transmission rather than during it. A station with no control cable now reads the SHAPE of the received spectrum instead (`FmShapeBusyDetector`, on by default): 15 of 15 and 11 of 11 transmissions found on real captures, a median 26 ms after the carrier arrives, no false busy in 620 s of idle channel, and no absolute level anywhere in the decision, which is what made the previous attempt unsafe. It needs 48 kHz and is measured not to work at 12. **Nothing about any waveform changed**; this is what a station will and will not transmit over. Record in [carrier-sense.md](carrier-sense.md).
- **2026-09-26 - `qpsk3600` works on air, both directions, for the first time: a whole connected session with GB7RDG from the in-process Windows station.** Status 🚧 not-yet-on-air -> ✅ working. The station was pdn-win (a WPF packet terminal running this library in process, the first consumer to do so outside the node) on the new `Packet.SoundModem.Windows` WASAPI and CM108/AIOC HID PTT backends (commit/PR TBD, branch `feat/windows-audio`), through an AIOC into a Quansheng handheld on FM with the squelch open, TXDELAY 400 ms and the Windows transmit level at 0 dB, deviation not measured. Against GB7RDG's qpsk3600 port: a pre-connect XID answered, SABM/UA, the node's two-line welcome, `?` answered with its command list, `B` answered with DISC and our UA. Nine frames each way; received SNR 4.2 to 6.5 dB by the band tracker, carrier offsets -4 to +6 Hz. One of our I-frames was not acknowledged and went again after the node's poll, which is AX.25 doing its job at that SNR rather than a modem fault. A third station's frames on the same channel (M9YYY-9 working GB7RDG) decoded alongside at 4.2 dB. No capture was kept; the next session should record one for a fixture.
- **2026-10-05 - MS110D receiver no longer stays locked on noise after a burst too weak to read (issue #553).** Status of every `ms110d-wn*` row unchanged; this is receive robustness, not performance. Found by pdn-mailcast (its PR #22): a weak burst whose preamble is acquired (about -3 to -12 dB SNR in 3 kHz) left the receiver demodulating noise for good, at many times the idle CPU and deaf to later bursts. The demodulator now lets go once its mini-probes have shown no signal for 16 s (`SignalAbsent`). The probes are matched-filtered against the raw received symbols, with up to four steady carriers cancelled and the background measured in the lags; reasoning and measurements in [ms110d/signal-absent.md](ms110d/signal-absent.md). The window was chosen so that no frame the old receiver read is lost across 1,598 slow-fade, mask and carrier cases (0.02 to 0.2 Hz Rayleigh, scripted 10 to 18 s fades, one to four carriers on and off the probe lines under readable bursts, every waveform number). The full mask battery is byte-identical to the 2026-08-21 G4 battery (120 of 120 censuses, every mask line). Every simulated weak burst, with or without carriers after it, was let go within 16.0 s of its end.
- **2026-10-09 - the built-in mailcast receiver's MS110D modem could not actually be moved to most of the audio centre a sound-card dial is allowed to put it at, caught by proving one on real IQ (PR #581).** Status of every `ms110d-wn*` row unchanged; this is mailcast's own placement, not the mode's performance or validation elsewhere. PR #581 let a sound-card rig's dial put the signal anywhere from 1000 to 2000 Hz of audio and asked the generic band-plan shift decorator (`FrequencyShiftedModem.Wrap`) to move MS110D there, the way a band plan moves any spec-fixed mode. That decorator refuses a shift that would bring the modem's own occupied band (measured almost 2.9 kHz wide) within 350 Hz of DC or Nyquist - a real, measured guard (its own remarks: downshifting unfiltered noise folds it onto the band, a measured +3 dB SNR cost) - and MS110D's width puts that refusal at every centre from 1000 up to about 1740 Hz, including 1278 Hz (the FT-450D worked example PR #581's own docs give) and 1500 Hz (the narrow-filter dial PR #580 already shipped, 2026-10-08): `MailcastReceiver`'s constructor threw `ArgumentException` building the modem rather than decoding anything. Fixed by `MailcastReceiveShift`, built for mailcast only: bandpass to the signal's own measured width, then shift the audio itself to MS110D's native centre upstream of the modem, which never leaves that centre and so never reaches the generic decorator's guard - the same way pdn-mailcast's own standalone receiver does it. Proven on real IQ, not just synthetic audio: a 20-minute GB7RDG capture (UberSDR station M9PSY, `sm-iqcapture` converted to SSB at each centre with `--dial-hz -1278/-1500/-1800`) decodes identically - 41 frames heard, 6 pending bulletins, the same BIDs - at 1278, 1500 and 1800 Hz.
- **2026-10-10 - every 12 kHz mode on a 48 kHz channel: receive fell to about 0.3x real time on GB7RDG, fixed by running those modems at 12 kHz (issue #648, PR #652).** Not a demodulator regression: the modes' own ladders never moved. Adding `ms110d-wn4` to GB7RDG on 2026-10-05 moved its shared channel from 12 kHz to 48 kHz, and every 12 kHz-native modem was then built at 48 kHz. Measured over a 300 s capture from the station on the Windows dev box, the station's `afsk300-il2pc`, `bpsk300` and `afsk300` id-beacon receivers cost 223.9, 293.4 and 149.0 s at 48 kHz (222 % of real time between them) against 10.6, 19.0 and 10.6 s bridged (13.4 %). On the station's Ryzen the receive thread sat at 100 % of a core from 0.85.0 to 0.87.5, and each raw-capture chunk took 15.5 to 17.5 minutes of wall clock to fill with its 300 s of samples: about 70 % of the received audio was being dropped, which is what slowed the waterfall and chopped Listen, and every slot missed most frames. Fixed by `RateBridgedModem` (`ModemCatalog.CreateForChannel`): a 12 kHz mode whose band fits under the decimator's passband runs at 12 kHz behind M0LTE.Dsp's `Decimator` and a per-burst `Upsampler`. Sim A/B (`sm-ota sim`, N=40, seed 1, native 12 kHz vs bridged at 48 kHz): bpsk300 AWGN -6/-5/-4/-3/-2 dB 8/37/40/40/40 vs 12/36/40/40/40, CFO 3.75 Hz at -3 dB 40 vs 40, Good 0/2/9 dB 24/26/39 vs 24/26/39, Moderate 0/2/9 18/25/25 vs 18/23/28, Poor 0/2/9 3/6/7 vs 3/3/8; afsk300-il2pc AWGN -2/-1/0/+1 25/37/40/40 vs 24/35/40/40; afsk300 AWGN -2/-1/0/+1 0/4/16/37 vs 0/3/21/33. Every pair is within its 95 % interval and every Watterson mask row for these modes holds; no mask moves. On air, deployed to GB7RDG as 0.87.5-648.1 at 19:15 UTC: the raw capture fills at 96045 bytes/s (1.000x real time) and the receive thread takes about 31 % of a core. On-air decodes after the fix are not recorded here yet: 40 m was down to 3 frames an hour by then.
- **2026-10-11 - the IQ receive front end, a new receive path for `afsk1200` and `fsk9600` (and their `-il2p` forms), proved in simulation only (issue #594).** Status of every mode row unchanged: nothing on air yet, and no existing receive path or mask moved. `IqReceiver` takes any IQ source down a shared halfband cascade, an NCO, M0LTE.Dsp's rational resampler as the channel (IF) filter and a flat FM discriminator, to audio at the DSP rate. An FM channel's IF is the mode's published occupied bandwidth (`IqChannelSpec.ForFmMode`: 10 kHz for afsk1200, 20 kHz for fsk9600). Laddered at 2.4 MS/s with the carrier 25 kHz off centre against the batch discriminator the FM ladders were built on (48 kS/s capture on the carrier, pre-filtered to the channel spacing's IF, 8 kHz narrow or 16 kHz wide), white noise at the stated CNR in that spacing IF, N=50, seed 1, TXDELAY 150 ms (`IqFrontEndFmLadderTests`, `SM_MASK_GATE=1`). Reference vs front end at the occupancy IF vs front end at the spacing IF: afsk1200-il2p +6/+8/+10/+12 dB 0/24/48/50 vs 29/49/50/50 vs 0/42/50/50; afsk1200 +8/+10/+12/+15 0/21/46/50 vs 20/47/50/50 vs 2/28/47/50; fsk9600-il2p +8/+10/+12 2/43/50 vs 4/38/49 vs 6/37/50; fsk9600 +10/+12/+15 6/34/50 vs 1/38/50 vs 4/40/50. The 8 kHz IF trims AFSK 1200's own sidebands (3 kHz deviation and 2.2 kHz tones need about 10 kHz), and widening it to the mode's occupancy is worth about 2 dB at the knee although it lets in 25 % more noise; for GFSK 9600 16 and 20 kHz are within each other's intervals. The 12 kHz AFSK rows also gain over the reference because the front end's audio resampler rejects the discriminator's out-of-band noise where the reference's 33-tap decimator lets it fold. No rung loses beyond its 95 % interval. Not comparable with the `fm-data` mask rows: there is no limiter, deviation error or audio shaping in this channel. New smoke mask rows (240 kS/s, N=20, measured 20/20 each, floor 17): afsk1200-il2p at +12 dB CNR and fsk9600-il2p at +15 dB CNR. On-air legs (a real IQ source behind #596, #597 or #598) are not done.
- **2026-10-11 - sound-card IQ (`iqcard:`, issue #596), a new receive path for the SSB modes, proved in simulation only: `bpsk300` and `afsk1200`.** Status of every mode row unchanged: nothing on air yet, and no existing receive path or mask moved. `SoundCardIqSource` reads a stereo card's left and right inputs as I and Q (swap, gain and phase correction, overruns as gaps) and feeds the #594 front end. Laddered with `SoundCardIqLadderTests` (`SM_MASK_GATE=1`): the mode's burst through the AWGN sim channel at the stated SNR in 3 kHz, then decoded as audio, or put on a USB dial 6 kHz above the centre of 48 kS/s IQ and heard through a matched 16-bit card, or through a card 0.5 dB and 3 degrees out, corrected, with a signal 34 dB stronger on the channel's mirror. N=50, seed 1, TXDELAY 150 ms. Audio vs matched card vs corrected card with the image: bpsk300 -6/-5/-4/-3 dB 9/44/49/50 vs 7/43/49/50 vs 8/44/50/50; afsk1200 +4/+6/+8 dB 1/32/50 vs 1/34/50 vs 1/34/50. No rung loses beyond its 95 % interval. The card's own DC offset (1 % and 0.7 % of full scale on I and Q, as big as the burst) and mains hum (50/100/150 Hz at -50/-55/-60 dBFS) were then added, with the channel at the 6 kHz default offset and on the centre (dial at 0 Hz, what a rig tuned to the dial gives): DC and hum at the offset vs channel on the centre vs DC and hum on the centre, bpsk300 -6/-5/-4/-3 dB 8/42/49/50 vs 7/44/50/50 vs 9/45/50/50; afsk1200 +4/+6/+8 dB 1/35/50 vs 1/33/50 vs 1/34/50, so no measurable cost for either mode at this level, since their tones sit well above the hum; the default offset stays as the margin for worse cards and modes low in the audio. Measured on the way: with the wanted signal left 40 dB below full scale and no make-up gain, afsk1200 loses about 2 dB at its knee (15/50 against 34/50 at +6 dB), a level matter for `iqCard`.`gain` rather than the path, so the ladder restores the audio arm's level. New smoke mask rows (N=20, measured 20/20 each, floor 18): bpsk300 at -3 dB and afsk1200 at +8 dB, both on the corrected card with the image. On-air legs (a G90 or another stereo-IQ receiver decoding a real signal, and image rejection measured on a real card) are not done.
- **2026-10-11 - the `rtltcp:` device, an RTL-SDR's 8-bit IQ over rtl_tcp into the IQ front end, proved in simulation only (issue #598).** Status of every mode row unchanged: nothing on air yet, and no existing receive path or mask moved. The new thing on the receive path is the dongle's 8-bit converter: the stream is scaled so its RMS on each axis is a fraction of full scale (the dongle's gain), clipped and rounded to unsigned bytes, and turned back to floats with the rtl_tcp source's own table, then heard through the front end exactly as in the 2026-10-11 front-end entry (2.4 MS/s, carrier 25 kHz off centre, white noise at the stated CNR in the spacing IF, N=50, seed 1, TXDELAY 150 ms; `RtlTcpLadderTests`, `SM_MASK_GATE=1`). Float IQ vs bytes at RMS 0.2 of full scale vs bytes at RMS 0.02 (gain far too low, the noise a few converter steps): afsk1200-il2p +6/+8/+10 dB 29/49/50 vs 30/49/50 vs 26/48/50; afsk1200 +8/+10/+12 20/47/50 vs 20/47/50 vs 16/46/50; fsk9600-il2p +8/+10/+12 4/38/49 vs 3/38/50 vs 3/37/48; fsk9600 +10/+12/+15 1/38/50 vs 1/38/50 vs 4/39/50. At a sensible gain eight bits cost nothing measurable; at a tenth of it the AFSK knees lose a few frames, inside their intervals. Not comparable with the `fm-data` mask rows. The transport itself (header, commands, read-only sending nothing, reconnects and quiet feeds reported as gaps, retunes, the rate watch) is pinned against a fake server in `RtlTcpIqSourceTests`, and AFSK 1200 and 9600 GFSK bursts cut to dongle bytes decode through the whole input in `RtlTcpAudioInputTests`. New smoke mask rows (250 kS/s, RMS 0.2 of full scale, N=20, measured 20/20 each, floor 17): afsk1200-il2p at +12 dB CNR and fsk9600-il2p at +15 dB CNR. On-air legs (a dongle behind `rtl_tcp`, and an OpenWebRX+ rtl_tcp-compatible port, each decoding real traffic) are not done.
